## What was wrong and what was done

No error was found in the results examined.

## Clarifications (not errors)

Nothing in this section is an error of the paper. Each item is something the paper determines but does not say, made explicit so that the statements can be read, and formalized, in one way.

**How a statement is read.**

- **Theorem 2.3.** For $\rho\in\{\mu,\nu\}$ and $t\in(0,1)$, $Q_\rho(t)=\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\rho((\! -\infty,a])\geqslant t\}$.
- **Theorem 2.3.** For $0<t<1$, $(Q_\mu(t),Q_\nu(t))=(\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,a])\geqslant t\},\inf\{b\in\mathbb R:\nu(( -\infty,b])\geqslant t\})$.
- **Proposition 3.1.** $Y=WY(1)+(1-W)Y(0)$ almost surely under the structural population law.
- **Proposition 3.1.** $W=\indicator(U\leqslant p(Z,X))$ almost surely under the structural population law.
- **Proposition 3.1.** $\mathcal L(U\mid X=x)=\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$ for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$.
- **Proposition 3.1.** Every constraint in $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ is imposed simultaneously for both $w\in\{0,1\}$.
- **Theorem 3.4.** Any two structural laws satisfying Structural Assumptions and inducing the same $\mathbb P_{obs}$ give the same $\omega(x,u)$ for $\mathbb P_{obs}(\mathrm dx)\,\mathrm du$-almost every $(x,u)$.
- **Theorem 3.4.** The continuity condition holds for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x\in\mathcal X$.
- **Theorem 3.4.** For $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$, the intervals $L_k(x)$, $1\leqslant k\leqslant K_x$, the gaps $G_k(x)$, $0\leqslant k<K_x$, and the tail $(\overline p_{K_x}(x),1]$ partition $[0,1]$ up to Lebesgue-null endpoints.
- **Theorem 3.4.** For $i=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $0<t<1$, $Q_{Y,i}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,i}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ and $Q_{\omega,i}(t)=\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:\mathbb P(\omega(U)\leqslant v)\geqslant t\}$, where $U\sim\mathrm{Unif}(p_i,p_{i+1})$.
- **Theorem 3.4.** For $0<t<1$, use the lower generalized inverses $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ and $Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\}$.
- **Theorem 3.4.** Interpret the kernel formula with any fixed jointly measurable $\mathcal Y$-valued extension of $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to ranks $0$ and $1$; the asserted gap marginal and attaining objective hold for every such extension.
- **Theorem 3.4.** For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$ and $0<t<1$, $(Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t),Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t))=(\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\},\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\})$.
- **Theorem 3.7.** The partition assertion means $\int_{[0,1]\setminus\left(\bigcup_{k=1}^{K}L_k\,\cup\,\bigcup_{k=0}^{K-1}G_k\,\cup\,(\overline p_K,1]\right)}\mathrm du=0$.
- **Theorem 3.7.** For each probability law $\mu$ used here, $Q_\mu(t)=\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,a])\geqslant t\}$ for $t\in(0,1)$, with the displayed quantile integrals interpreted as Lebesgue integrals over $(0,1)$.
- **Proposition 4.3.** The identified outcome components $J_{\text{full},k}$ and $J_{j,k}$ are defined for $0\leqslant k<K$, with $0\leqslant j\leqslant l_k$ for $J_{j,k}$; the final interval is represented by $\Delta_K$, without an identified law $\mu_{1,K\mid x}$.
- **Proposition 4.4.** For each fixed $k\in\{0,\dots,K-1\}$ and $j\in\{0,\dots,l_k-1\}$ to which part~(i) is applied, its continuity hypothesis is $\mu_{1,k\mid X}(\{\nu_{j,k}(X)\})=\mu_{1,k\mid X}(\{\nu_{j+1,k}(X)\})=0$ almost surely under the marginal law of $X$.
- **Proposition 4.4.** For $0<t<1$, use the standard lower generalized quantile $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$.
- **Proposition 4.4.** For $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, interpret $\nu_{j,k}(x)=Q_{Y,k\mid x}(\kappa_{j,k}(x))$ using $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ for $0<t<1$, with $\nu_{0,k}(x)=y_{\min}$.
- **Proposition 4.4.** The boundary quantile uses the bounded-domain lower generalized inverse $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathcal Y:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ for $0\leqslant t<1$, so $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0)=y_{\min}$.
- **Theorem 4.7.** For every $z\in\mathcal Z$ and $x\in\mathcal X$, $p\mapsto\phi(z,x,p)$ is differentiable on $(0,1)$ and has finite right and left derivatives at $p=0$ and $p=1$, respectively; these are its endpoint values of $\partial\phi/\partial p$.
- **Theorem 4.7.** There exist finite constants $C,L\geqslant0$ such that, for all $z\in\mathcal Z$, $x\in\mathcal X$, and $p,p'\in[0,1]$, $|\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p)|\leqslant C$ and $|\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p)-\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p')|\leqslant L|p-p'|$.
- **Theorem 4.7.** For every $x\in\mathcal X$ and every nonempty $T_{j,k}$, $\tau_{j,k}(x)\geqslant c_\pi$, where $c_\pi>0$ is the same constant used for $\pi_k$ in \cref{asp:overlap}.
- **Theorem 4.7.** For a fresh $X$ with the population marginal law and independent of $I_1$, $\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\|_{L_2}^2=\mathbb E[(\hat p(z,X)-p(z,X))^2\mid I_1]$, and $n^{1/4}\max_{z\in\mathcal Z}\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\|_{L_2}\xrightarrow{P}0$ as $n\to\infty$.
- **Theorem 4.7.** Pointwise in $x$, the fitted probability, propensity, share, and indicator-mean nuisances lie in $[0,1]$, $\hat\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)\in[-1,1]$, $\hat\nu_{j,k}(x),\hat J_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\pm}(x)\in[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$, and $\hat J_{j,k}^{\pm}(x)\in[\min\{0,y_{\min}\},\max\{0,y_{\max}\}]$, for each applicable fitted component; denominator clipping is applied further as stated.
- **Theorem 4.7.** Componentwise $L_2$ errors integrate over the marginal law of $X$ under $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}$, holding each fitted function and any instrument argument $z$ fixed; $o_P(n^{-1/4})$ refers to convergence in probability over the observed sample and random partition as $n\to\infty$.
- **Theorem 4.7.** $Y\in\{0,1\}$ almost surely.
- **Theorem 4.7.** Two remaining labeled vertices are connected exactly when their averaged values differ by less than $c_{\mathrm{gap}}/2$.
- **Theorem 4.7.** In this definition, $\psi_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\mathrm{prod}}$ and $\psi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{prod}}$ are the binary-outcome components of Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete}, and $\psi_{\Delta_K}$ is the common tail component of Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}.
- **Theorem 4.7.** The binary nuisance list supplements the separately fitted first-stage propensity, so $\eta(x)=\{p(z,x),p_k(x),P_{1,k}(x),\pi_k(x),\tau_{j,k}(x),a_{j,k}(z,x),\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}(x),\gamma_{j,k}(x),\gamma_K(x)\}_{j,k}$, with $p_0=P_{1,0}=0$ fixed and no fitted $\pi_0$.
- **Theorem 4.7.** The structural-zero assignment in Algorithm~\ref{alg:dml_continuous_outcome} is $\widehat S_0=\{z:\bar p_2(z)\leqslant\rho_n\}$, with deterministic $\rho_n\downarrow0$ satisfying $n^{1/4}\rho_n\to\infty$.
- **Theorem 4.7.** The estimated baseline bins used to index $\widehat T_{j,k}$ include their left endpoints and exclude their right endpoints for $k<K$; the final bin for $k=K$ includes both endpoints, including $1$.
- **Theorem 4.7.** At each evaluation covariate $X_i$, $(\hat P^*_{1,1}(X_i),\ldots,\hat P^*_{1,K}(X_i))$ is the nondecreasing sort of the clipped vector $(\hat P_{1,1}(X_i),\ldots,\hat P_{1,K}(X_i))$, with $\hat P^*_{1,0}(X_i)=0$; this same vector is used in every score plug-in and residual, without a separate gap projection.
- **Theorem 4.7.** In this definition, all components of $\psi(O;\eta)$ are those of Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}, in particular $\psi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{prod}}=\gamma_{j,k}\psi_{j-1,k}^{J}+J_{j-1,k}(\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k})$.
- **Theorem 4.7.** Every nuisance-error norm in this theorem is the population norm $\|\hat f-f\|_{L_2}^2=\mathbb E_X[|\hat f(X)-f(X)|^2]$, with the fitted function held fixed during integration.
- **Proposition 4.8.** For $0<t<1$, use the standard lower generalized quantile $Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$.
- **Proposition 4.8.** For $\underline p(x)>0$ and $0<t<1$, $Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$; at $t=0,1$ use the endpoint extension of the initial-gap outcome quantile in the referenced bound.
- **Theorem 4.10.** For every probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathbb R$ and $0<t<1$, $Q_\mu(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$; the integral defining $W_1$ is a Lebesgue integral over $(0,1)$ and requires no endpoint values.
- **Corollary O5.1.** $\|\hat p-p\|_\infty=\sup_{(z,x)\in\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X}|\hat p(z,x)-p(z,x)|$.
- **Corollary O5.1.** In (L2), $O_P(r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n})$ describes stochastic order over the training observations and random partition, while $\mathbb E_{Z,X}[\cdot\mid I_0,I_1]$ averages an independent population draw of $(Z,X)$ conditional on the partition and the observations indexed by $I_0\cup I_1$.
- **Corollary O5.1.** In (L3), $O_P(r_{Q,n})$ describes stochastic order over the training observations and random partition, while $\mathbb E_X[\cdot\mid I_0,I_1]$ averages an independent population draw of $X$ conditional on the partition and the observations indexed by $I_0\cup I_1$.
- **Corollary O5.1.** Off-grid evaluations of $\widehat C(\tau\mid x)$ use linear interpolation between adjacent cumulative-integral values on $\{0,\tau_1,\ldots,\tau_{M_Q-1},1\}$, whose values are computed by the trapezoid rule.
- **Corollary O5.1.** The endpoint-invariance assertion for population Lebesgue integrals concerns changes only at $\tau\in\{0,1\}$ of a fixed population quantile; the two permitted fitted interpolants may differ on $[0,M_Q^{-1}]\cup[1-M_Q^{-1},1]$.
- **Corollary O5.1.** For $0<t<1$, $Q^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$.
- **Corollary O5.1.** The superscript $c$ in $\widetilde Q_m^c$ labels clipping to $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$ and does not denote the transport cost $c$.
- **Corollary O5.1.** For $0<t<1$, $Q^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in[y_{\min},y_{\max}]:\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}(( -\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$.

**Side conditions the proofs use without stating them.**

- **Theorem 3.4.** For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, extend $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to $[0,1]$ by $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0):=y_{\min}$ and $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1):=y_{\max}$.
- **Theorem 4.7.** $K\geqslant1$.
- **Theorem 4.7.** $K\geqslant1$.

**Notation.**

- $\mathcal{Y}$: $\mathcal Y$ is the fixed, known, compact outcome domain contained in $\mathbb R$, equipped with its Borel $\sigma$-field, with known feasible endpoints $y_{\min}=\min\mathcal Y$ and $y_{\max}=\max\mathcal Y$.
- $Y$: $Y$ denotes the observed outcome taking values in the known compact Borel domain $\mathcal Y\subset\mathbb R$, with $y_{\min}\leqslant Y\leqslant y_{\max}$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}, Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In part~\textup{(ii)}, $Y\in\{0,1\}$.
- $\mathcal{Z}$: $\mathcal Z$ is the compact standard Borel instrument domain equipped with its Borel $\sigma$-field and is the common conditional support $\mathrm{Supp}(Z\mid X=x)=\mathcal Z$ for all $x\in\mathcal X$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:discrete_lower_bound}, Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}, Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}, Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\mathcal Z$ is finite. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}, Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: $\mathcal Z$ has finitely many connected components. In Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\mathcal Z$ is connected.
- $\mathcal{X}$: $\mathcal X\subset\mathbb R^{d_X}$ is the compact covariate domain, equipped with its Borel $\sigma$-field and regarded as a standard Borel space.
- $X$: $X$ denotes observed covariate information taking values in the compact Borel domain $\mathcal X\subset\mathbb R^{d_X}$; $x$ denotes a conditioning value, and expectations indexed by $X$ average over its marginal law. In Theorem~\ref{thm:discrete_lower_bound}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: $X$ is almost surely constant, so conditional covariate arguments are suppressed.
- $U$: $U$ denotes the latent resistance in the threshold-crossing model, invariant under the policy change, with interval-uniform uses denoting its law restricted to the indicated resistance interval. In Theorem~\ref{thm:discrete_lower_bound}: In defining $Q_{\omega,i}$, $U\sim\mathrm{Unif}(p_i,p_{i+1})$ denotes the resistance draw conditional on $U\in(p_i,p_{i+1}]$, for $i=0,\dots,K-1$ with $p_i<p_{i+1}$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: In defining $Q_{\omega,k}$, $U\sim\mathrm{Unif}(G_k)$ denotes the resistance draw restricted to the nonempty gap $G_k$, for $k=0,\dots,K-1$; no gap-specific law is defined for an empty gap. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: For fixed $x$ and a nonempty $G_k(x)$, the draw $U\sim\mathrm{Unif}(G_k(x))$ used to define $Q_{\omega,k\mid x}$ is the resistance law conditional on $X=x$ and $U\in G_k(x)$, for $k=0,\dots,K_x-1$; in the randomized rank construction this gap draw is independent of $S\sim\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$.
- $p$: $p(z,x)=\mathbb P(W=1\mid Z=z,X=x)$ denotes the baseline population conditional treatment propensity, with values in $[0,1]$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:discrete_lower_bound}: Since $X$ is almost surely constant, $p$ is written as $p(z)$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: Since $X$ is almost surely constant, $p$ is written as $p(z)$, using its continuous version on each connected component of $\mathcal Z$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}, Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: For almost every $x$, $z\mapsto p(z,x)$ is the version continuous on each connected component of $\mathcal Z$, so its range and extrema are taken over that version throughout the conditional support.
- $q$: $q:\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\to[0,1]$ denotes the alternative-policy propensity function determining $W^q=\indicator(U\leqslant q(Z,X))$ with the same latent resistance $U$ as under the baseline policy. In Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}, Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}, Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}, Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $q(z,x)=\phi(z,x,p(z,x))$, where the known map $\phi:\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\times[0,1]\to[0,1]$ is jointly measurable.
- $\phi$: $\phi:\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\times[0,1]\to[0,1]$ is the known jointly measurable policy map specifying $q(z,x)=\phi(z,x,p(z,x))$ and the substitution $\hat q(z,x)=\phi(z,x,\hat p(z,x))$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\phi(z,x,p)$ is differentiable in $p$, and $\partial\phi/\partial p$ is bounded and uniformly Lipschitz in $p$ over $z\in\mathcal Z$ and $x\in\mathcal X$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $|\phi(z,x,p')-\phi(z,x,p)|\leqslant L_\phi|p'-p|$ for all $(z,x,p,p')\in\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\times[0,1]^2$, with $L_\phi<\infty$.
- $P$: $P=p(Z,X)$ is the observed baseline propensity index obtained by evaluating the population propensity function at $(Z,X)$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The conditional propensity support is $\operatorname{supp}(P\mid X=x)=[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$ in the connected-instrument setting. In Theorem~\ref{thm:discrete_lower_bound}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: With $X$ almost surely constant, the covariate argument is suppressed and $P=p(Z)$.
- $W^q$: $W^q=\indicator(U\leqslant q(Z,X))$ uses the same latent resistance $U$ as baseline treatment $W$; $U$ is invariant to the policy change.
- $\omega$: $\omega:\mathcal X\times[0,1]\to\mathbb R$ is the population target weight, jointly measurable and identifiable from the observed law, with $\mathbb E_X[\int_0^1|\omega(X,u)|\,\mathrm du]<\infty$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:discrete_lower_bound}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: With $X$ almost surely constant, $\omega$ is written as a function $\omega(u)$ of latent resistance alone. In Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}, Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}, Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}, Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\omega(x,u)=\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}[\indicator(u\leqslant q(Z,X))-\indicator(u\leqslant p(Z,X))\mid X=x]$, where $q(z,x)=\phi(z,x,p(z,x))$ and $\phi$ is known; the target is the unscaled numerator $\mathbb E[Y^q-Y]$.
- $\theta_\omega$: $\theta_\omega=\mathbb E_X\int_0^1\text{MTE}(X,u)\omega(X,u)\,\mathrm du$ denotes the target under the latent law being considered, equivalently $\mathbb E[Y(1)\omega(X,U)]-\mathbb E[Y(0)\omega(X,U)]$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:sharpness}: When written with candidate-law arguments, $\theta_\omega(\pi_0,\pi_1)=\mathbb E_{\pi_1}[Y(1)\omega(X,U)]-\mathbb E_{\pi_0}[Y(0)\omega(X,U)]$, with $(\pi_0,\pi_1)\in\Gamma(\mathbb P_{obs})$; its value varies with the candidate marginal laws.
- $\theta_{\omega,w}$: For $w\in\{0,1\}$, $\theta_{\omega,w}(\pi_w)=\mathbb E_{\pi_w}[Y(w)\omega(X,U)]$ is the component functional of the candidate joint law $\pi_w$ of $(Y(w),U,X)$, so that $\theta_\omega=\theta_{\omega,1}-\theta_{\omega,0}$.
- $\hat{\underline{\theta}}_{\omega,1}$: $\hat{\underline\theta}_{\omega,1}$ denotes the sample-based estimator of the treated-component sharp lower bound, formed by averaging fitted score contributions over $I_2$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\hat{\underline\theta}_{\omega,1}=|I_2|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_2}\left[\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\left(\hat\psi_{\text{full},k}^{\text{prod}}(O_i;\hat\eta)+\sum_{j=1}^{l_k}\hat\psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}(O_i;\hat\eta)\right)+\hat\psi_{\Delta_K}(O_i;\hat\eta)\right]$, with the nuisance list and score formulas appropriate to the continuous or binary outcome regime. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\hat{\underline\theta}_{\omega,1}=|I_2|^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_2}(\hat\psi_1(O_i)+\hat\psi_2(O_i)+\hat\psi_3(O_i))$, where the three contributions substitute estimated nuisances into the three terms of equation~\ref{eq:continuous_simplified_target}, with predictions clipped to their known supports. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The nuisance estimates are those supplied by Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument}, including the local-linear regression and treated boundary conditional quantile construction.
- $\pi_w$: For $w\in\{0,1\}$, $\pi_w$ denotes a joint probability law of $(Y(w),U,X)$, including a candidate such law when varied in the CCOT program, and $\pi_w(\cdot\mid u,x)$ denotes a measurable regular conditional outcome law determined only $\pi_w$-almost everywhere with respect to its $(U,X)$-marginal.
- $\tilde{\pi}$: $\tilde{\pi}$ is a probability measure on $\mathcal Y^2\times[0,1]\times\mathcal X$, interpreted as a candidate joint law of $(Y(0),Y(1),U,X)$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:sharpness}: Its marginals satisfy $(\tilde{\pi}_0,\tilde{\pi}_1)\in\Gamma(\mathbb P_{obs})$, hence its $(U,X)$-marginal is $\mathrm du\,\mathbb P_{obs}(\mathrm dx)$; the coupling of $Y(0)$ and $Y(1)$ conditional on $(U,X)$ is unrestricted apart from those marginal constraints.
- $\tilde{\pi}_w$: For $w\in\{0,1\}$, $\tilde{\pi}_w$ is the $(Y(w),U,X)$-marginal of $\tilde{\pi}$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:sharpness}: The pair satisfies $(\tilde{\pi}_0,\tilde{\pi}_1)\in\Gamma(\mathbb P_{obs})$, so $\tilde{\pi}_w(\mathrm du,\mathrm dx)=\mathrm du\,\mathbb P_{obs}(\mathrm dx)$ and its conditional outcome kernels satisfy the observational constraints in (\ref{eq:ot_formulation}); these kernels are defined up to $(\mathrm du\otimes\mathbb P_{obs}(\mathrm dx))$-null sets.
- $\mathcal{R}_w$: $\mathcal R_w$ is the selected-region map for $w\in\{0,1\}$ and $p\in[0,1]$, with $\mathcal R_1(p)=(0,p]$ and $\mathcal R_0(p)=(p,1]$, including empty intervals at the corresponding boundary propensities.
- $H_{w,x}$: For $w\in\{0,1\}$ and almost every $x$, $H_{w,x}$ is the unique total-variation-continuous version on $\operatorname{supp}(P\mid X=x)$ of $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Y\in\mathrm dy,W=w\mid P=p,X=x)$, including support endpoints; its total mass is $p$ for $w=1$ and $1-p$ for $w=0$, and without covariates it is written $H_w$.
- $H_w$: $H_w$, $w\in\{0,1\}$, denotes the no-covariate canonical continuous version of $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Y\in\mathrm{d}y,W=w\mid P=p)$ on $\operatorname{supp}(P)$, uniquely determined there by its almost-sure observed values. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: $H_1(0,\cdot)=0$ is specified even when $0$ lies outside propensity support, so the initial gap uses a structural boundary submeasure.
- $K_{w,x}$: $K_{w,x}(\mathrm dy\mid u)$, for $w\in\{0,1\}$, is a regular conditional probability kernel for $Y(w)$ given $(U,X)=(u,x)$ under a compatible latent law, determined only $\mathbb P_{U,X}$-almost everywhere and not necessarily identified, with $H_{w,x}(p,\mathrm dy)=\int_{\mathcal R_w(p)}K_{w,x}(\mathrm dy\mid u)\,\mathrm du$ on the conditional propensity support for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$.
- $\mu$: $\mu$ denotes a probability measure on the state space appropriate to the coupling or quantile expression in which it occurs. In Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}: $\mu\in\mathcal P(\mathbb R)$, and either $\mu$ has bounded support and $\nu$ has a finite first moment, or vice versa.
- $\nu$: $\nu$ denotes a probability measure in transport notation, a locally bound candidate real-valued function of $X$ inside the conditional quantile-regression minimization, or an unperturbed scalar quantile boundary in a local boundary-perturbation calculation. In Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}: $\nu\in\mathcal P(\mathbb R)$, and either $\mu$ has bounded support and $\nu$ has a finite first moment, or vice versa. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome quantile-regression minimization, $\nu$ is a candidate function $\mathcal X\to\mathbb R$; the resulting population threshold is $\nu_{j,k}(x)=Q_{Y,k\mid x}(\kappa_{j,k}(x))$ for $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, while $\nu_{0,k}(x)=y_{\min}$ is fixed. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}: In the continuous-outcome proof's indicator-boundary calculation, unindexed $\nu$ denotes the scalar value of the threshold being perturbed at a given covariate value: $a_t=\nu+t\,\delta\nu$, and $\int_{\nu}^{a_t}(a_t-y)\,\mathrm{d}F(y)$ uses the oriented-integral convention when $a_t<\nu$.
- $c$: In transport notation, $c$ is the specified cost function on the two transported state spaces; a conditional cost $c(y,u,x)$ is read at fixed $x$ as the function $(y,u)\mapsto y\omega(x,u)$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}: $\mathcal A=\mathcal B=\mathbb R$ and $c(a,b)=a\cdot b$.
- $\xi$: In transport notation, $\xi\sim\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$ is the auxiliary variable shared by both coordinates of $(Q_\mu(\xi),Q_\nu(1-\xi))$ or $(Q_\mu(\xi),Q_\nu(\xi))$.
- $K$: $K$ is a population count in propensity decompositions and a chosen function when written as the local-linear smoothing kernel $K(t)$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:discrete_lower_bound}: $K=|\{p(z):z\in\mathcal Z,\ p(z)>0\}|$, excluding the structural boundary $p_0=0$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: $K$ is the number of ordered maximal closed intervals in $p(\mathcal Z)$ after merging overlaps, including degenerate singleton intervals and any interval containing zero. In Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}, Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}, Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $K$ counts the distinct positive baseline propensity levels at each $x$ and does not depend on $x$; $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ indexes the identified intervals and $k=K$ indexes the residual tail. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $K(t)$ is the base one-dimensional smoothing kernel in $K_{h_n}(t)=K(t/h_n)/h_n$, rather than a propensity-level count.
- $p_i$: $p_i$ denotes an ordered unique positive baseline propensity level for $1\leqslant i\leqslant K$, with structural integration boundaries $p_0=0$ and $p_{K+1}=1$; a structural boundary need not be an attained positive propensity level. In Theorem~\ref{thm:discrete_lower_bound}: The levels satisfy $0=p_0<p_1<\cdots<p_K\leqslant p_{K+1}=1$ and are the unique positive values of $p(z)$; $p_K=1$ makes the residual interval empty. In Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}, Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}, Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: The levels are functions $p_i(x)$ of the covariates, with $p_0(x)=0$ and $p_{K+1}(x)=1$. The positive level count, equality classes $S_i$, and their strict ordering are invariant in $x$ under the maintained separation condition, although the numerical levels may vary with $x$; $p_i$ without a hat denotes the population level.
- $I_i$: $I_i=(p_i,p_{i+1}]$ for $i=0,\ldots,K-1$, and $I_K=(p_K,1]$; the first $K$ intervals are nonempty and the residual interval is empty when $p_K=1$.
- $\mu_{1,i}$: $\mu_{1,i}$ is the identified conditional probability law of $Y(1)$ given $U\in I_i$, defined for $i=0,\ldots,K-1$.
- $Q_{Y,i}$: $Q_{Y,i}:(0,1)\to\mathcal Y$ is the quantile function of the identified conditional law $\mu_{1,i}$ of $Y(1)$ given $U\in(p_i,p_{i+1}]$, for $i=0,\ldots,K-1$.
- $Q_{\omega,i}$: $Q_{\omega,i}$ is the quantile function of the law of $\omega(U)$ under $U\sim\mathrm{Unif}(p_i,p_{i+1})$, for $t\in(0,1)$ and $i=0,\ldots,K-1$, using the nonempty identified baseline intervals.
- $\underline{p}_k$: $\underline p_k$ denotes the left endpoint of the $k$-th maximal closed interval in the propensity range ordered from left to right, allowing singleton intervals. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: The endpoints are indexed by $k=1,\ldots,K$ in $p(\mathcal Z)=\bigcup_{k=1}^K[\underline p_k,\overline p_k]$, with the additional boundary convention $\underline p_{K+1}=1$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: For almost every $x$, the endpoint is $\underline p_k(x)$, indexed by $k=1,\ldots,K_x$ in the conditional range $p(\mathcal Z,x)$, with $\underline p_{K_x+1}(x)=1$; both endpoints and the component count may vary with $x$.
- $\overline{p}_k$: $\overline p_k$ denotes the right endpoint of the $k$-th maximal closed interval in the propensity range ordered from left to right, allowing singleton intervals and using zero as the auxiliary initial endpoint. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: The endpoints are indexed by $k=1,\ldots,K$ in $p(\mathcal Z)=\bigcup_{k=1}^K[\underline p_k,\overline p_k]$, with $\overline p_0=0$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: For almost every $x$, the endpoint is $\overline p_k(x)$, indexed by $k=1,\ldots,K_x$ in $p(\mathcal Z,x)$, with $\overline p_0(x)=0$; endpoints and the component count may vary with $x$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}, Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}: Each conditional range component is a singleton: $\underline p_k(x)=\overline p_k(x)=p_k(x)$ for $k=1,\ldots,K$, with the structural initial endpoint $p_0(x)=\overline p_0(x)=0$.
- $K_x$: For almost every $x$, $K_x$ is the finite number of closed maximal intervals, including singleton intervals, obtained by merging overlapping component images in $p(\mathcal Z,x)$; its value may depend on $x$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}: The instrument domain is connected, so $K_x=1$ and the sole conditional range interval is $[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$, possibly a singleton.
- $L_k$: $L_k$ denotes the $k$-th closed maximal interval of the propensity range, ordered from left to right, including singleton intervals. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: $L_k=[\underline p_k,\overline p_k]$ for $k=1,\ldots,K$; singleton intervals contribute a Lebesgue-null integral. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: For almost every $x$, $L_k(x)=[\underline p_k(x),\overline p_k(x)]$ for $k=1,\ldots,K_x$; the intervals are those of $p(\mathcal Z,x)$ and may be singletons.
- $G_k$: $G_k$ denotes the open interval between consecutive ordered propensity-range components, with the initial gap starting at zero and with the final unconstrained tail excluded from the gap indices. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: $G_k=(\overline p_k,\underline p_{k+1})$ for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$, with $\overline p_0=0$; empty gaps are omitted from gap-specific definitions and contribute zero. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: $G_k(x)=(\overline p_k(x),\underline p_{k+1}(x))$ for $k=0,\ldots,K_x-1$ and almost every $x$, with $\overline p_0(x)=0$; empty conditional gaps contribute zero. In Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}, Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}: The propensity-range components are singletons, so $G_k(x)=(p_k(x),p_{k+1}(x))$ for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$, with $p_0(x)=0$; the interval beyond $p_K(x)$ is the separate trivial tail.
- $\mu_{1,k}$: $\mu_{1,k}$ is the identified marginal law $\mathcal L(Y(1)\mid U\in G_k)$ on each nonempty gap $G_k$, $k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}$, obtained from the canonical treated submeasures, including the structural zero submeasure at the initial boundary.
- $\mu_{1,k\mid x}$: $\mu_{1,k\mid x}$ is the population probability law of $Y(1)$ conditional on $X=x$ and $U\in G_k(x)$, defined for nonempty gaps with $k=0,\ldots,K_x-1$; empty-gap terms are omitted and contribute zero. In Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}, Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}, Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}: With finite $\mathcal Z$, the conditional propensity-range intervals are singletons, $G_k(x)=(p_k(x),p_{k+1}(x))$ for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$, and $\mu_{1,k\mid x}$ is the corresponding conditional complier outcome law.
- $Q_{Y,k}$: $Q_{Y,k}$ is the quantile function of $\mu_{1,k}$ for $t\in(0,1)$ and $k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}$ such that $\overline p_k<\underline p_{k+1}$; an empty gap has zero contribution and requires no quantile function.
- $Q_{\omega,k}$: $Q_{\omega,k}$ is the quantile function of the law of $\omega(U)$ under $U\sim\mathrm{Unif}(G_k)$, defined for $t\in(0,1)$ and $k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}$ such that $\overline p_k<\underline p_{k+1}$; an empty gap has zero contribution and requires no quantile function.
- $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$: $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ is the population quantile function of $\mu_{1,k\mid x}$ for each nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$; it is required only on such gaps, and contributions from empty gaps are defined as zero. In Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}, Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}, Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}: In the finite-instrument setting, the gap is the baseline interval between $p_k(x)$ and $p_{k+1}(x)$, with $k=0,\ldots,K-1$, and $\mu_{1,k\mid x}$ is its identified conditional complier outcome law.
- $Q_{\omega,k\mid x}$: $Q_{\omega,k\mid x}$ is defined for each nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, $0\leqslant k<K_x$, as the quantile function of the pushforward of $\mathrm{Unif}(G_k(x))$ by $u\mapsto\omega(x,u)$; empty-gap contributions are zero without requiring a quantile function there. In Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}: The conditional propensity-range intervals are singletons, so the relevant gap law is $\mathrm{Unif}((p_k(x),p_{k+1}(x)))$ for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$, with $p_0(x)=0$.
- $g_1^{\mathrm c}$: $g_1^{\mathrm c}(p,x)=\int_{\mathcal Y}y\,H_{1,x}(p,\mathrm dy)$ uses the canonical population observed submeasure on the conditional propensity support, including its endpoints; it is absolutely continuous on each non-degenerate LIV interval, and its derivatives mean intervalwise Lebesgue-a.e. derivatives. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: With no covariates, $g_1^{\mathrm c}(p)=\int_{\mathcal Y}y\,H_1(p,\mathrm dy)$, and derivatives are taken on the non-degenerate intervals $L_k$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}: $g_1=g_1^{\mathrm c}$ on the connected conditional support $[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$.
- $\mathcal A$: $\mathcal A$ is reused contextually for the first marginal's state space in the OT primer and for the explicitly specified countable measure-determining class in the LIV argument. In Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}: $\mathcal A=\mathbb R$ is the state space carrying the first marginal $\mu$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: In the LIV proof, $\mathcal A=\{\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,r]:r\in\mathbb Q\}\cup\{\mathcal Y\}$ is the countable measure-determining class of outcome events used to obtain a common exceptional null set.
- $p_k$: $p_k(x)$ denotes an ordered population baseline propensity level, with $p_k(x)=p(z,x)$ for $z\in S_k$ and $k=1,\ldots,K$, while $p_0(x)=0$ and $p_{K+1}(x)=1$ are structural integration boundaries; $K$ and the level sets $S_k$ are invariant in $x$.
- $q_{j,k}$: $q_{j,k}(x)$ denotes a population policy level for $k=0,\ldots,K$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, ordered distinctly within its baseline bin, with $T_{j,k}=\{z\in\mathcal Z:q(z,x)=q_{j,k}(x)\}$; $K,l_k,T_{j,k}$ are invariant in $x$, and $q_{0,k},q_{l_k+1,k}$ are integration boundaries with empty associated instrument sets. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: For every nonempty $T_{j,k}$ in a nonfinal bin $k<K$, $q_{j,k}(x)\neq p_k(x)$; the structural boundary $q_{0,k}=p_k$ is therefore distinct from every regular policy boundary used by the displayed scores.
- $l_k$: $l_k\in\mathbb N_0$, for $k=0,\ldots,K$, counts distinct population policy levels rather than instruments, is invariant in $x$ under the stipulated invariant ordering, and excludes structural integration boundaries while retaining an actual policy value equal to one in the final bin.
- $S_k$: $S_k=\{z\in\mathcal Z:p(z,x)=p_k(x)\}$ denotes the population baseline instrument level set for $k=1,\ldots,K$, invariant in $x$ under the maintained equality-class convention; $S_0=\{z\in\mathcal Z:p(z,x)=0\}$, when nonempty, is the structural-zero class, and $\widehat S_k$ denotes a recovered set rather than $S_k$ itself. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: At $k=0$, minus-side conditional expectations and residuals are omitted and assigned zero only for notation; no treated conditional regression on $S_0$ or division by $\pi_0(X)$ is used.
- $T_{j,k}$: $T_{j,k}$ denotes the population alternative-policy equality class for $k=0,\ldots,K$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, invariant in $x$ under the stipulated invariant ordering; structural integration-boundary sets are empty, an actual policy value equal to $p_k(x)$ belongs to $T_{1,k}$, and an actual value equal to one is retained in $T_{l_K,K}$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: For $k=0,\ldots,K-1$, every nonempty $T_{j,k}$ has $q_{j,k}(x)\neq p_k(x)$ for all $x\in\mathcal X$.
- $R_x$: $R_x=\{p(z,x),q(z,x):z\in\mathcal Z\}$ is the set of distinct baseline and alternative-policy propensity values at the covariate value $x$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}, Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: Distinct elements of $R_x$ are separated by more than $c_{\text{gap}}$, uniformly in $x$; the equality classes and their strict ordering are invariant in $x$, although the numerical propensity values may vary with $x$.
- $c_{\text{gap}}$: $c_{\text{gap}}$ is one common positive separation constant for distinct values in the combined baseline and alternative-policy range, uniformly over $x\in\mathcal X$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: Level recovery joins averaged fitted values lying within $c_{\mathrm{gap}}/2$ of each other, where $c_{\mathrm{gap}}$ denotes this same separation constant.
- $c_\pi$: $c_\pi$ is a common positive overlap constant for the specified nonempty level sets, with no overlap requirement on the structural index $k=0$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: The same $c_\pi$ determines the clipping interval $[c_\pi/2,1]$ for $\hat\pi_k$ and $\hat\tau_{j,k}$.
- $c_h$: $c_h$ is one positive constant uniform over the indicated indices and almost every covariate value, including the fixed lower boundary $q_{0,k}=p_k$.
- $\gamma_{\text{full},k}$: $\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)$ is the population difference of the two conditional probabilities in its defining display, taking values in $[-1,1]$ for $k=0,\ldots,K$, with $\gamma_{\mathrm{full},K}\equiv0$ by empty unions; the full-interval products use $k=0,\ldots,K-1$.
- $\gamma_{j,k}$: $\gamma_{j,k}(x)$ is the population conditional probability of $\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k}$, taking values in $[0,1]$ for $k=0,\ldots,K$ and $j=0,\ldots,l_k$, with $T_{0,k}=\emptyset$; the sub-interval products use $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$.
- $\gamma_K$: $\gamma_K(x)$ is the population conditional probability of $\bigcup_{j=1}^{l_K}T_{j,K}$ in the final closed policy bin $[p_K(x),1]$, taking values in $[0,1]$ and equaling zero when $l_K=0$; actual policy levels equal to one are included.
- $\pi_k$: $\pi_k(x)=\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z\in S_k\mid X=x)$ is a population conditional probability taking values in $[0,1]$, with fitted baseline nuisances indexed by $k=1,\ldots,K$; no $\pi_0$ regression or inverse-probability correction is fitted. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\pi_k(x)\geqslant c_\pi>0$ for every $x\in\mathcal X$ and $k=1,\ldots,K$.
- $\tau_{j,k}$: $\tau_{j,k}(x)=\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z\in T_{j,k}\mid X=x)$ is a population conditional probability in $[0,1]$, with fitted policy-set nuisances indexed by nonempty $T_{j,k}$, $k=0,\ldots,K$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$; structural boundary sets are empty and have probability zero. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\tau_{j,k}(x)\geqslant c_\pi>0$ for every $x\in\mathcal X$ and every nonempty $T_{j,k}$.
- $a_{j,k}$: $a_{j,k}(z,x)=\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z=z\mid Z\in T_{j,k},X=x)$ is the population assignment-share function for each nonempty policy set, indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, with $a_{j,k}(z,x)=0$ outside $T_{j,k}$ and $\sum_{z\in T_{j,k}}a_{j,k}(z,x)=1$ wherever the conditioning probability is positive.
- $\kappa_{j,k}$: $\kappa_{j,k}(x)$ is the population relative threshold for a nonempty baseline interval, indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=0,\ldots,l_k+1$, with $\kappa_{0,k}(x)=0$ and $\kappa_{l_k+1,k}(x)=1$.
- $J_{\text{full},k}$: $J_{\text{full},k}(x)$ is the population full-interval contribution $(p_{k+1}(x)-p_k(x))\mathbb E_{\mu_{1,k\mid x}}[Y]$ for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}, Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the binary-outcome case, $J_{\text{full},k}(x)=P_{1,k+1}(x)-P_{1,k}(x)$, where $P_{1,k}(x)=\mathbb E_{\text{obs}}[YW\mid Z\in S_k,X=x]$ for $k\geqslant1$ and $P_{1,0}(x)=0$.
- $J_{j,k}$: $J_{j,k}(x)=(p_{k+1}(x)-p_k(x))\int_{\kappa_{j,k}(x)}^{\kappa_{j+1,k}(x)}Q_{Y,k\mid x}(u)\,\mathrm du$ is the population lower-bound subinterval contribution on a nondegenerate identified bin, with structural thresholds $\kappa_{0,k}=0$ and $\kappa_{l_k+1,k}=1$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}, Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}, Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: The bound uses $J_{j-1,k}$ for $0\leq k<K$ and $1\leq j\leq l_k$. In the continuous-outcome representation, for $0\leq j<l_k$ and under the stated continuity conditions, $J_{j,k}=(p_{k+1}-p_k)\mathbb E_{\mu_{1,k\mid X}}[Y\indicator(\nu_{j,k}<Y\leqslant\nu_{j+1,k})]$; in the binary-outcome case, $J_{j-1,k}=h_{j,k}^{-}$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome score construction the plug-in component is $J_{j,k}=p_{k+1}J_{j,k}^{+}-p_kJ_{j,k}^{-}$; at $k=0$, the minus-side conditional expectation is omitted and set to zero for notation.
- $\Delta_K$: $\Delta_K(x)=y_{\min}\mathbb E_{\text{obs}}[\max\{0,q(Z,X)-p_K(X)\}\mid X=x]$ is the conditional lower-bound contribution on $(p_K(x),1]$, with the final policy bin $[p_K(x),1]$ retaining instruments whose alternative propensity equals one.
- $\nu_{j,k}$: $\nu_{j,k}$ denotes the population boundary function for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=0,\ldots,l_k$, with $\nu_{0,k}(x)\equiv y_{\min}$ and $\nu_{j,k}(x)=Q_{Y,k\mid x}(\kappa_{j,k}(x))$ for $j\geqslant1$; $\hat\nu_{j,k}$ denotes its estimate. In Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}: In part (i), the gap law $\mu_{1,k\mid X}$ is continuous at the two boundaries $\nu_{j,k}(X)$ and $\nu_{j+1,k}(X)$ used in each band, almost surely. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome part, the relevant treated conditional laws have uniformly bounded densities in fixed neighborhoods of $\nu_{j,k}(X)$ for $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, and no atom at the fixed boundary $\nu_{0,k}=y_{\min}$; only the boundaries with $j\geqslant1$ are estimated.
- $P_{1,k}$: For $k=1,\ldots,K$, $P_{1,k}(x)=\mathbb E_{\mathrm{obs}}[YW\mid Z\in S_k,X=x]$ is the population conditional treated-success regression in the binary-outcome setting, while $P_{1,0}(x)\equiv0$ is a structural boundary convention rather than a conditional regression on $S_0$.
- $h_{j,k}^{-}$: $h_{j,k}^{-}(x)$ is the binary-outcome population subinterval contribution for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, with $q_{0,k}(x)=p_k(x)$, and equals $J_{j-1,k}(x)$.
- $n$: $n$ is the total observed sample size indexing the estimation sequences and their asymptotics. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $n$ ranges over sample sizes permitting the stipulated equal-half split, so $n$ is even.
- $I_0$: $I_0$ denotes the latent interval $(p_0,p_1]=(0,p_1]$ in the latent-interval decomposition and a sample index set contained in $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ in sample splitting. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $I_0$ is the sample index set in the three-way partition $I_0,I_1,I_2$; $I_0\cup I_1$ supplies the training samples on which the target-evaluation expectations are conditioned, and $I_2$ supplies evaluation observations, with $|I_j|/n\to\rho_j\in(0,1)$ for $j=0,1,2$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $I_0$ trains $\hat p$ and the derived policy and endpoint fits; conditional on this training sample, $I_1$ trains the outcome regression and treated boundary-law learner.
- $I_1$: $I_1$ denotes $(p_1,p_2]$ in the latent-resistance decomposition and a subset of $\{1,\ldots,n\}$ in the estimation procedures. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $I_1$ is the auxiliary sample index set in the random disjoint partition into $I_1$ and $I_2$, each of size $n/2$; the propensity and remaining score nuisances are fitted on $I_1$, with level recovery using the covariates in $I_2$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $I_1$ is the nuisance sample index set in the three-way partition into $I_0,I_1,I_2$, with $|I_1|/n\to\rho_1\in(0,1)$; $I_0\cup I_1$ supplies the training samples and $I_2$ supplies target evaluation. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: Conditional on the propensity fit from $I_0$, $I_1$ is used to fit $\hat g_1$ and the treated boundary law.
- $I_2$: $I_2$ denotes the latent interval $(p_2,p_3]$ in the interval decomposition and the main evaluation sample index set in estimation. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $I_2\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}$ is the main estimation index set, disjoint from $I_1$, with $I_1\cup I_2=\{1,\ldots,n\}$ and $|I_2|=n/2$; its covariates are also used to recover the finite level sets before evaluation scores are averaged over it. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $I_2\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}$ is the evaluation index set in the disjoint partition $I_0,I_1,I_2$, with $|I_j|/n\to\rho_j\in(0,1)$ for $j=0,1,2$; it evaluates the target using nuisances fitted on $I_0$ and $I_1$.
- $\eta$: $\eta$ collects the nuisance functions entering the applicable bound and estimation formula, with its components determined by the instrument and outcome regime and $\hat\eta$ denoting their fitted counterparts. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In part~\textup{(i)}, $\eta(x)=\{p(z,x),p_k(x),\pi_k(x),\tau_{j,k}(x),a_{j,k}(z,x),\gamma_{\text{full},k}(x),\gamma_{j,k}(x),\gamma_K(x),\nu_{j,k}(x),M_{j,k}^{\pm}(x),J_{j,k}^{\pm}(x),J_{\text{full},k}^{\pm}(x)\}_{j,k}$; $\pi_k$ and minus-side objects are included only for $k\geqslant1$, and estimated quantiles have $j\geqslant1$, with structural boundary values fixed. In part~\textup{(ii)}, $\eta(x)=\{p(z,x),p_k(x),\pi_k(x),\tau_{j,k}(x),a_{j,k}(z,x),\gamma_{\text{full},k}(x),\gamma_{j,k}(x),\gamma_K(x),P_{1,k}(x)\}_{j,k}$, with $p_0=P_{1,0}=0$ and no fitted $\pi_0$; within-set share directions satisfy $\sum_{z\in T_{j,k}}\delta a_{j,k}(z,x)=0$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: The unaccented $\eta$ in $\sigma^2=\mathbb E[(\psi(O;\eta)-\underline\theta_{\omega,1})^2]$ denotes $\eta_0$, the true nuisance vector. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The nuisance functions are those entering the connected-instrument plug-in representation: $p$, the policy propensity and support endpoints derived from it, $g_1$, and the treated boundary law or its quantile function.
- $\hat\eta$: $\hat\eta$ is the vector of fitted nuisance functions entering the applicable bound estimator, with its components determined by the instrument and outcome regime. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: The first stage is fitted on $I_1$ and the remaining nuisances are fitted on $I_1$ given the recovered level sets, subject to the stated clipping conventions. Feasible recovery uses $\{X_i:i\in I_2\}$; in the oracle procedure the population level sets are used and $\hat\eta$ is fixed conditional on $I_1$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The fitted nuisances enter (\ref{eq:estimator_continuous_instrument}), including estimates of $p$, the derived policy and support endpoints, $g_1$, and the treated boundary law or its quantile function; they are trained on $I_0\cup I_1$ and held fixed conditional on these training samples for evaluation on $I_2$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The concrete procedure fits $p$ on $I_0$ and, conditional on that first stage, fits $g_1$ and the treated boundary law on $I_1$, with predictions clipped to their stated supports.
- $\eta_0$: $\eta_0$ is the vector of population nuisance values for the applicable score, including its known structural-boundary conventions, distinct from the fitted vector $\hat\eta$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In part~\textup{(i)}, $\eta_0$ supplies the true nuisances for the continuous-outcome score, including boundary quantiles and conditional indicator and treated-outcome regressions; in part~\textup{(ii)}, it supplies the binary-outcome nuisances $p_k$, $P_{1,k}$, $\pi_k$, $\tau_{j,k}$, $a_{j,k}$, $\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}$, $\gamma_{j,k}$, and $\gamma_K$.
- $\delta$: The notation $\delta$ denotes a nuisance perturbation direction, whereas the indexed notation $\delta_n$ denotes a fixed boundary-localization window sequence. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\delta$ lies in the same function space as $\eta$ and satisfies that $\eta_0+t\delta$ is admissible for every $t$ near zero; fixed structural components have zero perturbation, and within-set share components satisfy $\sum_{z\in T_{j,k}}\delta a_z(X)=0$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\delta_n\to0$ is the localization window in $\mathcal I_{\delta,1}=\{i\in I_1:W_i=1,\ \hat P_i\leqslant\hat{\underline p}(X_i)+\delta_n\}$, indexed by sample size $n$.
- $\mathcal T$: $\mathcal T$ consists of directions in the nuisance function space for which $\eta_0+t\delta$ remains admissible for every $t$ in a neighborhood of zero; these preserve fixed structural components and satisfy $\sum_{z\in T_{j,k}}\delta a_z(X)=0$ for each within-set share vector.
- $\psi$: $\psi$ denotes the uncentered total population integrand associated with the estimator of $\underline\theta_{\omega,1}$, with its nuisance arguments supplied explicitly when needed. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\psi(O;\eta)=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\bigl(\psi_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\mathrm{prod}}(O;\eta)+\sum_{j=1}^{l_k}\psi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{prod}}(O;\eta)\bigr)+\psi_{\Delta_K}(O;\eta)$, with the component formulas in Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous} for continuous outcomes and Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete} for binary outcomes; $\eta_0$ supplies the true nuisance values. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\psi(O)=-\int_{\min\{\underline p(X),q(Z,X)\}}^{\underline p(X)}Q_{1,\underline p\mid X}(v/\underline p(X))\,\mathrm dv+g_1(q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X),X)-g_1(P,X)+y_{\min}(q(Z,X)-\overline p(X))_+$, with the first integral zero when $\underline p(X)=0$; this is the population plug-in integrand.
- $\hat\psi$: $\hat\psi$ denotes the total fitted integrand averaged over $I_2$ to form $\hat{\underline\theta}_{\omega,1}$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\hat\psi(O;\hat\eta)=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\bigl(\hat\psi_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\mathrm{prod}}(O;\hat\eta)+\sum_{j=1}^{l_k}\hat\psi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{prod}}(O;\hat\eta)\bigr)+\hat\psi_{\Delta_K}(O;\hat\eta)$, using the continuous-outcome or binary-outcome score formulas according to the outcome regime. In the oracle procedure used in the proof, this fitted function is fixed conditional on $I_1$; the feasible procedure uses recovered level sets. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}: $\hat\psi=\hat\psi_1+\hat\psi_2+\hat\psi_3$, obtained by substituting fitted nuisances into the three terms of (\ref{eq:continuous_simplified_target}); this is the plug-in integrand, fixed conditional on $I_0,I_1$, rather than the finite-instrument orthogonal score.
- $J_{j,k}^{+}$: $J_{j,k}^{+}$ is the population treated conditional band-mean function at the true boundaries $\nu_{j,k}$ and $\nu_{j+1,k}$, indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=0,\ldots,l_k-1$, with conditioning on $Z\in S_{k+1}$, $W=1$, and $X=x$.
- $J_{j,k}^{-}$: $J_{j,k}^{-}(x)$ is the population truncated first moment $\mathbb E[Y\indicator(\nu_{j,k}(x)<Y\leqslant\nu_{j+1,k}(x))\mid X=x,Z\in S_k,W=1]$ for $k\in\{1,\ldots,K-1\}$ and $j\in\{0,\ldots,l_k-1\}$, using population quantile boundaries, with its $k=0$ value defined as zero solely for notational convenience and every corresponding minus-side score term omitted.
- $J_{\text{full},k}^{+}$: $J_{\text{full},k}^{+}(x)$ is the population conditional mean $\mathbb E[Y\mid X=x,Z\in S_{k+1},W=1]$ for $k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}$.
- $J_{\text{full},k}^{-}$: $J_{\text{full},k}^{-}(x)$ is the population conditional mean $\mathbb E[Y\mid X=x,Z\in S_k,W=1]$ for $k\in\{1,\ldots,K-1\}$, with its $k=0$ value defined as zero solely for notational convenience and every corresponding minus-side score term omitted.
- $M_{j,k}^{+}$: $M_{j,k}^{+}$ is the population conditional indicator-mean function at the true boundary $\nu_{j,k}$, indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=0,\ldots,l_k$, with conditioning on $Z\in S_{k+1}$, $W=1$, and $X=x$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome part, the no-atom condition at $y_{\min}$ and $\nu_{0,k}=y_{\min}$ imply $M_{0,k}^{+}(X)=0$ almost surely.
- $M_{j,k}^{-}$: $M_{j,k}^{-}(x)$ is the population conditional indicator mean among treated observations with $Z\in S_k$ at the continuous-outcome boundary $\nu_{j,k}(x)$ for $k\geqslant1$; at $k=0$ this minus-side regression is omitted and defined as zero solely for notational convenience.
- $R_{q,j,k}$: $R_{q,j,k}(O)$ pools the propensity residuals of the instruments sharing one nonempty regular policy level, normalizing by the total level-set probability $\tau_{j,k}(X)$, while its structural integration-boundary indices satisfy $R_{q,0,k}=R_{q,l_k+1,k}\equiv0$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: For $k<K$, a nonempty regular policy level is distinct from $q_{0,k}=p_k$ under part~\textup{(ii)} of Assumption~\ref{asp:prop_gap}; multiple instruments may nevertheless share that regular policy level.
- $R_{P,k}$: $R_{P,k}(O)$ is the binary-outcome treated-success IPW residual for observed positive-propensity levels $1\leqslant k\leqslant K$, with $R_{P,0}\equiv0$ at the fixed structural boundary, without evaluating $\pi_0$ or a conditional regression on $S_0$.
- $R_{p,k}$: $R_{p,k}(O)$ is the baseline propensity IPW residual for observed positive-propensity levels $1\leqslant k\leqslant K$, with $R_{p,0}\equiv0$ at the fixed structural boundary $p_0\equiv0$, without evaluating $\pi_0$ or a conditional regression on $S_0$.
- $I_{j,k}^{-}$: $I_{j,k}^{-}(x)=\indicator\!\big(q_{j,k}(x)>\max\{q_{j-1,k}(x),p_{k+1}(x)-J_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)\}\big)=\indicator(h_{j,k}^{-}(x)>0)$ is the binary-score active-set indicator for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, with $q_{0,k}(x)=p_k(x)$.
- $D_{j,k}^{-}$: $D_{j,k}^{-}(x)=\indicator\!\big(q_{j-1,k}(x)<p_{k+1}(x)-J_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)<q_{j,k}(x)\big)$ is the binary-score boundary indicator for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, with $q_{0,k}(x)=p_k(x)$; it is zero when the integration boundary equals either sub-interval endpoint.
- $E_{j,k}^{-}$: $E_{j,k}^{-}(x)=\indicator\!\big(q_{j-1,k}(x)>p_{k+1}(x)-J_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)\big)$ is the binary-score dominance indicator for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, with $q_{0,k}(x)=p_k(x)$; its strict inequality assigns zero at equality.
- $\psi_{j,k}^J$: $\psi_{j,k}^{J}(O;\eta)$ is the continuous-outcome uncentered lower-bound score for $\mathbb E[J_{j,k}(X)]$, used for $0\leq k<K$ and $0\leq j<l_k$, with $R_{q,0,k}=R_{q,l_k+1,k}=0$, all minus-side terms omitted at $k=0$, and no standalone $+\nu_{0,k}$ term in $\alpha_{p_k}$ at $j=0$.
- $\psi_{\text{full},k}^J$: $\psi_{\text{full},k}^{J}(O;\eta)$ is the uncentered full-interval component score for $\mathbb E[J_{\text{full},k}(X)]$, for $0\leq k<K$, with no quantile-constraint correction and with structural-zero rather than conditional-regression terms at $k=0$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome case, use the displayed treated-outcome and propensity residual corrections, omitting all minus-side terms at $k=0$; in the binary-outcome case, use $\psi_{\text{full},k}^{J}=J_{\text{full},k}+R_{P,k+1}-R_{P,k}$ with $R_{P,0}=0$.
- $\psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}$: $\psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}(O;\eta)$ is the uncentered lower-bound fractional-interval product score for $0\leq k<K$ and $1\leq j\leq l_k$, using the conditional union-indicator residual $\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k}(X)$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome case, $\psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}=\gamma_{j,k}\psi_{j-1,k}^{J}+J_{j-1,k}(\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k})$; in the binary-outcome case, $\psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}=\gamma_{j,k}\psi_{j,k}^{h}+h_{j,k}^{-}(\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k})$, with covariate and observation arguments as in the defining displays.
- $\psi_{\text{full},k}^{\text{prod}}$: $\psi_{\text{full},k}^{\text{prod}}(O;\eta)$ is the uncentered lower-bound product score for $\mathbb E[\gamma_{\text{full},k}(X)J_{\text{full},k}(X)]$, for $0\leq k<K$, using the full-interval component score appropriate to the outcome setting and the difference of policy and baseline higher-bin indicators.
- $\psi_{\Delta_K}$: $\psi_{\Delta_K}(O;\eta)$ is the uncentered lower-bound score for $\mathbb E[\Delta_K(X)]$ on the final bin $[p_K(X),1]$, with the same formula for continuous and binary outcomes and with the union taken over the actual policy levels $j=1,\ldots,l_K$, including a policy value equal to one.
- $\psi_{j,k}^{h}$: $\psi_{j,k}^{h}(O;\eta)$ is the uncentered binary-outcome score for $\mathbb E[h_{j,k}^{-}(X)]$, indexed by $0\leq k<K$ and $1\leq j\leq l_k$, with $R_{P,0}=R_{p,0}=0$ and $R_{q,0,k}=0$, so the lower-policy-boundary correction vanishes at $j=1$ and its propensity correction is included in $\alpha_{p,k}^{(1,k)}$.
- $\underline{p}$: $\underline p(x)=\inf_{z\in\mathcal Z}p(z,x)$ is the population lower propensity endpoint at covariate value $x$, with values in $[0,1]$ and with zero allowed. In Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\underline p(x)$ is the lower endpoint of the connected conditional support $[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$ of $P\mid X=x$.
- $\overline{p}$: $\overline p(x)=\sup_{z\in\mathcal Z}p(z,x)$ is the population upper propensity endpoint at covariate value $x$, with values in $[0,1]$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\overline p(x)$ is the upper endpoint of the connected conditional support $[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$ of $P\mid X=x$.
- $g_1$: $g_1=g_1^{\mathrm c}$ denotes the canonical population treated regression obtained by integrating $y$ against the canonical observed treated submeasure, identified on the conditional propensity support including its endpoints, with intervalwise Lebesgue-a.e. derivatives on non-degenerate LIV intervals. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: With no covariates, $g_1(u)=\int_{\mathcal Y}y\,H_1(u,\mathrm dy)$; derivatives are taken separately on the non-degenerate intervals $L_k$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The identified conditional support is $[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$, and target evaluations use $q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X)$ and $P$ within this support.
- $\hat p$: $\hat p:\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\to[0,1]$ is a fitted estimator of the conditional treatment propensity $p(z,x)$, with fitted values clipped to $[0,1]$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\hat p$ is the pre-recovery first-stage fit trained on $I_1$; the level-recovery averages evaluate this fit at the covariates in $I_2$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\max_{z\in\mathcal Z}\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\|_{L_2}=o_P(n^{-1/4})$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\hat p$ is trained on the propensity sample $I_0$ and is held fixed conditional on $I_0$ when constructing the generated propensity index and localization rules on $I_1$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\|\hat p-p\|_\infty=O_P(r_{p,n})$.
- $\hat q$: $\hat q(z,x)=\phi(z,x,\hat p(z,x))\in[0,1]$ is the fitted alternative propensity obtained by substituting the clipped baseline propensity estimator into the known policy map. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\hat q$ is formed from the pre-recovery propensity fit $\hat p$ trained on $I_1$; the level-recovery average evaluates this fitted function at the covariates in $I_2$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\hat q$ is formed from $\hat p$ trained on $I_0$; the notation $\hat q=\phi(Z,X,\hat p(Z,X))$ denotes evaluation of this fitted function at $(Z,X)$.
- $\hat P$: $\hat P=\hat p(Z,X)$ denotes the fitted baseline propensity index, with observation-indexed notation $\hat P_i=\hat p(Z_i,X_i)$ and values in $[0,1]$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The fitted map $\hat p$ is held fixed conditional on the training samples $I_0,I_1$ when evaluating expressions involving $\hat P=\hat p(Z,X)$ under the target distribution of $(Z,X)$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The fitted map $\hat p$ is trained on $I_0$, so the selection rule involving $\hat P$ is fixed conditional on $I_0$.
- $\hat{\underline p}$: $\hat{\underline p}(x)=\inf_{z\in\mathcal Z}\hat p(z,x)$ is the fitted lower propensity endpoint, a data-dependent function of $x$ taking values in $[0,1]$, held fixed conditional on the training samples $I_0,I_1$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\hat{\underline p}$ is obtained from the propensity fit trained on $I_0$ and is therefore fixed conditional on $I_0$ in the selected-law definition.
- $\hat{\overline p}$: $\hat{\overline p}(x)=\sup_{z\in\mathcal Z}\hat p(z,x)\in[0,1]$ is the fitted upper propensity endpoint, with the fitted function held fixed in target evaluation conditional on $I_0,I_1$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The endpoint is computed from $\hat p$ fitted on $I_0$.
- $\hat q^{\mathrm{tr}}$: $\hat q^{\mathrm{tr}}(z,x)=\min\{\hat{\overline p}(x),\max\{\hat{\underline p}(x),\hat q(z,x)\}\}$ is the fitted policy propensity projected onto $[\hat{\underline p}(x),\hat{\overline p}(x)]$, with its fitted map held fixed in target evaluation conditional on $I_0,I_1$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The fitted map is constructed on $I_0$ from the clipped $\hat p$ and $\hat q(z,x)=\phi(z,x,\hat p(z,x))$.
- $\hat g_1$: $\hat g_1(u,x)$ is an estimated regression on $[0,1]\times\mathcal X$, clipped to the known range $[\min\{0,y_{\min}\},\max\{0,y_{\max}\}]$ of $YW$, and evaluated at the estimated target indices $\hat q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X)$ and $\hat P$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: At $u_m=m/M_g$, $m=0,\ldots,M_g$, the fitted value is the intercept in $(\hat g_1(u_m,\cdot),\hat\beta_m)\in\arg\min_{g,\beta\in\mathcal F_n}\sum_{i\in I_1}K_{h_n}(\hat P_i-u_m)[Y_iW_i-g(X_i)-\beta(X_i)(\hat P_i-u_m)]^2$; between grid points it is the clipped linear interpolant. The learner loss compares $\hat g_1(\hat U_\ell,X)$ with $g_1(\Pi_X(\hat U_\ell),X)$ for $\ell\in\{1,2\}$.
- $\hat\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}$: $\hat\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}$ is an estimated probability law on $\mathcal Y$, indexed by $x\in\mathcal X$, whose quantile function is $\widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}$ and whose target is the canonical treated boundary law; its target-error loss is weighted by $\underline p(x)$, so its value where $\underline p(x)=0$ does not affect the target. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The law is generated by conditional check-function fits on $\mathcal I_{\delta,1}=\{i\in I_1:W_i=1,\hat P_i\leqslant\hat{\underline p}(X_i)+\delta_n\}$ at $\tau_m=m/M_Q$, $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$, clipped to $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$, sorted at each $x$, and linearly interpolated between adjacent quantile levels; endpoint values may be $y_{\min},y_{\max}$ or the smallest and largest fitted values.
- $\widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}$: $\widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}$ denotes the quantile function of $\hat\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}$, with values in $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$ and endpoint values supplied by the chosen endpoint convention. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: At $\tau_m=m/M_Q$, $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$, fit $\widetilde Q(\tau_m\mid\cdot)\in\arg\min_{f\in\mathcal F_Q}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{\delta,1}}\rho_{\tau_m}(Y_i-f(X_i))$, setting every grid fit to $y_{\min}$ if the selected sample is empty; clip to $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$, sort the fitted values for each $x$, and interpolate linearly in $\tau$, with endpoints either $y_{\min},y_{\max}$ or the smallest and largest fitted values.
- $\hat\pi_k$: $\hat\pi_k(x)$ estimates $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z\in S_k\mid X=x)$ by fitting the indicator of the recovered set $\widehat S_k$ on $I_1$, for $k=1,\ldots,K$, and is clipped first to $[0,1]$ and then to $[c_\pi/2,1]$; no denominator estimator $\hat\pi_0$ is required at the structural boundary.
- $\hat\tau_{j,k}$: $\hat\tau_{j,k}(x)$ estimates $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z\in T_{j,k}\mid X=x)$ by fitting the indicator of the recovered set $\widehat T_{j,k}$ on $I_1$, for nonempty policy level sets with $k=0,\ldots,K$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, and is clipped first to $[0,1]$ and then to $[c_\pi/2,1]$.
- $r_{e,n}$: $r_{e,n}$ is a nonnegative rate sequence tending to zero controlling each of the three training-conditional absolute-error losses for the boundary and tail maps. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $r_{e,n}=O(r_{p,n})$ for the concrete nuisance construction.
- $r_{g,n}$: $r_{g,n}$ is a nonnegative rate sequence tending to zero for the sum of the two training-conditional target-evaluation losses, comparing fitted regressions at estimated indices with population regressions at the corresponding true indices. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $r_{g,n}=O(r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}+r_{p,n})$ for the concrete nuisance construction.
- $r_{\mu,n}$: $r_{\mu,n}$ is a nonnegative deterministic rate sequence tending to zero for the training-conditional, $\underline p(X)$-weighted Wasserstein loss against the canonical treated boundary law. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: For the concrete nuisance construction, one may take $r_{\mu,n}=O(r_{Q,n}+\delta_n+r_{p,n}+\varepsilon_n+M_Q^{-1})$.
- $r_{p,n}$: $r_{p,n}$ is a nonnegative deterministic sample-size rate for uniform propensity estimation, rather than an integrated prediction loss. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $r_{p,n}\to0$.
- $r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}$: $r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}$ is a nonnegative deterministic rate sequence indexed by sample size for prediction under both target evaluation-index laws, using the projected estimated index in the population comparator. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}\to0$.
- $r_{Q,n}$: $r_{Q,n}$ is a nonnegative deterministic rate sequence indexed by sample size, measuring the interior-grid quantile-fit loss relative to the selected-law target, rather than the canonical boundary law. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $r_{Q,n}\to0$.
- $\Lambda$: $\Lambda$ is population notation whose meaning is determined by its local introduction: an evaluated endpoint or tail expression, or the logistic function in the simulation index. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}: $\Lambda$ ranges separately over $\underline p(X)$, $\min\{\underline p(X),q(Z,X)\}$, and $(q(Z,X)-\overline p(X))_+$, with the error condition required for each choice; these are evaluated population expressions under the law of $(Z,X)$.
- $\hat\Lambda$: $\hat\Lambda$ denotes the corresponding estimated endpoint or tail expression evaluated at $(Z,X)$; in $\mathbb E_{Z,X}[|\hat\Lambda-\Lambda|\mid I_0,I_1]$, the fitted functions are held fixed and the expectation integrates the evaluation law of $(Z,X)$.
- $\delta_n$: $\delta_n>0$ is the fixed, sample-size-indexed localization window used in the fitted selection rule $\hat P\le\hat{\underline p}(X)+\delta_n$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\delta_n\to0$ as $n\to\infty$.
- $\varepsilon_n$: $\varepsilon_n$ is the quantile-analysis trimming sequence satisfying $M_Q^{-1}\le\varepsilon_n<1/2$ and $\varepsilon_n\to0$; it restricts the learner analysis to intermediate quantiles without changing the estimator.
- $M_g$: $M_g$ is a fixed positive integer tuning choice for the regression grid at each sample size, giving spacing $M_g^{-1}$ and $M_g+1$ grid points $u_m$, $m=0,\ldots,M_g$.
- $M_Q$: $M_Q$ is the integer resolution of the quantile grid $\tau_m=m/M_Q$, $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$, and may vary with $n$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $M_Q^{-1}\to0$, so the quantile grid resolution diverges with sample size.
- $u_m$: $u_m=m/M_g$ for $m=0,\ldots,M_g$ is the fixed regression index grid spanning $[0,1]$, including both endpoints.
- $\tau_m$: $\tau_m=m/M_Q$ for $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$ denotes the interior quantile grid; the interpolation endpoint levels $0$ and $1$ are separate from these indexed grid points.
- $\mathcal F_n$: $\mathcal F_n$ is a user-chosen, potentially $n$-dependent class of real-valued functions of $x$, used for both the intercept function $g$ and the slope function $\beta$ in each local-linear fit.
- $\mathcal F_Q$: $\mathcal F_Q$ is the chosen class of real-valued functions of $x$ over which the boundary conditional quantile check-function regression is minimized; with no covariates it is the class of constants.
- $\widetilde Q$: $\widetilde Q$ is the raw interior-grid check-regression fit on the treated localized sample from $I_1$, whose selection rule is determined by the first stage on $I_0$, before clipping, rearrangement, and interpolation.
- $\rho_\tau$: $\rho_\tau(u)=u(\tau-\indicator(u<0))$ is the check loss for $u\in\mathbb R$ at the quantile levels $\tau=\tau_m=m/M_Q$, $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$, used in the boundary quantile regression.
- $\widehat C$: $\widehat C(\tau\mid x)=\int_0^\tau\widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}(s)\,\mathrm ds$ for $\tau\in[0,1]$ and $x\in\mathcal X$, with grid values computed exactly by the trapezoid rule and off-grid evaluation implemented by linear interpolation of adjacent grid values.
- $\hat\tau_i$: For $i\in I_2$ with $\hat{\underline p}(X_i)>0$, $\hat\tau_i=\min\{1,\hat q(Z_i,X_i)/\hat{\underline p}(X_i)\}\in[0,1]$ is the normalized boundary-integration cutoff; when $\hat{\underline p}(X_i)=0$ it is not evaluated, and when $\hat q(Z_i,X_i)\geqslant\hat{\underline p}(X_i)>0$ it equals one.
- $\hat\psi_1$: $\hat\psi_1(O)$ denotes the estimated lower-bound boundary contribution $-\int_{\min\{\hat{\underline p}(X),\hat q(Z,X)\}}^{\hat{\underline p}(X)}\widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid X}(v/\hat{\underline p}(X))\,\mathrm dv$, with value zero when $\hat{\underline p}(X)=0$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: For $\hat{\underline p}(X_i)>0$, the concrete implementation evaluates $-\hat{\underline p}(X_i)[\widehat C(1\mid X_i)-\widehat C(\hat\tau_i\mid X_i)]$, where $\hat\tau_i=\min\{1,\hat q(Z_i,X_i)/\hat{\underline p}(X_i)\}$; cumulative-integral grid values are computed by the trapezoid rule, and off-grid lookup uses linear interpolation between adjacent cumulative-integral grid values. For $\hat{\underline p}(X_i)=0$, it returns zero without evaluating $\hat\tau_i$.
- $\hat\psi_3$: $\hat\psi_3(O)=y_{\min}(\hat q(Z,X)-\hat{\overline p}(X))_+$ is the fitted lower-bound tail score, evaluated at $O_i$ for $i\in I_2$ with the fitted score map held fixed conditional on $I_0,I_1$.
- $\rho_j$: For $j\in\{0,1,2\}$, $\rho_j\in(0,1)$ is the limiting split proportion $|I_j|/n\to\rho_j$, with $\rho_0+\rho_1+\rho_2=1$.
- $L_\phi$: $L_\phi<\infty$ is a uniform Lipschitz bound satisfying $|\phi(z,x,p')-\phi(z,x,p)|\leqslant L_\phi|p'-p|$ for every $(z,x,p,p')\in\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\times[0,1]^2$.
- $U_\ell$: For $\ell\in\{1,2\}$, $U_1=q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X)$ and $U_2=P=p(Z,X)$ are the population evaluation indices, with $U_\ell\in\mathcal S_X$; their estimated counterparts are $\hat U_1=\hat q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X)$ and $\hat U_2=\hat P$.
- $\hat U_\ell$: For $\ell\in\{1,2\}$, $\hat U_1=\hat q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X)$ and $\hat U_2=\hat P$ are the two target-evaluation indices, with evaluation over $(Z,X)$ conditional on $I_0,I_1$, and the population comparison for $\hat g_1(\hat U_\ell,X)$ is $g_1(\Pi_X(\hat U_\ell),X)$.
- $\mathcal S_x$: $\mathcal S_x=[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$ is the population propensity interval at covariate value $x$, used as the domain of the population comparison after projection by $\Pi_x$.
- $\Pi_x$: $\Pi_x:\mathbb R\to\mathcal S_x$ is projection onto the population support interval $\mathcal S_x=[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$, given by $\Pi_x(u)=\min\{\overline p(x),\max\{\underline p(x),u\}\}$.
- $\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}$: $\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}$ is the conditional population outcome law under the fitted selection event $\{W=1,\hat p(Z,X)\le\hat{\underline p}(x)+\delta_n\}$ given $X=x$ and $I_0$, with the canonical boundary law used when that event has zero conditional probability.
- $Q^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}$: $Q^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}:(0,1)\to\mathcal Y$ is the quantile function of the $I_0$-conditional selected law $\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}$, indexed by sample size $n$ and covariate value $x$, including its stipulated zero-selection version.
- $\widetilde Q_m^c$: $\widetilde Q_m^c(x)$ is the clipped pre-rearrangement fit at $\tau_m=m/M_Q$, for $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$, obtained from the boundary quantile regression on $\mathcal I_{\delta,1}\subseteq I_1$ using the selection rule determined on $I_0$.
- $\mathcal J_{Q,n}$: $\mathcal J_{Q,n}=\{m\in\{1,\ldots,M_Q-1\}:\varepsilon_n\le\tau_m\le1-\varepsilon_n\}$ is the interior grid subset used for analytical quantile trimming; it does not trim the estimator.
- $\pi$: The meaning of $\pi$ is determined by its arguments: transport notation denotes a joint probability measure, whereas $\pi(z,x)$ denotes the conditional instrument assignment probability $\mathbb P(Z=z\mid X=x)$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}: $\pi$ ranges over $\Pi(\mu,\nu)\subseteq\mathcal P(\mathbb R^2)$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In instrument-specific probability expressions, $\pi(z,x)=\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z=z\mid X=x)$, rather than a transport coupling.
- $\mathcal B$: The notation $\mathcal B$ denotes an OT state space when standing alone and the Borel $\sigma$-field operator when applied to an outcome domain. In Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}: The second marginal state space is $\mathcal B=\mathbb R$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: In the kernel definition, $A\in\mathcal B(\mathcal Y)$ means that $A$ is a Borel subset of $\mathcal Y$.
- $\indep$: $\indep$ denotes independence, with $A\indep B\mid C$ denoting conditional independence given $C$, namely factorization of the conditional joint law into the two conditional marginal laws almost surely.
- $\Gamma_{1,x}$: $\Gamma_{1,x}$ is the conditional treated feasible class defined for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$, with its members understood through their Lebesgue-almost-everywhere kernel values in $u$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: For $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$, the observational constraint equivalently holds as $\int_0^p\pi_{1,x}(\mathrm dy\mid u)\,\mathrm du=H_{1,x}(p,\mathrm dy)$ for every $p\in p(\mathcal Z,x)$, using the canonical continuous version of $H_{1,x}$.
- $B_Y$: $B_Y$ is a fixed, finite, nonnegative constant determined by $y_{\min}$ and $y_{\max}$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: $B_Y=\max\{|y_{\min}|,|y_{\max}|\}$ bounds the absolute magnitude of feasible outcomes. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $B_Y=y_{\max}-y_{\min}$ is the outcome range, used to bound differences between values in $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$.
- $\bar p_2$: $\bar p_2(z)$ is the sample-dependent average of the clipped first-stage fit $\hat p(z,\cdot)$ trained on $I_1$ over the covariates indexed by $I_2$; conditional on $I_1$ it remains an empirical average over evaluation covariates, rather than the population marginal mean.
- $\widehat S_k$: $\widehat S_k$ is the sample-dependent baseline level-set estimate obtained from the pre-recovery fits on $I_1$ and their averages over the covariates in $I_2$, with a threshold-defined structural-zero set and positively indexed baseline components ordered by their averages.
- $\widehat T_{j,k}$: $\widehat T_{j,k}$ is the sample-dependent policy level-set estimate obtained from the pre-recovery fits on $I_1$ and their averages over the covariates in $I_2$; it depends on both of these inputs and is indexed by the recovered component ordering and baseline bins.
- $\alpha_{p_k}$: $\alpha_{p_k}$ is the population lower-boundary representer for the particular component $J_{j,k}$, with the dependence on $j$ suppressed in its notation, for $k=1,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=0,\ldots,l_k-1$; there is no $p_0$ perturbation or correction.
- $\alpha_{p_{k+1}}$: $\alpha_{p_{k+1}}$ denotes the upper interval-boundary representer for the continuous-outcome component $J_{j,k}$, with suppressed dependence on $j$, indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=0,\ldots,l_k-1$; it remains an upper-side representer at $k=0$.
- $IF_{J^+}$: $IF_{J^+}$ denotes the plus-side observation-level residual component indexed implicitly by $k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}$ and $j\in\{0,\ldots,l_k-1\}$, evaluated with the nuisance functions supplied to the continuous-outcome score.
- $IF_{J^-}$: $IF_{J^-}$ denotes the minus-side observation-level residual component indexed implicitly by $k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}$ and $j\in\{0,\ldots,l_k-1\}$, evaluated with the nuisance functions supplied to the continuous-outcome score, with its structural $k=0$ component identically zero.
- $IF_{M_{j,k}^+}$: $IF_{M_{j,k}^+}$ is the observation-level residual indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and boundary index $j=0,\ldots,l_k$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome part, the no-atom condition at $y_{\min}$ makes the true residual $IF_{M_{0,k}^+}$ vanish almost surely.
- $IF_{M_{j,k}^-}$: $IF_{M_{j,k}^-}$ is the observation-level residual indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and boundary index $j=0,\ldots,l_k$, with the structural zero convention at $k=0$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome part, the no-atom condition at $y_{\min}$ makes the true residual $IF_{M_{0,k}^-}$ vanish almost surely.
- $\alpha_{P,k+1}^{(j,k)}$: $\alpha_{P,k+1}^{(j,k)}(x)$ is the coefficient of a perturbation of $P_{1,k+1}(x)$ in the binary component $h_{j,k}^{-}(x)$, indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, holding the propensity and alternative-policy levels and $P_{1,k}$ fixed.
- $\alpha_{P,k}^{(j,k)}$: For $k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}$ and $j\in\{1,\ldots,l_k\}$, $\alpha_{P,k}^{(j,k)}(x)=-D_{j,k}^{-}(x)$ is the coefficient of the $P_{1,k}$ perturbation in the derivative of $h_{j,k}^{-}$, with the other nuisance arguments held fixed.
- $\alpha_{p,k+1}^{(j,k)}$: $\alpha_{p,k+1}^{(j,k)}(x)$ is the direct upper-propensity coefficient for the binary score component $h_{j,k}^{-}$, indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, holding $P_{1,k}$, $P_{1,k+1}$, and the alternative-policy levels fixed.
- $\alpha_{p,k}^{(j,k)}$: $\alpha_{p,k}^{(j,k)}(x)$ denotes the direct lower-propensity coefficient in the binary score for $h_{j,k}^{-}$, indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, holding the success regressions and alternative-policy levels fixed; at $k=0$ its residual correction is omitted because $p_0\equiv0$ is known.
- $m$: The notation $m(X;\eta)$ denotes the binary lower-bound integrand, whereas bare $m$ in $u_m$ or $\tau_m$ denotes a grid index. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the binary-outcome case, $m(X;\eta)$ is the displayed integrand with nuisance argument $\eta$; evaluating at $\eta_0$ gives the population target integrand. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $m$ is an integer grid index: $m=0,\ldots,M_g$ for $u_m=m/M_g$ and $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$ for the fitted quantiles at $\tau_m=m/M_Q$.
