- $\nu_{k\mid x}$: $\nu_{k\mid x}$ is the normalized Lebesgue probability law on $G_k(x)$, equivalently the law of $U$ conditional on $X=x$ and $U\in G_k(x)$, for almost every $x$ and $k=0,\ldots,K_x-1$ with $|G_k(x)|>0$.
- $T_x$: $T_x:[0,1]\to\mathbb R$, $T_x(u)=\omega(x,u)$, is the target-weight map with the covariate argument fixed at $x$.
- $\rho_{k\mid x}$: $\rho_{k\mid x}=(T_x)_{\#}\mathrm{Unif}(G_k(x))$ is the population probability law of $\omega(x,U)$ for an auxiliary $U\sim\mathrm{Unif}(G_k(x))$, defined for fixed $x$ and $k=0,\ldots,K_x-1$ with $|G_k(x)|>0$.
- $F_{\omega,k\mid x}$: $F_{\omega,k\mid x}$ is the population distribution function of the pushforward of $\nu_{k\mid x}$ under $u\mapsto\omega(x,u)$, using a jointly measurable version in $(x,v)$ for almost every $x$ and each nonempty gap indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K_x-1$.
- $R_{k,x}$: $R_{k,x}$ is a measurable map of $(u,s)\in G_k(x)\times[0,1]$ for almost every $x$ and each $k\in\{0,\ldots,K_x-1\}$ with $|G_k(x)|>0$, using the left limit of $F_{\omega,k\mid x}$ to distribute each weight atom across its rank interval.
- $S$: $S$ is an auxiliary $\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$ draw independent of $U\sim\nu_{k\mid x}$ for each fixed $x$ and nonempty gap $G_k(x)$, used to randomize ties in $R_{k,x}(U,S)$ and integrated out in the attaining outcome kernel.
- $\pi_{1,x}^\star$: $\pi_{1,x}^\star$ is a jointly measurable population probability kernel for $Y(1)$ given $(X,U)=(x,u)$, defined for almost every $x$ and pasted across the nonempty gaps, non-degenerate LIV intervals and trivial tail, with arbitrary probability-law values at region boundaries.
- $\mathcal E_n$: $\mathcal E_n$ is the event of simultaneous exact recovery of all baseline and policy level sets with their population ordering and bin indices, including $\widehat S_0=S_0$, by the procedure using first-stage fits on $I_1$ and their averages over $\{X_i:i\in I_2\}$.
- $r_{\mathrm{reg},n}$: $r_{\mathrm{reg},n}$ is the maximum population $L_2$ error of the fitted outcome regressions, propensity regressions, pooled policy levels, and boundary quantiles, with integration over the marginal law of $X$ while holding the fitted functions fixed. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome proof, the errors are those of the oracle nuisance fits trained on $I_1$ using the population level sets; hence their magnitudes are fixed conditional on $I_1$ and satisfy $r_{\mathrm{reg},n}=o_P(n^{-1/4})$.
- $r_{\mathrm{wt},n}$: $r_{\mathrm{wt},n}$ is the maximum of the componentwise $L_2(\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(\mathrm{d}x))$ errors of the fitted weights $\hat\pi_k,\hat\tau_{j,k},\hat a_{j,k}(z,\cdot),\hat\gamma_{j,k},\hat\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k},\hat\gamma_K$ relative to their population counterparts, over the finitely many fitted indices and instrument categories.
- $r_{\mathrm{core},n}$: $r_{\mathrm{core},n}$ is the maximum of the $L_2(\mathbb P_X)$ norms of fitted-minus-population errors for the finitely many $P_{1,k}$, $p_k$, instrument-specific $p(z,\cdot)$, and pooled $q_{j,k}$ nuisances used by the binary oracle score, with the fitted $P_{1,k}$ taken after clipping and rearrangement and the structural zero boundaries retained.

**Objections raised during the review and withdrawn.** The paper's owner showed that each misreads the paper; the printed statement stands under the reading given.

- **Theorem 3.4.** A finite instrument domain $\mathcal Z$ carries the discrete topology, so the common-support condition $\mathrm{Supp}(Z\mid X=x)=\mathcal Z$ says that every instrument value has positive conditional probability given $X=x$.
- **Proposition 4.3.** A finite instrument domain $\mathcal Z$ carries the discrete topology, so the common-support condition $\mathrm{Supp}(Z\mid X=x)=\mathcal Z$ says that every instrument value has positive conditional probability given $X=x$.

### 1. 1D Optimal Transport with Product Cost; see, e.g., \citep{villani2021topics, galichon2016optimal} `thm:1d-ot`

| field | value |
| --- | --- |
| bucket | `verified_with_bookkeeping` |
| source | `sections/preliminaries.tex:88-95` (`theorem`, label `thm:1d_ot`) |
| statement | \label{thm:1d_ot} Let $\mu$ and $\nu$ be probability measures on $\mathbb{R}$ with quantile functions $Q_\mu$ and $Q_\nu$. Suppose either that $\mu$ has bounded support and $\nu$ has a finite first moment, or vice versa, and let the cost be the simple product $c(a,b) = a \cdot b$. Then, the minimum and maximum of the expected cost over all valid couplings are completely characterized by the countermonotonic and comonotonic couplings, respectively: \begin{align*} \min_{\pi \in \Pi(\mu, \nu)} \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} a \cdot b \, \mathrm{d}\pi(a, b) &= \int_0^1 Q_\mu(t) Q_\nu(1-t) \, \mathrm{d}t, \\ \max_{\pi \in \Pi(\mu, \nu)} \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} a \cdot b \, \mathrm{d}\pi(a, b) &= \int_0^1 Q_\mu(t) Q_\nu(t) \, \mathrm{d}t. \end{align*} The minimum is attained by the countermonotonic coupling $(Q_\mu(\xi), Q_\nu(1-\xi))$, and the maximum by the comonotonic coupling $(Q_\mu(\xi), Q_\nu(\xi))$, where $\xi \sim \mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$. |

(The supplied Villani source, Section 2.2.1, printed page 73, defines the generalized inverse using a strict inequality. The theorem's integrals and uniform coupling draws do not use endpoint values. The paper fixes no convention; the non-strict lower generalized inverse is used throughout (the strict inverse of the cited source differs from it at countably many levels only, which no statement uses).) ／ [note, V1.5] cited source checked: source supplied by the user at discovery/cited_sources/villani2021topics.txt |

### 2. Sharp Identified Set of the Joint Latent Law `prop:sharpness`

| field | value |
| --- | --- |
| bucket | `verified_with_bookkeeping` |
| source | `sections/pid.tex:53-61` (`proposition`, label `prop:sharpness`) |
| statement | \label{prop:sharpness} Suppose the observed distribution $\mathbb{P}_{obs}$ is generated by a true structural model satisfying \cref{asp:structure}. Let $\tilde{\pi}$ be any probability measure on $\mathcal{Y} \times \mathcal{Y} \times [0,1] \times \mathcal{X}$ whose $(Y(w), U, X)$-marginals $\tilde{\pi}_w$ satisfy $(\tilde{\pi}_0, \tilde{\pi}_1) \in \Gamma(\mathbb{P}_{obs})$. Then there exists a joint distribution of the latent and observable variables $(Y(0), Y(1), U, X, Z, W)$ such that: \begin{enumerate} \item[(i)] the joint marginal distribution of $(Y(0), Y(1), U, X)$ is exactly $\tilde{\pi}$; \item[(ii)] \cref{asp:structure} holds for $(Y(0), Y(1), U, X, Z, W)$; \item[(iii)] the induced distribution of $(Z, X, W, Y)$ exactly matches $\mathbb{P}_{obs}$. \end{enumerate} Conversely, every joint marginal $\tilde{\pi}$ arising from a distribution of $(Y(0), Y(1), U, X, Z, W)$ satisfying \textup{(ii)}--\textup{(iii)} has $(Y(w), U, X)$-marginals in $\Gamma(\mathbb{P}_{obs})$ for $w \in \{0, 1\}$. |

### 3. Closed-Form Sharp Bounds `thm:discrete-lower-bound`

| field | value |
| --- | --- |
| bucket | `verified_with_bookkeeping` |
| source | `sections/pid.tex:157-170` (`theorem`, label `thm:discrete_lower_bound`) |
| statement | \label{thm:discrete_lower_bound} Suppose \cref{asp:structure} and \cref{asp:target_weight} hold, $X$ is almost surely constant, and $\mathcal{Z}$ is finite. For $i=0, \dots, K-1$, let $Q_{Y, i}(t)$ be the quantile function of the identified conditional distribution $\mu_{1, i}$ defined in (\ref{eq:y_identified_measure}). Let $Q_{\omega, i}(t)$ be the quantile function of the random variable $\omega(U)$ where $U \sim \text{Unif}(p_i, p_{i+1})$. The sharp lower bound for the $W=1$ component of the PRTE is given by: \begin{equation}\label{eq:discrete_lower_bound} \begin{split} \underline{\theta}_{\omega, 1} = \ & \sum_{i=0}^{K-1} (p_{i+1} - p_i) \int_0^1 Q_{Y, i}(t) Q_{\omega, i}(1-t) \, \mathrm{d}t \\ & + \int_{p_K}^1 \Big( y_{\min} \max\{0, \omega(u)\} + y_{\max} \min\{0, \omega(u)\} \Big) \mathrm{d}u. \end{split} \end{equation} Similarly, the sharp upper bound is given by replacing $Q_{\omega,i}(1-t)$ with $Q_{\omega,i}(t)$ in the OT term and swapping $y_{\min}$ and $y_{\max}$ in the trivial tail term. |

### 4. General Closed-Form Sharp Bounds `thm:continuous-lower-bound`

| field | value |
| --- | --- |
| bucket | `verified_with_bookkeeping` |
| source | `sections/pid.tex:400-417` (`theorem`, label `thm:continuous_lower_bound`) |
| statement | \label{thm:continuous_lower_bound} Suppose \cref{asp:structure}, \cref{asp:target_weight}, and \cref{asp:continuity_p} hold, and $X$ is almost surely constant. Let $p(\mathcal{Z}) = \bigcup_{k=1}^{K}[\underline{p}_k, \overline{p}_k]$ be the maximal-interval decomposition in (\ref{eq:range_decomposition}). For each nonempty gap $G_k$, let $Q_{Y,k}(t)$ be the quantile function of $\mu_{1,k}$ defined in (\ref{eq:gap_identified_measure}), and $Q_{\omega,k}(t)$ the quantile function of $\omega(U)$ for $U \sim \text{Unif}(G_k)$. In the displayed sums, a term indexed by an empty gap is defined to be zero. The sharp lower bound for the $W=1$ component of the target parameter is given by \begin{equation}\label{eq:continuous_lower_bound} \begin{split} \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1}\ =\ & \underbrace{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(\underline{p}_{k+1}-\overline{p}_k)\int_0^1 Q_{Y,k}(t)\,Q_{\omega,k}(1-t)\,\mathrm{d}t}_{\text{OT Bound on the Gaps}} + \underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{\underline{p}_k}^{\overline{p}_k}\frac{\diff}{\diff u}g_1^{\mathrm c}(u)\,\omega(u)\,\mathrm{d}u}_{\text{Point-Identified LIV on the Intervals}} \\ & \quad\quad + \underbrace{\int_{\overline{p}_K}^{1}\Big(y_{\min}\max\{0,\omega(u)\} + y_{\max}\min\{0,\omega(u)\}\Big)\mathrm{d}u}_{\text{Trivial Lower Bound on the Tail}}. \end{split} \end{equation} Similarly, the sharp upper bound is given by replacing $Q_{\omega,k}(1-t)$ with $Q_{\omega,k}(t)$ in the OT term and swapping $y_{\min}$ and $y_{\max}$ in the trivial tail term. |

### 7. Specialization of the Sub-Interval Contribution `prop:j-specialization`

| field | value |
| --- | --- |
| bucket | `verified_with_bookkeeping` |
| source | `sections/est_inf.tex:75-91` (`proposition`, label `prop:J_specialization`) |
| statement | \label{prop:J_specialization} Fix $k \in \{0,\dots,K-1\}$. Set the lower boundary $\nu_{0,k}(x)\equiv y_{\min}$, and let $\nu_{j,k}(x) \coloneqq Q_{Y,k\mid x}(\kappa_{j,k}(x))$ for $j \in \{1,\dots,l_k\}$. \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)] \item \textbf{Continuous outcome.} If $\mu_{1,k\mid X}$ is continuous at $\nu_{j,k}(X)$ and $\nu_{j+1,k}(X)$ almost surely, then, for $j \in \{0,\dots,l_k-1\}$, \begin{equation}\label{eq:J_cexpectation} J_{j,k}(X) = (p_{k+1}(X) - p_k(X))\,\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{1,k\mid X}}\!\big[ Y\,\indicator\!\big(\nu_{j,k}(X) < Y \leqslant \nu_{j+1,k}(X)\big) \big]. \end{equation} \item \textbf{Binary outcome.} If $Y \in \{0,1\}$, then $J_{\text{full},k}(X) = P_{1,k+1}(X) - P_{1,k}(X)$ with $P_{1,k}(x) \coloneqq \mathbb{E}_{\text{obs}}[YW \mid Z \in S_k, X=x]$ for $k\geqslant1$ and the boundary convention $P_{1,0}(x)\equiv0$, and for $j \in \{1,\dots,l_k\}$, \begin{equation}\label{eq:h_jk_binary} J_{j-1,k}(X) = h_{j,k}^{-}(X) \coloneqq \max\!\Big(0,\; q_{j,k}(X) - \max\!\big(q_{j-1,k}(X),\; p_{k+1}(X) - J_{\text{full},k}(X)\big)\Big). \end{equation} Consequently, (\ref{eq:target_discrete_iv}) collapses to \begin{align}\label{eq:binary_lower_bound} \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1} = \mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\bigg(\gamma_{\text{full},k}(X)\, J_{\text{full},k}(X) + \sum_{j=1}^{l_k} \gamma_{j,k}(X)\, h_{j,k}^{-}(X)\bigg) + \Delta_K(X)\Bigg]. \end{align} \end{enumerate} |

### 8. Asymptotic Normality `thm:dml-normality`

| field | value |
| --- | --- |
| bucket | `verified_with_bookkeeping` |
| source | `sections/est_inf.tex:141-152` (`theorem`, label `thm:dml_normality`) |
| statement | \label{thm:dml_normality} Suppose \cref{asp:structure}, \cref{asp:phi_smooth}, \cref{asp:prop_gap}, and \cref{asp:overlap} hold and $\mathcal{Z}$ is finite. Suppose the pre-recovery first stage fitted on $I_1$ satisfies $\max_{z\in\mathcal Z}\\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\\|_{L_2}=o_P(n^{-1/4})$. Given the recovered level sets, suppose the remaining score nuisances fitted on $I_1$ are clipped to their known bounded ranges, every estimated conditional probability is clipped to $[0,1]$ before any difference is formed, $\hat\pi_k$ and $\hat\tau_{j,k}$ are clipped further to $[c_\pi/2,1]$, and the estimators converge componentwise at rate $o_P(n^{-1/4})$ in $L_2$. Suppose in addition that one of the following holds. \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}] \item \textbf{Continuous outcome.} The nuisances include the boundary quantiles $\nu_{j,k}$, and, uniformly in $X$, each relevant conditional law $Y\mid X,Z\in S_{k'},W=1$ has a density bounded in a fixed neighborhood of every true boundary $\nu_{j,k}(X)$, $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, for $k'=k+1$ and also for $k'=k$ when $k\geqslant1$; at the fixed boundary $\nu_{0,k}=y_{\min}$, these conditional laws have no atom at $y_{\min}$. \item \textbf{Discrete outcome.} The outcome is binary, \cref{asp:discrete_outcome_gap} holds, and the nuisances are $p_k$, $P_{1,k}$, $\pi_k$, $\tau_{j,k}$, $a_{j,k}$, $\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}$, $\gamma_{j,k}$, and $\gamma_K$. \end{enumerate} Then the sample-split estimator (\ref{eq:estimator_dml}) is $\sqrt{n}$-consistent and asymptotically normal: \begin{equation*} \sqrt{n/2} \big( \hat{\underline{\theta}}_{\omega, 1} - \underline{\theta}_{\omega, 1} \big) \xrightarrow{d} \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma^2), \end{equation*} where the asymptotic variance is the variance of the true orthogonal score, $\sigma^2 = \mathbb{E}\big[ ({\psi}(O; \eta) - \underline{\theta}_{\omega, 1})^2 \big]$. It can be consistently estimated by its sample analog $\hat{\sigma}^2 = \frac{1}{\|I_2\|} \sum_{i \in I_2} \big(\hat{\psi}(O_i; \hat{\eta}) - \hat{\underline{\theta}}_{\omega, 1}\big)^2$. |

### 9. Continuous-Instrument Reduction via Fubini `prop:continuous-reduction`

| field | value |

| statement | \label{prop:continuous_reduction} Suppose \cref{asp:structure} and \cref{asp:continuity_p} hold, and $\mathcal{Z}$ is connected. Then the sharp lower bound in \cref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} admits the representation \begin{equation} \label{eq:continuous_simplified_target} \begin{split} \underline{\theta}_{\omega, 1} &= \mathbb{E}_{Z,X} \Bigg[ - \int_{\min\{\underline{p}(X), q(Z,X)\}}^{\underline{p}(X)} Q_{1,\underline p\mid X}\!\left({v}/{\underline{p}(X)}\right) \, \mathrm{d}v \\ &\qquad + g_1\big(q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X), X\big) - g_1\big(P, X\big) \\ &\qquad + y_{\min} \max\{0, q(Z,X) - \overline{p}(X)\} \Bigg], \end{split} \end{equation} where the first integral is defined as zero when $\underline{p}(X)=0$. The sharp upper bound is obtained by replacing $Q_{1,\underline p\mid X}(v/\underline{p}(X))$ with $Q_{1,\underline p\mid X}(1-v/\underline{p}(X))$ and $y_{\min}$ with $y_{\max}$. |

| source | `sections/appendix.tex:805-818` (`lemma`, label `lem:orthogonality_discrete`) |
| statement | \label{lem:orthogonality_discrete} Let $\eta_0$ denote the vector of true nuisance parameter values, let $\theta_0 = \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1}$, and let $\mathcal{T}$ be the set of nuisance directions $\delta$ such that $\eta_0 + t\,\delta$ is an admissible nuisance value for all $t$ in a neighborhood of $0$. \begin{enumerate} \item[\textup{(i)}] Under the hypotheses of \cref{thm:dml_normality}\textup{(i)}, the continuous-outcome score $\psi$ assembled in \cref{app:scores_continuous} satisfies \[ \mathbb{E}[\psi(O;\eta_0)] = \theta_0 \qquad\text{and}\qquad \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi(O;\eta_0 + t\,\delta)\bigr]\Big\|_{t=0} = 0 \quad\text{for every } \delta \in \mathcal{T}, \] where $\theta_0$ is the target~(\ref{eq:target_discrete_iv}). \item[\textup{(ii)}] Under the hypotheses of \cref{thm:dml_normality}\textup{(ii)}, the same two properties hold for the binary-outcome score~(\ref{eq:binary_score}), with $\theta_0$ the target~(\ref{eq:binary_lower_bound}). \end{enumerate} |

| statement | \label{cor:continuous_primitive_rate} Suppose \cref{asp:structure} and \cref{asp:continuity_p} hold. Suppose additionally that $\phi$ is uniformly Lipschitz in its last argument: for some $L_\phi<\infty$, \[ \|\phi(z,x,p')-\phi(z,x,p)\|\leqslant L_\phi\|p'-p\| \] for all $(z,x,p,p')\in\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\times[0,1]^2$. Suppose that \cref{asp:continuous_learners} holds with rates $r_{p,n}$, $r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}$, $r_{Q,n}$ and trimming sequence $\varepsilon_n$, that $\|I_j\|/n\to\rho_j\in(0,1)$ for $j=0,1,2$, and that \[ r_{p,n},\ r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n},\ r_{Q,n},\ \delta_n,\ \varepsilon_n,\ M_Q^{-1}\longrightarrow0. \] Then the nuisance estimators produced by \cref{alg:continuous_instrument} satisfy \cref{asp:continuous_rates} with \[ \begin{aligned} r_{e,n}&=O(r_{p,n}),\\ r_{g,n}&=O\!\left(r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}+r_{p,n}\right),\\ r_{\mu,n}&=O\!\left( r_{Q,n}+\delta_n+r_{p,n}+\varepsilon_n+M_Q^{-1}\right). \end{aligned} \] and consequently \[ \|\hat{\underline\theta}_{\omega,1}-\underline\theta_{\omega,1}\| =O_P\!\left(n^{-1/2}+r_{p,n}+r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n} +r_{Q,n}+\delta_n+\varepsilon_n+M_Q^{-1}\right). \] |

### `thm:1d-ot` (`sections/preliminaries.tex:88-95`)

- as printed: \label{thm:1d_ot} Let $\mu$ and $\nu$ be probability measures on $\mathbb{R}$ with quantile functions $Q_\mu$ and $Q_\nu$. Suppose either that $\mu$ has bounded support and $\nu$ has a finite first moment, or vice versa, and let the cost be the simple product $c(a,b) = a \cdot b$. Then, the minimum and maximum of the expected cost over all valid couplings are completely characterized by the countermonotonic and comonotonic couplings, respectively: \begin{align*} \min_{\pi \in \Pi(\mu, \nu)} \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} a \cdot b \, \mathrm{d}\pi(a, b) &= \int_0^1 Q_\mu(t) Q_\nu(1-t) \, \mathrm{d}t, \\ \max_{\pi \in \Pi(\mu, \nu)} \int_{\mathbb{R}^2} a \cdot b \, \mathrm{d}\pi(a, b) &= \int_0^1 Q_\mu(t) Q_\nu(t) \, \mathrm{d}t. \end{align*} The minimum is attained by the countermonotonic coupling $(Q_\mu(\xi), Q_\nu(1-\xi))$, and the maximum by the comonotonic coupling $(Q_\mu(\xi), Q_\nu(\xi))$, where $\xi \sim \mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$.
- corrected: statement unchanged
- no error: only a side condition or reading the referee made explicit (see below)
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-20`: For $\rho\in\{\mu,\nu\}$ and $t\in(0,1)$, $Q_\rho(t)=\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\rho((\! -\infty,a])\geqslant t\}$. — pins reading of ass:1d-ot-probability-laws: The standard generalized-inverse convention for probability quantiles applies to the notation section and Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}; the theorem evaluates these functions at uniform draws and inside Lebesgue integrals.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-63`: For $0<t<1$, $(Q_\mu(t),Q_\nu(t))=(\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,a])\geqslant t\},\inf\{b\in\mathbb R:\nu(( -\infty,b])\geqslant t\})$. — pins reading of thm:1d-ot: The supplied Villani source, Section 2.2.1, printed page 73, defines the generalized inverse using a strict inequality. The theorem's integrals and uniform coupling draws do not use endpoint values. The paper fixes no convention; the non-strict lower generalized inverse is used throughout (the strict inverse of the cited source differs from it at countably many levels only, which no statement uses).

### `prop:sharpness` (`sections/pid.tex:53-61`)

- as printed: \label{prop:sharpness} Suppose the observed distribution $\mathbb{P}_{obs}$ is generated by a true structural model satisfying \cref{asp:structure}. Let $\tilde{\pi}$ be any probability measure on $\mathcal{Y} \times \mathcal{Y} \times [0,1] \times \mathcal{X}$ whose $(Y(w), U, X)$-marginals $\tilde{\pi}_w$ satisfy $(\tilde{\pi}_0, \tilde{\pi}_1) \in \Gamma(\mathbb{P}_{obs})$. Then there exists a joint distribution of the latent and observable variables $(Y(0), Y(1), U, X, Z, W)$ such that: \begin{enumerate} \item[(i)] the joint marginal distribution of $(Y(0), Y(1), U, X)$ is exactly $\tilde{\pi}$; \item[(ii)] \cref{asp:structure} holds for $(Y(0), Y(1), U, X, Z, W)$; \item[(iii)] the induced distribution of $(Z, X, W, Y)$ exactly matches $\mathbb{P}_{obs}$. \end{enumerate} Conversely, every joint marginal $\tilde{\pi}$ arising from a distribution of $(Y(0), Y(1), U, X, Z, W)$ satisfying \textup{(ii)}--\textup{(iii)} has $(Y(w), U, X)$-marginals in $\Gamma(\mathbb{P}_{obs})$ for $w \in \{0, 1\}$.
- corrected: statement unchanged
- no error: only a side condition or reading the referee made explicit (see below)
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-12`: $Y=WY(1)+(1-W)Y(0)$ almost surely under the structural population law. — pins reading of ass:structure-consistency: Assumption \ref{asp:structure} states consistency as a structural equality of random variables; the subsequent observational derivation states conditional identities almost everywhere, consistently with the standard almost-sure convention.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-13`: $W=\indicator(U\leqslant p(Z,X))$ almost surely under the structural population law. — pins reading of ass:structure-threshold-crossing: The threshold-crossing clause of Assumption \ref{asp:structure} is a structural equality of random variables. The derivation preceding equation \ref{eq:canonical_submeasures} uses it to establish observational identities almost everywhere.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-14`: $\mathcal L(U\mid X=x)=\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$ for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$. — pins reading of ass:structure-uniform-resistance: Assumption \ref{asp:structure} uses conditional-distribution notation without a pointwise covariate quantifier, while its common-support clause explicitly says every covariate value. The surrounding prose also recognizes that conditional probabilities are identified only almost surely.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-29`: Every constraint in $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ is imposed simultaneously for both $w\in\{0,1\}$. — pins reading of def:ot-formulation: The sentence immediately preceding equation $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ explicitly says that every constraint is imposed for both treatment states.

### `thm:discrete-lower-bound` (`sections/pid.tex:157-170`)

- as printed: \label{thm:discrete_lower_bound} Suppose \cref{asp:structure} and \cref{asp:target_weight} hold, $X$ is almost surely constant, and $\mathcal{Z}$ is finite. For $i=0, \dots, K-1$, let $Q_{Y, i}(t)$ be the quantile function of the identified conditional distribution $\mu_{1, i}$ defined in (\ref{eq:y_identified_measure}). Let $Q_{\omega, i}(t)$ be the quantile function of the random variable $\omega(U)$ where $U \sim \text{Unif}(p_i, p_{i+1})$. The sharp lower bound for the $W=1$ component of the PRTE is given by: \begin{equation}\label{eq:discrete_lower_bound} \begin{split} \underline{\theta}_{\omega, 1} = \ & \sum_{i=0}^{K-1} (p_{i+1} - p_i) \int_0^1 Q_{Y, i}(t) Q_{\omega, i}(1-t) \, \mathrm{d}t \\ & + \int_{p_K}^1 \Big( y_{\min} \max\{0, \omega(u)\} + y_{\max} \min\{0, \omega(u)\} \Big) \mathrm{d}u. \end{split} \end{equation} Similarly, the sharp upper bound is given by replacing $Q_{\omega,i}(1-t)$ with $Q_{\omega,i}(t)$ in the OT term and swapping $y_{\min}$ and $y_{\max}$ in the trivial tail term.

### `thm:continuous-lower-bound` (`sections/pid.tex:400-417`)

- as printed: \label{thm:continuous_lower_bound} Suppose \cref{asp:structure}, \cref{asp:target_weight}, and \cref{asp:continuity_p} hold, and $X$ is almost surely constant. In the displayed sums, a term indexed by an empty gap is defined to be zero. The sharp lower bound for the $W=1$ component of the target parameter is given by \begin{equation}\label{eq:continuous_lower_bound} \begin{split} \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1}\ =\ & \underbrace{\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}(\underline{p}_{k+1}-\overline{p}_k)\int_0^1 Q_{Y,k}(t)\,Q_{\omega,k}(1-t)\,\mathrm{d}t}_{\text{OT Bound on the Gaps}} + \underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\int_{\underline{p}_k}^{\overline{p}_k}\frac{\diff}{\diff u}g_1^{\mathrm c}(u)\,\omega(u)\,\mathrm{d}u}_{\text{Point-Identified LIV on the Intervals}} \\ & \quad\quad + \underbrace{\int_{\overline{p}_K}^{1}\Big(y_{\min}\max\{0,\omega(u)\} + y_{\max}\min\{0,\omega(u)\}\Big)\mathrm{d}u}_{\text{Trivial Lower Bound on the Tail}}. \end{split} \end{equation} Similarly, the sharp upper bound is given by replacing $Q_{\omega,k}(1-t)$ with $Q_{\omega,k}(t)$ in the OT term and swapping $y_{\min}$ and $y_{\max}$ in the trivial tail term.

- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-20`: For $\rho\in\{\mu,\nu\}$ and $t\in(0,1)$, $Q_\rho(t)=\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\rho((\! -\infty,a])\geqslant t\}$. — pins reading of ass:1d-ot-probability-laws: The standard generalized-inverse convention for probability quantiles applies to the notation section and Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}; the theorem evaluates these functions at uniform draws and inside Lebesgue integrals.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-29`: Every constraint in $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ is imposed simultaneously for both $w\in\{0,1\}$. — pins reading of def:ot-formulation: The sentence immediately preceding equation $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ explicitly says that every constraint is imposed for both treatment states.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-31`: The partition assertion means $\int_{[0,1]\setminus\left(\bigcup_{k=1}^{K}L_k\,\cup\,\bigcup_{k=0}^{K-1}G_k\,\cup\,(\overline p_K,1]\right)}\mathrm du=0$. — pins reading of def:range-regions: Structural Assumptions gives uniform latent resistance, and the objective decomposition immediately following the region definition uses Lebesgue integrals over precisely these regions in deriving General Closed-Form Sharp Bounds.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-32`: For $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$, the intervals $L_k(x)$, $1\leqslant k\leqslant K_x$, the gaps $G_k(x)$, $0\leqslant k<K_x$, and the tail $(\overline p_{K_x}(x),1]$ partition $[0,1]$ up to Lebesgue-null endpoints. — pins reading of def:range-decomposition-cov: The introduction to the definition gives almost-sure covariate scope. The maximal-interval decomposition step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound explicitly states the index ranges and the partition up to Lebesgue-null endpoints.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-44`: For $i=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $0<t<1$, $Q_{Y,i}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,i}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ and $Q_{\omega,i}(t)=\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:\mathbb P(\omega(U)\leqslant v)\geqslant t\}$, where $U\sim\mathrm{Unif}(p_i,p_{i+1})$. — pins reading of def:discrete-lower-bound-quantiles: The standard quantile convention applies to the probability laws in Theorem~\ref{thm:discrete_lower_bound}; Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot} uses their quantiles for comonotonic and countermonotonic coupling without assuming atomless laws.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-45`: For each probability law $\mu$ used here, $Q_\mu(t)=\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,a])\geqslant t\}$ for $t\in(0,1)$, with the displayed quantile integrals interpreted as Lebesgue integrals over $(0,1)$. — pins reading of def:continuous-lower-bound-quantiles: This is the standard generalized-inverse convention for the quantiles in 1D Optimal Transport with Product Cost, which General Closed-Form Sharp Bounds invokes; the settled quantile domains are $(0,1)$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-46`: For $0<t<1$, use the lower generalized inverses $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ and $Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\}$. — pins reading of def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-quantiles: The notation section uses the standard quantile convention, and the measurability step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound explicitly constructs jointly measurable generalized inverses.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-48`: Interpret the kernel formula with any fixed jointly measurable $\mathcal Y$-valued extension of $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to ranks $0$ and $1$; the asserted gap marginal and attaining objective hold for every such extension. — pins reading of def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-rank-kernel: The sharpness step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound constructs a probability kernel and states that the randomized rank is uniform. Consequently endpoint ranks have zero probability under the product law of the independent gap and auxiliary draws.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-59`: For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$ and $0<t<1$, $(Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t),Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t))=(\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\},\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\})$. — pins reading of def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-quantiles: The pinned quantile convention of \cref{thm:1d_ot}, applied to the conditional outcome and weight laws, and the generalized inverses used in the sharpness construction of \cref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} determine this reading.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-63`: For $0<t<1$, $(Q_\mu(t),Q_\nu(t))=(\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,a])\geqslant t\},\inf\{b\in\mathbb R:\nu(( -\infty,b])\geqslant t\})$. — pins reading of thm:1d-ot: The supplied Villani source, Section 2.2.1, printed page 73, defines the generalized inverse using a strict inequality. The theorem's integrals and uniform coupling draws do not use endpoint values. The paper fixes no convention; the non-strict lower generalized inverse is used throughout (the strict inverse of the cited source differs from it at countably many levels only, which no statement uses).

### `thm:covariate-continuous-lower-bound` (`sections/pid.tex:442-459`)

- as printed: \label{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} Suppose \cref{asp:structure}, \cref{asp:target_weight}, and \cref{asp:continuity_p} hold. For each nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, let $Q_{Y, k \mid x}(t)$ be the quantile function of $\mu_{1,k\mid x}$ defined in (\ref{eq:gap_identified_measure_cov}), and $Q_{\omega, k \mid x}(t)$ the quantile function of $\omega(x, U)$ for $U \sim \text{Unif}(G_k(x))$. In the displayed sums, a term indexed by an empty conditional gap is defined to be zero. The sharp lower bound for the $W=1$ component of the target parameter is \begin{equation} \label{eq:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} \begin{split} \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1} = \mathbb{E}_{X} \Bigg[ & \sum_{k=0}^{K_X-1}(\underline{p}_{k+1}(X)-\overline{p}_k(X))\int_0^1 Q_{Y,k\mid X}(t)\,Q_{\omega,k\mid X}(1-t)\,\mathrm{d}t \\ & + \sum_{k=1}^{K_X}\int_{\underline{p}_k(X)}^{\overline{p}_k(X)} \frac{\diff}{\diff u}g_1^{\mathrm c}(u,X)\, \omega(X, u) \, \mathrm{d}u \\ & + \int_{\overline{p}_{K_X}(X)}^1 \Big( y_{\min} \max\{0, \omega(X, u)\} + y_{\max} \min\{0, \omega(X, u)\} \Big) \mathrm{d}u \Bigg]. \end{split} \end{equation} Similarly, the sharp upper bound is given by replacing $Q_{\omega,k\mid X}(1-t)$ with $Q_{\omega,k\mid X}(t)$ in the OT term and swapping $y_{\min}$ and $y_{\max}$ in the trivial tail term.
- corrected: statement unchanged
- no error: only a side condition or reading the referee made explicit (see below)
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:bk-3`: For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, extend $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to $[0,1]$ by $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0):=y_{\min}$ and $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1):=y_{\max}$. — Equation (\ref{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel}) and its upper-bound analogue require feasible outcome-quantile values at exceptional randomized-rank endpoints.

### `prop:discrete-reduction` (`sections/est_inf.tex:63-69`)

- as printed: \label{prop:discrete_reduction} Suppose \cref{asp:structure} and part~\textup{(i)} of \cref{asp:prop_gap} hold and $\mathcal{Z}$ is finite. Then the sharp lower bound in \cref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} admits the representation \begin{align}\label{eq:target_discrete_iv} \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1} = \mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\bigg(\gamma_{\text{full},k}(X)\, J_{\text{full},k}(X) + \sum_{j=1}^{l_k} \gamma_{j,k}(X)\, J_{j-1,k}(X)\bigg) + \Delta_K(X) \Bigg]. \end{align} The sharp upper bound admits an analogous representation, obtained by replacing $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(u)$ by $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1-u)$ inside $J_{j,k}$ and swapping $y_{\min}$ with $y_{\max}$ in $\Delta_K$.

### `prop:j-specialization` (`sections/est_inf.tex:75-91`)

- as printed: \label{prop:J_specialization} Fix $k \in \{0,\dots,K-1\}$. Set the lower boundary $\nu_{0,k}(x)\equiv y_{\min}$, and let $\nu_{j,k}(x) \coloneqq Q_{Y,k\mid x}(\kappa_{j,k}(x))$ for $j \in \{1,\dots,l_k\}$. \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)] \item \textbf{Continuous outcome.} If $\mu_{1,k\mid X}$ is continuous at $\nu_{j,k}(X)$ and $\nu_{j+1,k}(X)$ almost surely, then, for $j \in \{0,\dots,l_k-1\}$, \begin{equation}\label{eq:J_cexpectation} J_{j,k}(X) = (p_{k+1}(X) - p_k(X))\,\mathbb{E}_{\mu_{1,k\mid X}}\!\big[ Y\,\indicator\!\big(\nu_{j,k}(X) < Y \leqslant \nu_{j+1,k}(X)\big) \big]. \end{equation} \item \textbf{Binary outcome.} If $Y \in \{0,1\}$, then $J_{\text{full},k}(X) = P_{1,k+1}(X) - P_{1,k}(X)$ with $P_{1,k}(x) \coloneqq \mathbb{E}_{\text{obs}}[YW \mid Z \in S_k, X=x]$ for $k\geqslant1$ and the boundary convention $P_{1,0}(x)\equiv0$, and for $j \in \{1,\dots,l_k\}$, \begin{equation}\label{eq:h_jk_binary} J_{j-1,k}(X) = h_{j,k}^{-}(X) \coloneqq \max\!\Big(0,\; q_{j,k}(X) - \max\!\big(q_{j-1,k}(X),\; p_{k+1}(X) - J_{\text{full},k}(X)\big)\Big). \end{equation} Consequently, (\ref{eq:target_discrete_iv}) collapses to \begin{align}\label{eq:binary_lower_bound} \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1} = \mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\bigg(\gamma_{\text{full},k}(X)\, J_{\text{full},k}(X) + \sum_{j=1}^{l_k} \gamma_{j,k}(X)\, h_{j,k}^{-}(X)\bigg) + \Delta_K(X)\Bigg]. \end{align} \end{enumerate}

- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:bk-3`: For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, extend $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to $[0,1]$ by $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0):=y_{\min}$ and $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1):=y_{\max}$. — Equation (\ref{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel}) and its upper-bound analogue require feasible outcome-quantile values at exceptional randomized-rank endpoints.

This fixes generalized inversion rather than ordinary inversion; the displayed formula specifies the standard lower-quantile convention at flat pieces.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-35`: For $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, interpret $\nu_{j,k}(x)=Q_{Y,k\mid x}(\kappa_{j,k}(x))$ using $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ for $0<t<1$, with $\nu_{0,k}(x)=y_{\min}$. — pins reading of def:j-specialization-boundaries: Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization} defines the regular boundaries through the quantile function and explicitly fixes the lower boundary. The lower generalized inverse is the standard quantile-function convention; the propensity-bin and separation conventions place regular thresholds inside $(0,1)$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-46`: For $0<t<1$, use the lower generalized inverses $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ and $Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\}$. — pins reading of def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-quantiles: The notation section uses the standard quantile convention, and the measurability step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound explicitly constructs jointly measurable generalized inverses.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-48`: Interpret the kernel formula with any fixed jointly measurable $\mathcal Y$-valued extension of $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to ranks $0$ and $1$; the asserted gap marginal and attaining objective hold for every such extension. — pins reading of def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-rank-kernel: The sharpness step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound constructs a probability kernel and states that the randomized rank is uniform. Consequently endpoint ranks have zero probability under the product law of the independent gap and auxiliary draws.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-59`: For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$ and $0<t<1$, $(Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t),Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t))=(\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\},\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\})$. — pins reading of def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-quantiles: The pinned quantile convention of \cref{thm:1d_ot}, applied to the conditional outcome and weight laws, and the generalized inverses used in the sharpness construction of \cref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} determine this reading.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-63`: For $0<t<1$, $(Q_\mu(t),Q_\nu(t))=(\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,a])\geqslant t\},\inf\{b\in\mathbb R:\nu(( -\infty,b])\geqslant t\})$. — pins reading of thm:1d-ot: The supplied Villani source, Section 2.2.1, printed page 73, defines the generalized inverse using a strict inequality. The theorem's integrals and uniform coupling draws do not use endpoint values. The paper fixes no convention; the non-strict lower generalized inverse is used throughout (the strict inverse of the cited source differs from it at countably many levels only, which no statement uses).
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-64`: The boundary quantile uses the bounded-domain lower generalized inverse $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathcal Y:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ for $0\leqslant t<1$, so $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0)=y_{\min}$. — pins reading of def:j-specialization-boundaries: The standard lower generalized-inverse convention supplies the interior quantiles. The known compact outcome domain and the structural boundary $\nu_{0,k}=y_{\min}$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization} supply the bounded extension at zero; the policy-level definition explicitly permits $q_{1,k}=p_k$. The proof of part (i) uses the resulting boundary relation $F(\nu_{j,k})=\kappa_{j,k}$ under its continuity hypothesis.

### `thm:dml-normality` (`sections/est_inf.tex:141-152`)

- as printed: \label{thm:dml_normality} Suppose \cref{asp:structure}, \cref{asp:phi_smooth}, \cref{asp:prop_gap}, and \cref{asp:overlap} hold and $\mathcal{Z}$ is finite. Suppose the pre-recovery first stage fitted on $I_1$ satisfies $\max_{z\in\mathcal Z}\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\|_{L_2}=o_P(n^{-1/4})$. Given the recovered level sets, suppose the remaining score nuisances fitted on $I_1$ are clipped to their known bounded ranges, every estimated conditional probability is clipped to $[0,1]$ before any difference is formed, $\hat\pi_k$ and $\hat\tau_{j,k}$ are clipped further to $[c_\pi/2,1]$, and the estimators converge componentwise at rate $o_P(n^{-1/4})$ in $L_2$. Suppose in addition that one of the following holds. \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}] \item \textbf{Continuous outcome.} The nuisances include the boundary quantiles $\nu_{j,k}$, and, uniformly in $X$, each relevant conditional law $Y\mid X,Z\in S_{k'},W=1$ has a density bounded in a fixed neighborhood of every true boundary $\nu_{j,k}(X)$, $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, for $k'=k+1$ and also for $k'=k$ when $k\geqslant1$; at the fixed boundary $\nu_{0,k}=y_{\min}$, these conditional laws have no atom at $y_{\min}$. \item \textbf{Discrete outcome.} The outcome is binary, \cref{asp:discrete_outcome_gap} holds, and the nuisances are $p_k$, $P_{1,k}$, $\pi_k$, $\tau_{j,k}$, $a_{j,k}$, $\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}$, $\gamma_{j,k}$, and $\gamma_K$. \end{enumerate} Then the sample-split estimator (\ref{eq:estimator_dml}) is $\sqrt{n}$-consistent and asymptotically normal: \begin{equation*} \sqrt{n/2} \big( \hat{\underline{\theta}}_{\omega, 1} - \underline{\theta}_{\omega, 1} \big) \xrightarrow{d} \mathcal{N}(0, \sigma^2), \end{equation*} where the asymptotic variance is the variance of the true orthogonal score, $\sigma^2 = \mathbb{E}\big[ ({\psi}(O; \eta) - \underline{\theta}_{\omega, 1})^2 \big]$. It can be consistently estimated by its sample analog $\hat{\sigma}^2 = \frac{1}{|I_2|} \sum_{i \in I_2} \big(\hat{\psi}(O_i; \hat{\eta}) - \hat{\underline{\theta}}_{\omega, 1}\big)^2$.
- corrected: statement unchanged
- no error: only a side condition or reading the referee made explicit (see below)
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:bk-1`: $K\geqslant1$. — The printed tail correction in $\psi_{\Delta_K}$ evaluates $1/\pi_K(X)$ at an observed positive-propensity level.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:bk-2`: $K\geqslant1$. — The printed tail score divides by $\pi_K(X)$; no denominator is defined at the structural index zero.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:bk-3`: For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, extend $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to $[0,1]$ by $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0):=y_{\min}$ and $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1):=y_{\max}$. — Equation (\ref{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel}) and its upper-bound analogue require feasible outcome-quantile values at exceptional randomized-rank endpoints.

The derivation preceding equation \ref{eq:canonical_submeasures} uses it to establish observational identities almost everywhere.

The surrounding prose also recognizes that conditional probabilities are identified only almost surely.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-15`: Any two structural laws satisfying Structural Assumptions and inducing the same $\mathbb P_{obs}$ give the same $\omega(x,u)$ for $\mathbb P_{obs}(\mathrm dx)\,\mathrm du$-almost every $(x,u)$. — pins reading of ass:target-weight-identifiable: The paragraph following Structural Assumptions states that the observed law identifies conditional probabilities almost surely; equation (\ref{eq:omega_form}) constructs weights from such conditional expectations, and equation (\ref{eq:target_mte}) integrates against the covariate marginal and Lebesgue measure.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-16`: The continuity condition holds for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x\in\mathcal X$. — pins reading of ass:continuity-p-on-components: The prose preceding the conditional range decomposition specifies almost every covariate value under the observed law, and the proof of the Conditional General Bound disintegrates over the marginal law of the covariates.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-17`: For every $z\in\mathcal Z$ and $x\in\mathcal X$, $p\mapsto\phi(z,x,p)$ is differentiable on $(0,1)$ and has finite right and left derivatives at $p=0$ and $p=1$, respectively; these are its endpoint values of $\partial\phi/\partial p$. — pins reading of ass:phi-smooth-differentiable: The policy map has domain $\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\times[0,1]$. ### `prop:continuous-reduction` (`sections/est_inf.tex:172-182`)

- as printed: \label{prop:continuous_reduction} Suppose \cref{asp:structure} and \cref{asp:continuity_p} hold, and $\mathcal{Z}$ is connected. Then the sharp lower bound in \cref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} admits the representation \begin{equation} \label{eq:continuous_simplified_target} \begin{split} \underline{\theta}_{\omega, 1} &= \mathbb{E}_{Z,X} \Bigg[ - \int_{\min\{\underline{p}(X), q(Z,X)\}}^{\underline{p}(X)} Q_{1,\underline p\mid X}\!\left({v}/{\underline{p}(X)}\right) \, \mathrm{d}v \\ &\qquad + g_1\big(q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X), X\big) - g_1\big(P, X\big) \\ &\qquad + y_{\min} \max\{0, q(Z,X) - \overline{p}(X)\} \Bigg], \end{split} \end{equation} where the first integral is defined as zero when $\underline{p}(X)=0$. The sharp upper bound is obtained by replacing $Q_{1,\underline p\mid X}(v/\underline{p}(X))$ with $Q_{1,\underline p\mid X}(1-v/\underline{p}(X))$ and $y_{\min}$ with $y_{\max}$.

- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-16`: The continuity condition holds for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x\in\mathcal X$. — pins reading of ass:continuity-p-on-components: The prose preceding the conditional range decomposition specifies almost every covariate value under the observed law, and the proof of the Conditional General Bound disintegrates over the marginal law of the covariates.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-20`: For $\rho\in\{\mu,\nu\}$ and $t\in(0,1)$, $Q_\rho(t)=\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\rho((\! -\infty,a])\geqslant t\}$. — pins reading of ass:1d-ot-probability-laws: The standard generalized-inverse convention for probability quantiles applies to the notation section and Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}; the theorem evaluates these functions at uniform draws and inside Lebesgue integrals.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-29`: Every constraint in $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ is imposed simultaneously for both $w\in\{0,1\}$. — pins reading of def:ot-formulation: The sentence immediately preceding equation $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ explicitly says that every constraint is imposed for both treatment states.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-32`: For $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$, the intervals $L_k(x)$, $1\leqslant k\leqslant K_x$, the gaps $G_k(x)$, $0\leqslant k<K_x$, and the tail $(\overline p_{K_x}(x),1]$ partition $[0,1]$ up to Lebesgue-null endpoints. — pins reading of def:range-decomposition-cov: The introduction to the definition gives almost-sure covariate scope. The maximal-interval decomposition step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound explicitly states the index ranges and the partition up to Lebesgue-null endpoints.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-38`: For $0<t<1$, use the standard lower generalized quantile $Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$. — pins reading of def:boundary-treated-law: The Notation subsection uses quantile functions without imposing atomlessness or strict monotonicity. Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction} integrates this quantile against Lebesgue measure, so the standard generalized-inverse convention applies and endpoint values do not affect the population integrals.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-46`: For $0<t<1$, use the lower generalized inverses $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ and $Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\}$. — pins reading of def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-quantiles: The notation section uses the standard quantile convention, and the measurability step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound explicitly constructs jointly measurable generalized inverses.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-48`: Interpret the kernel formula with any fixed jointly measurable $\mathcal Y$-valued extension of $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to ranks $0$ and $1$; the asserted gap marginal and attaining objective hold for every such extension. — pins reading of def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-rank-kernel: The sharpness step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound constructs a probability kernel and states that the randomized rank is uniform. Consequently endpoint ranks have zero probability under the product law of the independent gap and auxiliary draws.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-59`: For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$ and $0<t<1$, $(Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t),Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t))=(\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\},\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\})$. — pins reading of def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-quantiles: The pinned quantile convention of \cref{thm:1d_ot}, applied to the conditional outcome and weight laws, and the generalized inverses used in the sharpness construction of \cref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} determine this reading.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-60`: For $\underline p(x)>0$ and $0<t<1$, $Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$; at $t=0,1$ use the endpoint extension of the initial-gap outcome quantile in the referenced bound. — pins reading of def:boundary-treated-law: The boundary-treated-law definition identifies this law with the outcome law on the initial gap. The covariate continuous lower-bound theorem supplies the binding generalized-inverse reading and endpoint extension; the continuous-reduction proof uses that same gap law.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-63`: For $0<t<1$, $(Q_\mu(t),Q_\nu(t))=(\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,a])\geqslant t\},\inf\{b\in\mathbb R:\nu(( -\infty,b])\geqslant t\})$. — pins reading of thm:1d-ot: The supplied Villani source, Section 2.2.1, printed page 73, defines the generalized inverse using a strict inequality. The theorem's integrals and uniform coupling draws do not use endpoint values. The paper fixes no convention; the non-strict lower generalized inverse is used throughout (the strict inverse of the cited source differs from it at countably many levels only, which no statement uses).

### `thm:continuous-rate` (`sections/est_inf.tex:223-229`)

- as printed: \label{thm:continuous_rate} Suppose \cref{asp:structure}, \cref{asp:continuity_p}, and \cref{asp:continuous_rates} hold. Then \[ |\hat{\underline\theta}_{\omega,1}-\underline\theta_{\omega,1}| =O_P\!\left(n^{-1/2}+r_{e,n}+r_{g,n}+r_{\mu,n}\right). \]
- corrected: statement unchanged
- no error: only a side condition or reading the referee made explicit (see below)
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:bk-3`: For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, extend $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to $[0,1]$ by $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0):=y_{\min}$ and $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1):=y_{\max}$. — Equation (\ref{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel}) and its upper-bound analogue require feasible outcome-quantile values at exceptional randomized-rank endpoints.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-12`: $Y=WY(1)+(1-W)Y(0)$ almost surely under the structural population law. — pins reading of ass:structure-consistency: Assumption \ref{asp:structure} states consistency as a structural equality of random variables; the subsequent observational derivation states conditional identities almost everywhere, consistently with the standard almost-sure convention.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-13`: $W=\indicator(U\leqslant p(Z,X))$ almost surely under the structural population law. — pins reading of ass:structure-threshold-crossing: The threshold-crossing clause of Assumption \ref{asp:structure} is a structural equality of random variables. The derivation preceding equation \ref{eq:canonical_submeasures} uses it to establish observational identities almost everywhere.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-14`: $\mathcal L(U\mid X=x)=\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$ for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$. — pins reading of ass:structure-uniform-resistance: Assumption \ref{asp:structure} uses conditional-distribution notation without a pointwise covariate quantifier, while its common-support clause explicitly says every covariate value. ### `lem:orthogonality-discrete` (`sections/appendix.tex:805-818`)

- as printed: \label{lem:orthogonality_discrete} Let $\eta_0$ denote the vector of true nuisance parameter values, let $\theta_0 = \underline{\theta}_{\omega,1}$, and let $\mathcal{T}$ be the set of nuisance directions $\delta$ such that $\eta_0 + t\,\delta$ is an admissible nuisance value for all $t$ in a neighborhood of $0$. \begin{enumerate} \item[\textup{(i)}] Under the hypotheses of \cref{thm:dml_normality}\textup{(i)}, the continuous-outcome score $\psi$ assembled in \cref{app:scores_continuous} satisfies \[ \mathbb{E}[\psi(O;\eta_0)] = \theta_0 \qquad\text{and}\qquad \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\,\mathbb{E}\bigl[\psi(O;\eta_0 + t\,\delta)\bigr]\Big|_{t=0} = 0 \quad\text{for every } \delta \in \mathcal{T}, \] where $\theta_0$ is the target~(\ref{eq:target_discrete_iv}). \item[\textup{(ii)}] Under the hypotheses of \cref{thm:dml_normality}\textup{(ii)}, the same two properties hold for the binary-outcome score~(\ref{eq:binary_score}), with $\theta_0$ the target~(\ref{eq:binary_lower_bound}). \end{enumerate}
- corrected: statement unchanged
- no error: only a side condition or reading the referee made explicit (see below)
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:bk-1`: $K\geqslant1$. — The printed tail correction in $\psi_{\Delta_K}$ evaluates $1/\pi_K(X)$ at an observed positive-propensity level.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:bk-3`: For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, extend $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to $[0,1]$ by $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0):=y_{\min}$ and $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1):=y_{\max}$. — Equation (\ref{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel}) and its upper-bound analogue require feasible outcome-quantile values at exceptional randomized-rank endpoints.

The derivation preceding equation \ref{eq:canonical_submeasures} uses it to establish observational identities almost everywhere.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-14`: $\mathcal L(U\mid X=x)=\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$ for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$. — pins reading of ass:structure-uniform-resistance: Assumption \ref{asp:structure} uses conditional-distribution notation without a pointwise covariate quantifier, while its common-support clause explicitly says every covariate value. The surrounding prose also recognizes that conditional probabilities are identified only almost surely.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-15`: Any two structural laws satisfying Structural Assumptions and inducing the same $\mathbb P_{obs}$ give the same $\omega(x,u)$ for $\mathbb P_{obs}(\mathrm dx)\,\mathrm du$-almost every $(x,u)$. — pins reading of ass:target-weight-identifiable: The paragraph following Structural Assumptions states that the observed law identifies conditional probabilities almost surely; equation (\ref{eq:omega_form}) constructs weights from such conditional expectations, and equation (\ref{eq:target_mte}) integrates against the covariate marginal and Lebesgue measure.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-16`: The continuity condition holds for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x\in\mathcal X$. — pins reading of ass:continuity-p-on-components: The prose preceding the conditional range decomposition specifies almost every covariate value under the observed law, and the proof of the Conditional General Bound disintegrates over the marginal law of the covariates.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-17`: For every $z\in\mathcal Z$ and $x\in\mathcal X$, $p\mapsto\phi(z,x,p)$ is differentiable on $(0,1)$ and has finite right and left derivatives at $p=0$ and $p=1$, respectively; these are its endpoint values of $\partial\phi/\partial p$. — pins reading of ass:phi-smooth-differentiable: The policy map has domain $\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\times[0,1]$. The estimation procedure clips $\hat p$ to this interval, and the policy Taylor remainder in (\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}) uses differentiation within that domain.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-18`: There exist finite constants $C,L\geqslant0$ such that, for all $z\in\mathcal Z$, $x\in\mathcal X$, and $p,p'\in[0,1]$, $|\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p)|\leqslant C$ and $|\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p)-\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p')|\leqslant L|p-p'|$. — pins reading of ass:phi-smooth-derivative: The exact-recovery argument for \cref{thm:dml_normality} uses a uniformly bounded derivative, and (\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}) uses its uniformly Lipschitz derivative for the quadratic policy remainder.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-19`: For every $x\in\mathcal X$ and every nonempty $T_{j,k}$, $\tau_{j,k}(x)\geqslant c_\pi$, where $c_\pi>0$ is the same constant used for $\pi_k$ in \cref{asp:overlap}. — pins reading of ass:overlap-policy: Both clauses belong to the single quantified overlap assumption. The proof of \cref{thm:dml_normality} uses this assumption to bound the inverse-probability weights uniformly.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-20`: For $\rho\in\{\mu,\nu\}$ and $t\in(0,1)$, $Q_\rho(t)=\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\rho((\! -\infty,a])\geqslant t\}$. — pins reading of ass:1d-ot-probability-laws: The standard generalized-inverse convention for probability quantiles applies to the notation section and Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}; the theorem evaluates these functions at uniform draws and inside Lebesgue integrals.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-21`: For each fixed $k\in\{0,\dots,K-1\}$ and $j\in\{0,\dots,l_k-1\}$ to which part~(i) is applied, its continuity hypothesis is $\mu_{1,k\mid X}(\{\nu_{j,k}(X)\})=\mu_{1,k\mid X}(\{\nu_{j+1,k}(X)\})=0$ almost surely under the marginal law of $X$. — pins reading of ass:j-specialization-continuity: The proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}(i) fixes the band indices and uses continuity of the distribution function at the two boundaries to identify their probability levels and exclude boundary atoms.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-22`: For a fresh $X$ with the population marginal law and independent of $I_1$, $\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\|_{L_2}^2=\mathbb E[(\hat p(z,X)-p(z,X))^2\mid I_1]$, and $n^{1/4}\max_{z\in\mathcal Z}\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\|_{L_2}\xrightarrow{P}0$ as $n\to\infty$. — pins reading of ass:dml-normality-first-stage: The exact-recovery argument in the proof of \cref{thm:dml_normality} compares $\mathbb E[\hat p(z,X)\mid I_1]$ with $\mathbb E[p(z,X)]$ using this norm, before evaluating the fit on independent covariates in $I_2$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-23`: Pointwise in $x$, the fitted probability, propensity, share, and indicator-mean nuisances lie in $[0,1]$, $\hat\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)\in[-1,1]$, $\hat\nu_{j,k}(x),\hat J_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\pm}(x)\in[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$, and $\hat J_{j,k}^{\pm}(x)\in[\min\{0,y_{\min}\},\max\{0,y_{\max}\}]$, for each applicable fitted component; denominator clipping is applied further as stated. — pins reading of ass:dml-normality-score-clipping: The nuisance definitions and estimation steps in \cref{app:procedure_continuous_outcome,app:procedure_binary_outcome} determine these ranges. The quantile remainder in (\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}) explicitly uses both quantiles lying in $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-24`: Componentwise $L_2$ errors integrate over the marginal law of $X$ under $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}$, holding each fitted function and any instrument argument $z$ fixed; $o_P(n^{-1/4})$ refers to convergence in probability over the observed sample and random partition as $n\to\infty$. — pins reading of ass:dml-normality-nuisance-rates: The sampling setup for Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality} specifies the random sample and partition; its supplied second-order remainder bound, equation~(\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}), uses population covariate integrals of the fitted errors.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-25`: $Y\in\{0,1\}$ almost surely. — pins reading of ass:dml-normality-binary: Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}\textup{(ii)} explicitly states this coding before defining the binary subinterval contribution used by Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}\textup{(ii)}.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-29`: Every constraint in $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ is imposed simultaneously for both $w\in\{0,1\}$. — pins reading of def:ot-formulation: The sentence immediately preceding equation $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ explicitly says that every constraint is imposed for both treatment states.

- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-63`: For $0<t<1$, $(Q_\mu(t),Q_\nu(t))=(\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,a])\geqslant t\},\inf\{b\in\mathbb R:\nu(( -\infty,b])\geqslant t\})$. — pins reading of thm:1d-ot: The supplied Villani source, Section 2.2.1, printed page 73, defines the generalized inverse using a strict inequality. The theorem's integrals and uniform coupling draws do not use endpoint values. The paper fixes no convention; the non-strict lower generalized inverse is used throughout (the strict inverse of the cited source differs from it at countably many levels only, which no statement uses).
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-64`: The boundary quantile uses the bounded-domain lower generalized inverse $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathcal Y:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ for $0\leqslant t<1$, so $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0)=y_{\min}$. — pins reading of def:j-specialization-boundaries: The standard lower generalized-inverse convention supplies the interior quantiles. The known compact outcome domain and the structural boundary $\nu_{0,k}=y_{\min}$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization} supply the bounded extension at zero; the policy-level definition explicitly permits $q_{1,k}=p_k$. The proof of part (i) uses the resulting boundary relation $F(\nu_{j,k})=\kappa_{j,k}$ under its continuity hypothesis.

### `thm:continuous-primitive-rate` (`sections/online_appendix.tex:474-498`)

- as printed: \label{cor:continuous_primitive_rate} Suppose \cref{asp:structure} and \cref{asp:continuity_p} hold. Suppose additionally that $\phi$ is uniformly Lipschitz in its last argument: for some $L_\phi<\infty$, \[ |\phi(z,x,p')-\phi(z,x,p)|\leqslant L_\phi|p'-p| \] for all $(z,x,p,p')\in\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\times[0,1]^2$. Suppose that \cref{asp:continuous_learners} holds with rates $r_{p,n}$, $r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}$, $r_{Q,n}$ and trimming sequence $\varepsilon_n$, that $|I_j|/n\to\rho_j\in(0,1)$ for $j=0,1,2$, and that \[ r_{p,n},\ r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n},\ r_{Q,n},\ \delta_n,\ \varepsilon_n,\ M_Q^{-1}\longrightarrow0. \] Then the nuisance estimators produced by \cref{alg:continuous_instrument} satisfy \cref{asp:continuous_rates} with \[ \begin{aligned} r_{e,n}&=O(r_{p,n}),\\ r_{g,n}&=O\!\left(r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}+r_{p,n}\right),\\ r_{\mu,n}&=O\!\left( r_{Q,n}+\delta_n+r_{p,n}+\varepsilon_n+M_Q^{-1}\right). \end{aligned} \] and consequently \[ |\hat{\underline\theta}_{\omega,1}-\underline\theta_{\omega,1}| =O_P\!\left(n^{-1/2}+r_{p,n}+r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n} +r_{Q,n}+\delta_n+\varepsilon_n+M_Q^{-1}\right). \]
- corrected: statement unchanged
- no error: only a side condition or reading the referee made explicit (see below)
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:bk-3`: For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, extend $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to $[0,1]$ by $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0):=y_{\min}$ and $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1):=y_{\max}$. — Equation (\ref{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel}) and its upper-bound analogue require feasible outcome-quantile values at exceptional randomized-rank endpoints.
- side condition added by the confirming referee `ass:pin-12`: $Y=WY(1)+(1-W)Y(0)$ almost surely under the structural population law. — pins reading of ass:structure-consistency: Assumption \ref{asp:structure} states consistency as a structural equality of random variables; the subsequent observational derivation states conditional identities almost everywhere, consistently with the standard almost-sure convention.

The surrounding prose also recognizes that conditional probabilities are identified only almost surely.

## Findings withdrawn by the owner

Not errors: the paper's owner showed each finding misreads the paper. The printed statement stands, under the owner's reading (or side condition), which the formalization adopts.

- `thm:discrete-lower-bound` (raised at F2.5): the owner's reading (`ass:pin-owner-1`): A finite instrument domain $\mathcal Z$ carries the discrete topology, so the common-support condition $\mathrm{Supp}(Z\mid X=x)=\mathcal Z$ says that every instrument value has positive conditional probability given $X=x$. — the withdrawn finding: [claim-false] Finite compact standard Borel topology and positive mass on every nonempty open set do not ensure positive mass at each instrument value, which the interval identification and sharp-bound formula require.
- `prop:discrete-reduction` (raised at F3): the owner's reading (`ass:pin-owner-2`): A finite instrument domain $\mathcal Z$ carries the discrete topology, so the common-support condition $\mathrm{Supp}(Z\mid X=x)=\mathcal Z$ says that every instrument value has positive conditional probability given $X=x$. — the withdrawn finding: [statement-wrong] The frozen finite instrument setup lacks the topology/singleton-support condition needed to identify every instrument level; Lean supplies it through DiscreteTopology Z.

The lower generalized inverse is the standard quantile-function convention; the propensity-bin and separation conventions place regular thresholds inside $(0,1)$.

The known compact outcome domain and the structural boundary $\nu_{0,k}=y_{\min}$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization} supply the bounded extension at zero; the policy-level definition explicitly permits $q_{1,k}=p_k$. The proof of part (i) uses the resulting boundary relation $F(\nu_{j,k})=\kappa_{j,k}$ under its continuity hypothesis.

The estimation procedure clips $\hat p$ to this interval, and the policy Taylor remainder in (\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}) uses differentiation within that domain.
- clarification (`ass:pin-18`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): There exist finite constants $C,L\geqslant0$ such that, for all $z\in\mathcal Z$, $x\in\mathcal X$, and $p,p'\in[0,1]$, $\|\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p)\|\leqslant C$ and $\|\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p)-\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p')\|\leqslant L\|p-p'\|$. — pins reading of ass:phi-smooth-derivative: The exact-recovery argument for \cref{thm:dml_normality} uses a uniformly bounded derivative, and (\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}) uses its uniformly Lipschitz derivative for the quadratic policy remainder.
- clarification (`ass:pin-19`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): For every $x\in\mathcal X$ and every nonempty $T_{j,k}$, $\tau_{j,k}(x)\geqslant c_\pi$, where $c_\pi>0$ is the same constant used for $\pi_k$ in \cref{asp:overlap}. — pins reading of ass:overlap-policy: Both clauses belong to the single quantified overlap assumption. The proof of \cref{thm:dml_normality} uses this assumption to bound the inverse-probability weights uniformly.
- clarification (`ass:pin-22`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): For a fresh $X$ with the population marginal law and independent of $I_1$, $\\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\\|_{L_2}^2=\mathbb E[(\hat p(z,X)-p(z,X))^2\mid I_1]$, and $n^{1/4}\max_{z\in\mathcal Z}\\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\\|_{L_2}\xrightarrow{P}0$ as $n\to\infty$. — pins reading of ass:dml-normality-first-stage: The exact-recovery argument in the proof of \cref{thm:dml_normality} compares $\mathbb E[\hat p(z,X)\mid I_1]$ with $\mathbb E[p(z,X)]$ using this norm, before evaluating the fit on independent covariates in $I_2$.
- clarification (`ass:pin-23`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): Pointwise in $x$, the fitted probability, propensity, share, and indicator-mean nuisances lie in $[0,1]$, $\hat\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)\in[-1,1]$, $\hat\nu_{j,k}(x),\hat J_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\pm}(x)\in[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$, and $\hat J_{j,k}^{\pm}(x)\in[\min\{0,y_{\min}\},\max\{0,y_{\max}\}]$, for each applicable fitted component; denominator clipping is applied further as stated. — pins reading of ass:dml-normality-score-clipping: The nuisance definitions and estimation steps in \cref{app:procedure_continuous_outcome,app:procedure_binary_outcome} determine these ranges. The quantile remainder in (\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}) explicitly uses both quantiles lying in $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$.
- clarification (`ass:pin-24`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): Componentwise $L_2$ errors integrate over the marginal law of $X$ under $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}$, holding each fitted function and any instrument argument $z$ fixed; $o_P(n^{-1/4})$ refers to convergence in probability over the observed sample and random partition as $n\to\infty$. — pins reading of ass:dml-normality-nuisance-rates: The sampling setup for Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality} specifies the random sample and partition; its supplied second-order remainder bound, equation~(\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}), uses population covariate integrals of the fitted errors.
- clarification (`ass:pin-25`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): $Y\in\{0,1\}$ almost surely. — pins reading of ass:dml-normality-binary: Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}\textup{(ii)} explicitly states this coding before defining the binary subinterval contribution used by Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}\textup{(ii)}.
- clarification (`ass:pin-3`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): Two remaining labeled vertices are connected exactly when their averaged values differ by less than $c_{\mathrm{gap}}/2$. — pins reading of def:dml-estimation-procedure: Both Algorithm~\ref{alg:dml_continuous_outcome} and the Level Sets step in Section~\ref{app:procedure_continuous_outcome} explicitly use ``less than''.
- clarification (`ass:pin-36`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): In this definition, $\psi_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\mathrm{prod}}$ and $\psi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{prod}}$ are the binary-outcome components of Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete}, and $\psi_{\Delta_K}$ is the common tail component of Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}. — pins reading of def:binary-score: The binary nuisance-estimation subsection introduces this display and immediately directs the reader to Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete} for its component formulas.
- clarification (`ass:pin-37`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): The binary nuisance list supplements the separately fitted first-stage propensity, so $\eta(x)=\{p(z,x),p_k(x),P_{1,k}(x),\pi_k(x),\tau_{j,k}(x),a_{j,k}(z,x),\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}(x),\gamma_{j,k}(x),\gamma_K(x)\}_{j,k}$, with $p_0=P_{1,0}=0$ fixed and no fitted $\pi_0$. — pins reading of def:dml-normality-binary-nuisances: Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality} separately imposes the instrument-specific first-stage rate before specifying the remaining score nuisances. - clarification (`ass:pin-4`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): The structural-zero assignment in Algorithm~\ref{alg:dml_continuous_outcome} is $\widehat S_0=\{z:\bar p_2(z)\leqslant\rho_n\}$, with deterministic $\rho_n\downarrow0$ satisfying $n^{1/4}\rho_n\to\infty$. — pins reading of def:dml-continuous-outcome-algorithm: The Level Sets step in Section~\ref{app:procedure_continuous_outcome} explicitly defines this assignment and removes precisely its baseline vertices from the graph.
- clarification (`ass:pin-43`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): The estimated baseline bins used to index $\widehat T_{j,k}$ include their left endpoints and exclude their right endpoints for $k<K$; the final bin for $k=K$ includes both endpoints, including $1$. — pins reading of def:dml-level-recovery: The baseline and alternative instrument level-set definition specifies $[p_k(x),p_{k+1}(x))$ for $k<K$ and $[p_K(x),1]$ for the final bin; the recovery paragraph explicitly uses those bin conventions.
- clarification (`ass:pin-47`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): At each evaluation covariate $X_i$, $(\hat P^*_{1,1}(X_i),\ldots,\hat P^*_{1,K}(X_i))$ is the nondecreasing sort of the clipped vector $(\hat P_{1,1}(X_i),\ldots,\hat P_{1,K}(X_i))$, with $\hat P^*_{1,0}(X_i)=0$; this same vector is used in every score plug-in and residual, without a separate gap projection. — pins reading of def:dml-binary-rearrangement: The third step of the binary-outcome algorithm and the appendix's Rearrangement paragraph explicitly give this operation and its use throughout the scores.
- clarification (`ass:pin-49`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): In this definition, all components of $\psi(O;\eta)$ are those of Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}, in particular $\psi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{prod}}=\gamma_{j,k}\psi_{j-1,k}^{J}+J_{j-1,k}(\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k})$. — pins reading of def:continuous-outcome-score: The sentence immediately following the display states that all components are given in Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}; that subsection explicitly supplies the fractional product formula and its index shift.
- clarification (`ass:pin-65`, used by `thm:dml-normality`): Every nuisance-error norm in this theorem is the population norm $\\|\hat f-f\\|_{L_2}^2=\mathbb E_X[\|\hat f(X)-f(X)\|^2]$, with the fitted function held fixed during integration. — pins reading of thm:dml-normality: The exact-recovery proof bounds marginal-mean errors by these norms, the rearrangement proof integrates squared errors over $x$, and both remainder arguments condition on $I_1$ before integrating over the evaluation law.
- clarification (`ass:pin-38`, used by `prop:continuous-reduction`): For $0<t<1$, use the standard lower generalized quantile $Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$. — pins reading of def:boundary-treated-law: The Notation subsection uses quantile functions without imposing atomlessness or strict monotonicity. - clarification (`ass:pin-60`, used by `prop:continuous-reduction`): For $\underline p(x)>0$ and $0<t<1$, $Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$; at $t=0,1$ use the endpoint extension of the initial-gap outcome quantile in the referenced bound. — pins reading of def:boundary-treated-law: The boundary-treated-law definition identifies this law with the outcome law on the initial gap. - clarification (`ass:pin-30`, used by `thm:continuous-rate`): For every probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathbb R$ and $0<t<1$, $Q_\mu(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$; the integral defining $W_1$ is a Lebesgue integral over $(0,1)$ and requires no endpoint values. — pins reading of def:wasserstein-distance: The notation paragraph calls $Q_\mu$ the quantile function, invoking the standard lower generalized-inverse convention. Its displayed formula for $W_1$ integrates quantiles against Lebesgue measure.
- clarification (`ass:pin-26`, used by `thm:continuous-primitive-rate`): $\\|\hat p-p\\|_\infty=\sup_{(z,x)\in\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X}\|\hat p(z,x)-p(z,x)\|$. — pins reading of ass:continuous-learners-first-stage: The prose introducing the Continuous-Instrument Learner Conditions requires uniform first-stage control because the fit determines conditional support extrema and shrinking boundary-sample membership.
- clarification (`ass:pin-27`, used by `thm:continuous-primitive-rate`): In (L2), $O_P(r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n})$ describes stochastic order over the training observations and random partition, while $\mathbb E_{Z,X}[\cdot\mid I_0,I_1]$ averages an independent population draw of $(Z,X)$ conditional on the partition and the observations indexed by $I_0\cup I_1$. — pins reading of ass:continuous-learners-outcome: The three-way sample-splitting description holds fitted functions fixed for evaluation; the prose following the learner conditions explicitly distinguishes training and target-evaluation laws. The notation section defines stochastic order as boundedness in probability.
- clarification (`ass:pin-28`, used by `thm:continuous-primitive-rate`): In (L3), $O_P(r_{Q,n})$ describes stochastic order over the training observations and random partition, while $\mathbb E_X[\cdot\mid I_0,I_1]$ averages an independent population draw of $X$ conditional on the partition and the observations indexed by $I_0\cup I_1$. — pins reading of ass:continuous-learners-boundary-grid-rate: The learner-condition preamble specifies training conditioning, and the three-way sample-splitting description fixes learned functions during target evaluation. The notation section defines stochastic order as boundedness in probability.
- clarification (`ass:pin-39`, used by `thm:continuous-primitive-rate`): Off-grid evaluations of $\widehat C(\tau\mid x)$ use linear interpolation between adjacent cumulative-integral values on $\{0,\tau_1,\ldots,\tau_{M_Q-1},1\}$, whose values are computed by the trapezoid rule. — pins reading of def:continuous-instrument: The Target Evaluation paragraph in the Continuous Instrument estimation appendix explicitly specifies interpolation between adjacent cumulative-integral grid values for off-grid lookup.

- clarification (`ass:pin-41`, used by `thm:continuous-primitive-rate`): For $0<t<1$, $Q^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$. — pins reading of def:continuous-learners-selected-law: This is the standard lower generalized-inverse reading of the probability-measure quantile notation introduced in the paper's notation section and used for the selected-law target.
- clarification (`ass:pin-42`, used by `thm:continuous-primitive-rate`): The superscript $c$ in $\widetilde Q_m^c$ labels clipping to $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$ and does not denote the transport cost $c$. — pins reading of def:continuous-learners-clipped-grid: The defining minimum-maximum formula is immediately described as the clipped grid fit before rearrangement.
- clarification (`ass:pin-55`, used by `thm:continuous-primitive-rate`): For $0<t<1$, $Q^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in[y_{\min},y_{\max}]:\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}(( -\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$. — pins reading of def:continuous-learners-selected-law: This is the standard lower generalized-inverse convention for the selected-law quantile function in \cref{asp:continuous_learners}; the proof compares the sorted fitted grid with this monotone population quantile vector. - `ass:dml-normality-first-stage`: pinned reading: For a fresh $X$ with the population marginal law and independent of $I_1$, $\\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\\|_{L_2}^2=\mathbb E[(\hat p(z,X)-p(z,X))^2\mid I_1]$, and $n^{1/4}\max_{z\in\mathcal Z}\\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\\|_{L_2}\xrightarrow{P}0$ as $n\to\infty$. - `ass:dml-normality-nuisance-rates`: pinned reading: Componentwise $L_2$ errors integrate over the marginal law of $X$ under $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}$, holding each fitted function and any instrument argument $z$ fixed; $o_P(n^{-1/4})$ refers to convergence in probability over the observed sample and random partition as $n\to\infty$. - `ass:dml-normality-score-clipping`: pinned reading: Pointwise in $x$, the fitted probability, propensity, share, and indicator-mean nuisances lie in $[0,1]$, $\hat\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)\in[-1,1]$, $\hat\nu_{j,k}(x),\hat J_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\pm}(x)\in[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$, and $\hat J_{j,k}^{\pm}(x)\in[\min\{0,y_{\min}\},\max\{0,y_{\max}\}]$, for each applicable fitted component; denominator clipping is applied further as stated.
