In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome part, the no-atom condition at $y_{\min}$ and $\nu_{0,k}=y_{\min}$ imply $M_{0,k}^{+}(X)=0$ almost surely. — The defining display at flat.tex lines 825--829 fixes the conditioning; Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization} fixes the boundary indices. The estimation paragraph at lines 1810--1816 and Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}(i) give the lower-boundary no-atom convention.
- reading of the symbol `M_{j,k}^{-}`: $M_{j,k}^{-}(x)$ is the population conditional indicator mean among treated observations with $Z\in S_k$ at the continuous-outcome boundary $\nu_{j,k}(x)$ for $k\geqslant1$; at $k=0$ this minus-side regression is omitted and defined as zero solely for notational convenience. — The indicator-mean definitions in Section~\ref{subsubsec:continuous_outcome}, the estimation convention following their display in Section~\ref{app:procedure_continuous_outcome}, and the structural-boundary paragraph in Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}.
- reading of the symbol `R_{q,j,k}`: $R_{q,j,k}(O)$ pools the propensity residuals of the instruments sharing one nonempty regular policy level, normalizing by the total level-set probability $\tau_{j,k}(X)$, while its structural integration-boundary indices satisfy $R_{q,0,k}=R_{q,l_k+1,k}\equiv0$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: For $k<K$, a nonempty regular policy level is distinct from $q_{0,k}=p_k$ under part~\textup{(ii)} of Assumption~\ref{asp:prop_gap}; multiple instruments may nevertheless share that regular policy level. — The pooled policy-level residual display and its following normalization and structural-boundary conventions in Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}; Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete} uses the same residual.
- reading of the symbol `R_{P,k}`: $R_{P,k}(O)$ is the binary-outcome treated-success IPW residual for observed positive-propensity levels $1\leqslant k\leqslant K$, with $R_{P,0}\equiv0$ at the fixed structural boundary, without evaluating $\pi_0$ or a conditional regression on $S_0$. — The IPW residual displays and the immediately following structural-boundary sentence in Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete}, together with Assumption~\ref{asp:overlap}.
- reading of the symbol `R_{p,k}`: $R_{p,k}(O)$ is the baseline propensity IPW residual for observed positive-propensity levels $1\leqslant k\leqslant K$, with $R_{p,0}\equiv0$ at the fixed structural boundary $p_0\equiv0$, without evaluating $\pi_0$ or a conditional regression on $S_0$. — The IPW residual displays and the immediately following structural-boundary sentence in Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete}, together with Assumption~\ref{asp:overlap}.
- reading of the symbol `I_{j,k}^{-}`: $I_{j,k}^{-}(x)=\indicator\!\big(q_{j,k}(x)>\max\{q_{j-1,k}(x),p_{k+1}(x)-J_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)\}\big)=\indicator(h_{j,k}^{-}(x)>0)$ is the binary-score active-set indicator for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, with $q_{0,k}(x)=p_k(x)$. — Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete} gives both defining expressions; the finite-instrument decomposition specifies the sub-interval indices and structural lower boundary.
- reading of the symbol `D_{j,k}^{-}`: $D_{j,k}^{-}(x)=\indicator\!\big(q_{j-1,k}(x)<p_{k+1}(x)-J_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)<q_{j,k}(x)\big)$ is the binary-score boundary indicator for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, with $q_{0,k}(x)=p_k(x)$; it is zero when the integration boundary equals either sub-interval endpoint. — Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete} defines the strict boundary indicator; the finite-instrument decomposition specifies its indices and structural lower boundary, and Section~\ref{oa:orthogonality_binary} repeats the same definition.
- reading of the symbol `E_{j,k}^{-}`: $E_{j,k}^{-}(x)=\indicator\!\big(q_{j-1,k}(x)>p_{k+1}(x)-J_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)\big)$ is the binary-score dominance indicator for $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, with $q_{0,k}(x)=p_k(x)$; its strict inequality assigns zero at equality. — Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete} defines the indicator; the finite-instrument decomposition specifies the sub-interval indices and structural lower boundary.
- reading of the symbol `\psi_{j,k}^J`: $\psi_{j,k}^{J}(O;\eta)$ is the continuous-outcome uncentered lower-bound score for $\mathbb E[J_{j,k}(X)]$, used for $0\leq k<K$ and $0\leq j<l_k$, with $R_{q,0,k}=R_{q,l_k+1,k}=0$, all minus-side terms omitted at $k=0$, and no standalone $+\nu_{0,k}$ term in $\alpha_{p_k}$ at $j=0$. — Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous} gives the complete score, structural-zero convention, pooled residual boundaries, and the $\indicator(j\geqslant1)\nu_{j,k}$ term in the Riesz representer.
- reading of the symbol `\psi_{\text{full},k}^J`: $\psi_{\text{full},k}^{J}(O;\eta)$ is the uncentered full-interval component score for $\mathbb E[J_{\text{full},k}(X)]$, for $0\leq k<K$, with no quantile-constraint correction and with structural-zero rather than conditional-regression terms at $k=0$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome case, use the displayed treated-outcome and propensity residual corrections, omitting all minus-side terms at $k=0$; in the binary-outcome case, use $\psi_{\text{full},k}^{J}=J_{\text{full},k}+R_{P,k+1}-R_{P,k}$ with $R_{P,0}=0$. — The full-interval score display and its following structural-boundary sentence in Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}, and the binary full-interval formula in Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete}.
- reading of the symbol `\psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}`: $\psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}(O;\eta)$ is the uncentered lower-bound fractional-interval product score for $0\leq k<K$ and $1\leq j\leq l_k$, using the conditional union-indicator residual $\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k}(X)$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome case, $\psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}=\gamma_{j,k}\psi_{j-1,k}^{J}+J_{j-1,k}(\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k})$; in the binary-outcome case, $\psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}=\gamma_{j,k}\psi_{j,k}^{h}+h_{j,k}^{-}(\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k})$, with covariate and observation arguments as in the defining displays. — The product-score displays in Sections~\ref{app:scores_continuous} and~\ref{app:scores_discrete} specify the different component scores and the offset $j-1$.
- reading of the symbol `\psi_{\text{full},k}^{\text{prod}}`: $\psi_{\text{full},k}^{\text{prod}}(O;\eta)$ is the uncentered lower-bound product score for $\mathbb E[\gamma_{\text{full},k}(X)J_{\text{full},k}(X)]$, for $0\leq k<K$, using the full-interval component score appropriate to the outcome setting and the difference of policy and baseline higher-bin indicators. — The identical product-score displays in Sections~\ref{app:scores_continuous} and~\ref{app:scores_discrete}.
- reading of the symbol `\psi_{\Delta_K}`: $\psi_{\Delta_K}(O;\eta)$ is the uncentered lower-bound score for $\mathbb E[\Delta_K(X)]$ on the final bin $[p_K(X),1]$, with the same formula for continuous and binary outcomes and with the union taken over the actual policy levels $j=1,\ldots,l_K$, including a policy value equal to one. — The trivial-bound score display in Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}, the final-bin conventions preceding Assumption~\ref{asp:prop_gap}, and the concluding sentence of Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete}.
- reading of the symbol `\psi_{j,k}^{h}`: $\psi_{j,k}^{h}(O;\eta)$ is the uncentered binary-outcome score for $\mathbb E[h_{j,k}^{-}(X)]$, indexed by $0\leq k<K$ and $1\leq j\leq l_k$, with $R_{P,0}=R_{p,0}=0$ and $R_{q,0,k}=0$, so the lower-policy-boundary correction vanishes at $j=1$ and its propensity correction is included in $\alpha_{p,k}^{(1,k)}$. — Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete} defines the residuals, structural-zero conventions, derivatives, and complete score display.
- reading of the symbol `\underline{p}`: $\underline p(x)=\inf_{z\in\mathcal Z}p(z,x)$ is the population lower propensity endpoint at covariate value $x$, with values in $[0,1]$ and with zero allowed. In Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\underline p(x)$ is the lower endpoint of the connected conditional support $[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$ of $P\mid X=x$. — Lines 878--883 define the infimum over $\mathcal Z$ and identify the connected conditional support. Lines 890 and 903 explicitly handle $\underline p(x)=0$.
- reading of the symbol `\overline{p}`: $\overline p(x)=\sup_{z\in\mathcal Z}p(z,x)$ is the population upper propensity endpoint at covariate value $x$, with values in $[0,1]$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\overline p(x)$ is the upper endpoint of the connected conditional support $[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$ of $P\mid X=x$. — Lines 878--883 define the supremum over $\mathcal Z$ and identify the connected conditional support; the later positive-part tail and projection formulas retain this meaning.
- reading of the symbol `g_1`: $g_1=g_1^{\mathrm c}$ denotes the canonical population treated regression obtained by integrating $y$ against the canonical observed treated submeasure, identified on the conditional propensity support including its endpoints, with intervalwise Lebesgue-a.e. derivatives on non-degenerate LIV intervals. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound}: With no covariates, $g_1(u)=\int_{\mathcal Y}y\,H_1(u,\mathrm dy)$; derivatives are taken separately on the non-degenerate intervals $L_k$. In Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction}, Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The identified conditional support is $[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$, and target evaluations use $q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X)$ and $P$ within this support. — The canonical-version discussion following Equation~(\ref{eq:canonical_submeasures}) establishes identification on support; the definitions preceding Theorems~\ref{thm:continuous_lower_bound} and~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} specify intervalwise derivatives; the connected-instrument subsection explicitly sets $g_1=g_1^{\mathrm c}$.
- reading of the symbol `\hat p`: $\hat p:\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\to[0,1]$ is a fitted estimator of the conditional treatment propensity $p(z,x)$, with fitted values clipped to $[0,1]$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\hat p$ is the pre-recovery first-stage fit trained on $I_1$; the level-recovery averages evaluate this fit at the covariates in $I_2$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\max_{z\in\mathcal Z}\\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\\|_{L_2}=o_P(n^{-1/4})$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\hat p$ is trained on the propensity sample $I_0$ and is held fixed conditional on $I_0$ when constructing the generated propensity index and localization rules on $I_1$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\\|\hat p-p\\|_\infty=O_P(r_{p,n})$. — The discrete estimation procedure at flat.tex line 834, its algorithm at line 1761, and the continuous-instrument construction at line 1884.
- reading of the symbol `\hat q`: $\hat q(z,x)=\phi(z,x,\hat p(z,x))\in[0,1]$ is the fitted alternative propensity obtained by substituting the clipped baseline propensity estimator into the known policy map. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: $\hat q$ is formed from the pre-recovery propensity fit $\hat p$ trained on $I_1$; the level-recovery average evaluates this fitted function at the covariates in $I_2$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\hat q$ is formed from $\hat p$ trained on $I_0$; the notation $\hat q=\phi(Z,X,\hat p(Z,X))$ denotes evaluation of this fitted function at $(Z,X)$. — Flat.tex lines 834, 1761 and 1773 specify the discrete first-stage substitution on $I_1$; lines 908--912, 1884--1889 and 1964 specify its continuous-instrument construction on $I_0$.
- reading of the symbol `\hat P`: $\hat P=\hat p(Z,X)$ denotes the fitted baseline propensity index, with observation-indexed notation $\hat P_i=\hat p(Z_i,X_i)$ and values in $[0,1]$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The fitted map $\hat p$ is held fixed conditional on the training samples $I_0,I_1$ when evaluating expressions involving $\hat P=\hat p(Z,X)$ under the target distribution of $(Z,X)$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The fitted map $\hat p$ is trained on $I_0$, so the selection rule involving $\hat P$ is fixed conditional on $I_0$. — Line 912 defines the unindexed notation; Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} defines its observation-indexed form and fits the clipped propensity on $I_0$. The selected-law definition conditions on $I_0$, and the target-evaluation losses condition on $I_0,I_1$.
- reading of the symbol `\hat{\underline p}`: $\hat{\underline p}(x)=\inf_{z\in\mathcal Z}\hat p(z,x)$ is the fitted lower propensity endpoint, a data-dependent function of $x$ taking values in $[0,1]$, held fixed conditional on the training samples $I_0,I_1$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\hat{\underline p}$ is obtained from the propensity fit trained on $I_0$ and is therefore fixed conditional on $I_0$ in the selected-law definition. — Line 912 defines fitted endpoints by substitution and clips propensity estimates to their support; Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} explicitly takes the infimum of the clipped fit trained on $I_0$. Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_rates} conditions endpoint losses on $I_0,I_1$.
- reading of the symbol `\hat{\overline p}`: $\hat{\overline p}(x)=\sup_{z\in\mathcal Z}\hat p(z,x)\in[0,1]$ is the fitted upper propensity endpoint, with the fitted function held fixed in target evaluation conditional on $I_0,I_1$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The endpoint is computed from $\hat p$ fitted on $I_0$. — Flat.tex lines 908--912 define the split and plug-in endpoints; lines 1884--1890 specify the supremum over $\mathcal Z$, and Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} constructs it from clipped first-stage predictions.
- reading of the symbol `\hat q^{\mathrm{tr}}`: $\hat q^{\mathrm{tr}}(z,x)=\min\{\hat{\overline p}(x),\max\{\hat{\underline p}(x),\hat q(z,x)\}\}$ is the fitted policy propensity projected onto $[\hat{\underline p}(x),\hat{\overline p}(x)]$, with its fitted map held fixed in target evaluation conditional on $I_0,I_1$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The fitted map is constructed on $I_0$ from the clipped $\hat p$ and $\hat q(z,x)=\phi(z,x,\hat p(z,x))$. — Flat.tex lines 908--933 define substitution of fitted nuisances and training-conditional target evaluation; lines 1884--1890 and Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} specify the first-stage construction.
- reading of the symbol `\hat g_1`: $\hat g_1(u,x)$ is an estimated regression on $[0,1]\times\mathcal X$, clipped to the known range $[\min\{0,y_{\min}\},\max\{0,y_{\max}\}]$ of $YW$, and evaluated at the estimated target indices $\hat q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X)$ and $\hat P$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: At $u_m=m/M_g$, $m=0,\ldots,M_g$, the fitted value is the intercept in $(\hat g_1(u_m,\cdot),\hat\beta_m)\in\arg\min_{g,\beta\in\mathcal F_n}\sum_{i\in I_1}K_{h_n}(\hat P_i-u_m)[Y_iW_i-g(X_i)-\beta(X_i)(\hat P_i-u_m)]^2$; between grid points it is the clipped linear interpolant. The learner loss compares $\hat g_1(\hat U_\ell,X)$ with $g_1(\Pi_X(\hat U_\ell),X)$ for $\ell\in\{1,2\}$. — Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_rates}, part (2), specifies clipping and target arguments; Section~\ref{app:procedure_continuous_instrument} specifies the local-linear construction; Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_learners}, part (L2), specifies its projected population comparison.
- reading of the symbol `\hat\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}`: $\hat\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}$ is an estimated probability law on $\mathcal Y$, indexed by $x\in\mathcal X$, whose quantile function is $\widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}$ and whose target is the canonical treated boundary law; its target-error loss is weighted by $\underline p(x)$, so its value where $\underline p(x)=0$ does not affect the target. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: The law is generated by conditional check-function fits on $\mathcal I_{\delta,1}=\{i\in I_1:W_i=1,\hat P_i\leqslant\hat{\underline p}(X_i)+\delta_n\}$ at $\tau_m=m/M_Q$, $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$, clipped to $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$, sorted at each $x$, and linearly interpolated between adjacent quantile levels; endpoint values may be $y_{\min},y_{\max}$ or the smallest and largest fitted values. — Equation~(\ref{eq:boundary_treated_law}) and its following zero-boundary convention specify the target; Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_rates}, part (3), specifies the estimated law and weighted loss; Section~\ref{app:procedure_continuous_instrument} specifies the concrete construction.
- reading of the symbol `\widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}`: $\widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}$ denotes the quantile function of $\hat\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}$, with values in $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$ and endpoint values supplied by the chosen endpoint convention. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: At $\tau_m=m/M_Q$, $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$, fit $\widetilde Q(\tau_m\mid\cdot)\in\arg\min_{f\in\mathcal F_Q}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{\delta,1}}\rho_{\tau_m}(Y_i-f(X_i))$, setting every grid fit to $y_{\min}$ if the selected sample is empty; clip to $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$, sort the fitted values for each $x$, and interpolate linearly in $\tau$, with endpoints either $y_{\min},y_{\max}$ or the smallest and largest fitted values. — Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_rates} identifies the estimator as the quantile function of the estimated law; flat.tex line 1920 and Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} specify the concrete construction and the allowed endpoint conventions.
- reading of the symbol `\hat\pi_k`: $\hat\pi_k(x)$ estimates $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z\in S_k\mid X=x)$ by fitting the indicator of the recovered set $\widehat S_k$ on $I_1$, for $k=1,\ldots,K$, and is clipped first to $[0,1]$ and then to $[c_\pi/2,1]$; no denominator estimator $\hat\pi_0$ is required at the structural boundary. — Algorithm~\ref{alg:dml_continuous_outcome} specifies indicator fitting and clipping; Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality} repeats the clipping condition; the paragraph following Assumption~\ref{asp:overlap} omits all minus-side corrections at $k=0$.
- reading of the symbol `\hat\tau_{j,k}`: $\hat\tau_{j,k}(x)$ estimates $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z\in T_{j,k}\mid X=x)$ by fitting the indicator of the recovered set $\widehat T_{j,k}$ on $I_1$, for nonempty policy level sets with $k=0,\ldots,K$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, and is clipped first to $[0,1]$ and then to $[c_\pi/2,1]$. — The estimation paragraph in Section~\ref{subsubsec:continuous_outcome} describes recovery using evaluation covariates; Algorithm~\ref{alg:dml_continuous_outcome} specifies indicator fitting and clipping; Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality} imposes the same clipping.
- reading of the symbol `r_{e,n}`: $r_{e,n}$ is a nonnegative rate sequence tending to zero controlling each of the three training-conditional absolute-error losses for the boundary and tail maps. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $r_{e,n}=O(r_{p,n})$ for the concrete nuisance construction. — Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_rates}, part (1), defines the three losses; Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate} and its proof derive their first-stage rate.
- reading of the symbol `r_{g,n}`: $r_{g,n}$ is a nonnegative rate sequence tending to zero for the sum of the two training-conditional target-evaluation losses, comparing fitted regressions at estimated indices with population regressions at the corresponding true indices. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $r_{g,n}=O(r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}+r_{p,n})$ for the concrete nuisance construction. — Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_rates}, part (2), specifies both evaluation arguments and the conditioning; Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate} specifies the concrete learner rate.
- reading of the symbol `r_{\mu,n}`: $r_{\mu,n}$ is a nonnegative deterministic rate sequence tending to zero for the training-conditional, $\underline p(X)$-weighted Wasserstein loss against the canonical treated boundary law. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: For the concrete nuisance construction, one may take $r_{\mu,n}=O(r_{Q,n}+\delta_n+r_{p,n}+\varepsilon_n+M_Q^{-1})$. — Part (3) of Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_rates} defines this loss and its vanishing rate; Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate} supplies the construction-specific bound.
- reading of the symbol `r_{p,n}`: $r_{p,n}$ is a nonnegative deterministic sample-size rate for uniform propensity estimation, rather than an integrated prediction loss. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $r_{p,n}\to0$. — Condition (L1) of Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_learners} gives the sup-norm rate, and the preceding discussion explains its role in support extrema and boundary-sample membership.
- reading of the symbol `r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}`: $r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}$ is a nonnegative deterministic rate sequence indexed by sample size for prediction under both target evaluation-index laws, using the projected estimated index in the population comparator. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}\to0$. — Condition (L2) of Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_learners} specifies both arguments and the projection; its following discussion distinguishes the evaluation laws from the generated training design.
- reading of the symbol `r_{Q,n}`: $r_{Q,n}$ is a nonnegative deterministic rate sequence indexed by sample size, measuring the interior-grid quantile-fit loss relative to the selected-law target, rather than the canonical boundary law. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $r_{Q,n}\to0$. — Condition (L3) of Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_learners} defines the selected-law comparison and interior grid; Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate} explicitly requires the rate to vanish.
- reading of the symbol `\Lambda`: $\Lambda$ is population notation whose meaning is determined by its local introduction: an evaluated endpoint or tail expression, or the logistic function in the simulation index. In Theorem~\ref{thm:continuous_rate}: $\Lambda$ ranges separately over $\underline p(X)$, $\min\{\underline p(X),q(Z,X)\}$, and $(q(Z,X)-\overline p(X))_+$, with the error condition required for each choice; these are evaluated population expressions under the law of $(Z,X)$. — Part (1) of Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_rates} introduces the endpoint expressions; the simulation covariate-index sentence at flat.tex line 3283 explicitly assigns the logistic meaning.
- reading of the symbol `\hat\Lambda`: $\hat\Lambda$ denotes the corresponding estimated endpoint or tail expression evaluated at $(Z,X)$; in $\mathbb E_{Z,X}[\|\hat\Lambda-\Lambda\|\mid I_0,I_1]$, the fitted functions are held fixed and the expectation integrates the evaluation law of $(Z,X)$. — Part (1) of Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_rates} lists the three population expressions and explicitly conditions their estimation loss on $I_0,I_1$.
- reading of the symbol `\delta_n`: $\delta_n>0$ is the fixed, sample-size-indexed localization window used in the fitted selection rule $\hat P\le\hat{\underline p}(X)+\delta_n$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $\delta_n\to0$ as $n\to\infty$. — Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} fixes the localization window and uses it to select treated observations; Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate} explicitly requires $\delta_n\to0$.
- reading of the symbol `\varepsilon_n`: $\varepsilon_n$ is the quantile-analysis trimming sequence satisfying $M_Q^{-1}\le\varepsilon_n<1/2$ and $\varepsilon_n\to0$; it restricts the learner analysis to intermediate quantiles without changing the estimator. — Condition (L3) states the inequalities and convergence; its explanatory paragraph states that trimming leaves the estimator unchanged.
- reading of the symbol `M_g`: $M_g$ is a fixed positive integer tuning choice for the regression grid at each sample size, giving spacing $M_g^{-1}$ and $M_g+1$ grid points $u_m$, $m=0,\ldots,M_g$. — Flat.tex line 1893 and Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} specify the fixed grid $u_m=m/M_g$, $m=0,\ldots,M_g$.
- reading of the symbol `M_Q`: $M_Q$ is the integer resolution of the quantile grid $\tau_m=m/M_Q$, $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$, and may vary with $n$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $M_Q^{-1}\to0$, so the quantile grid resolution diverges with sample size. — Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} fixes the grid, and Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate} requires $M_Q^{-1}\to0$.
- reading of the symbol `u_m`: $u_m=m/M_g$ for $m=0,\ldots,M_g$ is the fixed regression index grid spanning $[0,1]$, including both endpoints. — Flat.tex line 1893 and the first step of Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} both give the same formula and index range.
- reading of the symbol `\tau_m`: $\tau_m=m/M_Q$ for $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$ denotes the interior quantile grid; the interpolation endpoint levels $0$ and $1$ are separate from these indexed grid points. — The treated-boundary-law construction at line 1912 and Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} specify the index range and separately discuss endpoint values.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal F_n`: $\mathcal F_n$ is a user-chosen, potentially $n$-dependent class of real-valued functions of $x$, used for both the intercept function $g$ and the slope function $\beta$ in each local-linear fit. — The local-linear minimization and its following sentence in Section~\ref{app:procedure_continuous_instrument} specify that both $g$ and $\beta$ belong to $\mathcal F_n$.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal F_Q`: $\mathcal F_Q$ is the chosen class of real-valued functions of $x$ over which the boundary conditional quantile check-function regression is minimized; with no covariates it is the class of constants. — Equation~(\ref{eq:boundary_quantile_regression}) and the sentences immediately following it specify the optimization domain and the no-covariate convention.
- reading of the symbol `\widetilde Q`: $\widetilde Q$ is the raw interior-grid check-regression fit on the treated localized sample from $I_1$, whose selection rule is determined by the first stage on $I_0$, before clipping, rearrangement, and interpolation. — Equation~\ref{eq:boundary_quantile_regression} gives the optimization; Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} specifies the grid and empty-sample branch. Line 1920 assigns interpolation and endpoint conventions to the resulting $\widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}$.
- reading of the symbol `\rho_\tau`: $\rho_\tau(u)=u(\tau-\indicator(u<0))$ is the check loss for $u\in\mathbb R$ at the quantile levels $\tau=\tau_m=m/M_Q$, $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$, used in the boundary quantile regression. — Equation~\ref{eq:boundary_quantile_regression} at flat.tex lines 1912--1918 specifies both the check loss and its interior quantile grid.
- reading of the symbol `\widehat C`: $\widehat C(\tau\mid x)=\int_0^\tau\widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}(s)\,\mathrm ds$ for $\tau\in[0,1]$ and $x\in\mathcal X$, with grid values computed exactly by the trapezoid rule and off-grid evaluation implemented by linear interpolation of adjacent grid values. — The definition and numerical evaluation convention are given at flat.tex lines 1931--1942 and in Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument}.
- reading of the symbol `\hat\tau_i`: For $i\in I_2$ with $\hat{\underline p}(X_i)>0$, $\hat\tau_i=\min\{1,\hat q(Z_i,X_i)/\hat{\underline p}(X_i)\}\in[0,1]$ is the normalized boundary-integration cutoff; when $\hat{\underline p}(X_i)=0$ it is not evaluated, and when $\hat q(Z_i,X_i)\geqslant\hat{\underline p}(X_i)>0$ it equals one. — Flat.tex lines 1930--1942 specify its positive-denominator domain, zero branch, and empty-region value. Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} defines it during evaluation on $I_2$.
- reading of the symbol `\hat\psi_1`: $\hat\psi_1(O)$ denotes the estimated lower-bound boundary contribution $-\int_{\min\{\hat{\underline p}(X),\hat q(Z,X)\}}^{\hat{\underline p}(X)}\widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid X}(v/\hat{\underline p}(X))\,\mathrm dv$, with value zero when $\hat{\underline p}(X)=0$. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: For $\hat{\underline p}(X_i)>0$, the concrete implementation evaluates $-\hat{\underline p}(X_i)[\widehat C(1\mid X_i)-\widehat C(\hat\tau_i\mid X_i)]$, where $\hat\tau_i=\min\{1,\hat q(Z_i,X_i)/\hat{\underline p}(X_i)\}$; cumulative-integral grid values are computed by the trapezoid rule, and off-grid lookup uses linear interpolation between adjacent cumulative-integral grid values. For $\hat{\underline p}(X_i)=0$, it returns zero without evaluating $\hat\tau_i$. — The target-evaluation passage in Section~\ref{app:procedure_continuous_instrument} defines the integral and its zero branch, distinguishes $\hat\psi_1^{\,+}$, and specifies trapezoid evaluation and interpolation at flat.tex lines 1924--1942; Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} supplies the implementation.
- reading of the symbol `\hat\psi_3`: $\hat\psi_3(O)=y_{\min}(\hat q(Z,X)-\hat{\overline p}(X))_+$ is the fitted lower-bound tail score, evaluated at $O_i$ for $i\in I_2$ with the fitted score map held fixed conditional on $I_0,I_1$. — Flat.tex lines 908--912 define evaluation on $I_2$; lines 1948--1954 and Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument} give the score formula and its evaluation.
- reading of the symbol `\rho_j`: For $j\in\{0,1,2\}$, $\rho_j\in(0,1)$ is the limiting split proportion $\|I_j\|/n\to\rho_j$, with $\rho_0+\rho_1+\rho_2=1$. — Flat.tex line 908 and line 1882 describe a partition into three samples with these positive limits; Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate} repeats the index range at line 3014.
- reading of the symbol `L_\phi`: $L_\phi<\infty$ is a uniform Lipschitz bound satisfying $\|\phi(z,x,p')-\phi(z,x,p)\|\leqslant L_\phi\|p'-p\|$ for every $(z,x,p,p')\in\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\times[0,1]^2$. — Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}, flat.tex lines 3010--3013, states the bound and its full domain; its proof uses the same constant for the sup-norm policy error.
- reading of the symbol `U_\ell`: For $\ell\in\{1,2\}$, $U_1=q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X)$ and $U_2=P=p(Z,X)$ are the population evaluation indices, with $U_\ell\in\mathcal S_X$; their estimated counterparts are $\hat U_1=\hat q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X)$ and $\hat U_2=\hat P$. — The definitions preceding Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_learners} specify all four indices, and the proof of Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate} explicitly uses $U_\ell\in\mathcal S_X$.
- reading of the symbol `\hat U_\ell`: For $\ell\in\{1,2\}$, $\hat U_1=\hat q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X)$ and $\hat U_2=\hat P$ are the two target-evaluation indices, with evaluation over $(Z,X)$ conditional on $I_0,I_1$, and the population comparison for $\hat g_1(\hat U_\ell,X)$ is $g_1(\Pi_X(\hat U_\ell),X)$. — The definitions at flat.tex lines 3039--3045 and condition (L2) at lines 3052--3061 specify the indices and conditional evaluation. Lines 3094--3097 explain that the policy index can have a different evaluation distribution and endpoint mass.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal S_x`: $\mathcal S_x=[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$ is the population propensity interval at covariate value $x$, used as the domain of the population comparison after projection by $\Pi_x$. — The definitions preceding Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_learners} give $\mathcal S_x$ and $\Pi_x$; the subsequent explanation says projection keeps the population comparison inside its identified support.
- reading of the symbol `\Pi_x`: $\Pi_x:\mathbb R\to\mathcal S_x$ is projection onto the population support interval $\mathcal S_x=[\underline p(x),\overline p(x)]$, given by $\Pi_x(u)=\min\{\overline p(x),\max\{\underline p(x),u\}\}$. — Lines 3039--3043 define $\mathcal S_x$ and $\Pi_x$ together; line 3094 explains that this projection keeps the population comparison inside its identified support.
- reading of the symbol `\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}`: $\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}$ is the conditional population outcome law under the fitted selection event $\{W=1,\hat p(Z,X)\le\hat{\underline p}(x)+\delta_n\}$ given $X=x$ and $I_0$, with the canonical boundary law used when that event has zero conditional probability. — Lines 3063--3072 give the conditioning event and fallback explicitly; lines 3146--3155 treat this law, conditional on $I_0$, as a mixture of treated population laws.
- reading of the symbol `Q^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}`: $Q^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}:(0,1)\to\mathcal Y$ is the quantile function of the $I_0$-conditional selected law $\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}$, indexed by sample size $n$ and covariate value $x$, including its stipulated zero-selection version. — Lines 3063--3073 define the selected law conditional on $I_0$, specify its zero-selection version, and introduce its quantile function; lines 3096 and 3219 identify this as the target of the localized quantile regression. Line 3169 only recalls monotonicity and boundedness and does not redefine the symbol.
- reading of the symbol `\widetilde Q_m^c`: $\widetilde Q_m^c(x)$ is the clipped pre-rearrangement fit at $\tau_m=m/M_Q$, for $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$, obtained from the boundary quantile regression on $\mathcal I_{\delta,1}\subseteq I_1$ using the selection rule determined on $I_0$. — The construction and grid are specified at lines 1963--1975; lines 3074--3089 define the clipped fit and compare it to the selected-law quantile.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal J_{Q,n}`: $\mathcal J_{Q,n}=\{m\in\{1,\ldots,M_Q-1\}:\varepsilon_n\le\tau_m\le1-\varepsilon_n\}$ is the interior grid subset used for analytical quantile trimming; it does not trim the estimator. — Condition (L3) defines the subset, and the paragraph following Assumption~\ref{asp:continuous_learners} explicitly says that this analytical trimming leaves the estimator unchanged.
- reading of the symbol `\pi`: The meaning of $\pi$ is determined by its arguments: transport notation denotes a joint probability measure, whereas $\pi(z,x)$ denotes the conditional instrument assignment probability $\mathbb P(Z=z\mid X=x)$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}: $\pi$ ranges over $\Pi(\mu,\nu)\subseteq\mathcal P(\mathbb R^2)$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In instrument-specific probability expressions, $\pi(z,x)=\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(Z=z\mid X=x)$, rather than a transport coupling. — The coupling definition at flat.tex line 233 fixes the transport meaning; the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete} explicitly uses $\mathbb E[\indicator(Z=z)\mid X]=\pi(z,X)$ at line 2859.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal B`: The notation $\mathcal B$ denotes an OT state space when standing alone and the Borel $\sigma$-field operator when applied to an outcome domain. In Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}: The second marginal state space is $\mathcal B=\mathbb R$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: In the kernel definition, $A\in\mathcal B(\mathcal Y)$ means that $A$ is a Borel subset of $\mathcal Y$. — The standalone state-space notation is introduced at line 233; Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot} places both marginals on $\mathbb R$. The kernel display at line 1742 uses $A\in\mathcal B(\mathcal Y)$, with the Borel convention specified at line 159.
- reading of the symbol `\indep`: $\indep$ denotes independence, with $A\indep B\mid C$ denoting conditional independence given $C$, namely factorization of the conditional joint law into the two conditional marginal laws almost surely. — The notation paragraph at line 257 explicitly declares $\indep$ to mean (conditional) independence; Assumption~\ref{asp:structure} and the construction in the proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:sharpness} use conditional factorization given $X$.
- reading of the symbol `\Gamma_{1,x}`: $\Gamma_{1,x}$ is the conditional treated feasible class defined for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$, with its members understood through their Lebesgue-almost-everywhere kernel values in $u$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: For $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$, the observational constraint equivalently holds as $\int_0^p\pi_{1,x}(\mathrm dy\mid u)\,\mathrm du=H_{1,x}(p,\mathrm dy)$ for every $p\in p(\mathcal Z,x)$, using the canonical continuous version of $H_{1,x}$. — The disintegration paragraph at lines 1632--1642 specifies the constraints and the almost-everywhere qualification in $x$; the paragraph preceding equation~(\ref{eq:pf_cts_obs}) extends the cumulative constraint by total-variation continuity.
- reading of the symbol `B_Y`: $B_Y$ is a fixed, finite, nonnegative constant determined by $y_{\min}$ and $y_{\max}$. In Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound}: $B_Y=\max\{\|y_{\min}\|,\|y_{\max}\|\}$ bounds the absolute magnitude of feasible outcomes. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}, Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $B_Y=y_{\max}-y_{\min}$ is the outcome range, used to bound differences between values in $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$. — Line 1642 defines the absolute-magnitude bound; line 2204 explicitly redefines the notation as the outcome range. Lines 3136--3176 use the range bound for distances and quantile errors.
- reading of the symbol `\bar p_2`: $\bar p_2(z)$ is the sample-dependent average of the clipped first-stage fit $\hat p(z,\cdot)$ trained on $I_1$ over the covariates indexed by $I_2$; conditional on $I_1$ it remains an empirical average over evaluation covariates, rather than the population marginal mean. — The formula at flat.tex lines 1762 and 1776 and the conditional concentration calculation at lines 2179--2191.
- reading of the symbol `\widehat S_k`: $\widehat S_k$ is the sample-dependent baseline level-set estimate obtained from the pre-recovery fits on $I_1$ and their averages over the covariates in $I_2$, with a threshold-defined structural-zero set and positively indexed baseline components ordered by their averages. — The explicit recovery definition at flat.tex line 1780 and the algorithm at lines 1760--1764.
- reading of the symbol `\widehat T_{j,k}`: $\widehat T_{j,k}$ is the sample-dependent policy level-set estimate obtained from the pre-recovery fits on $I_1$ and their averages over the covariates in $I_2$; it depends on both of these inputs and is indexed by the recovered component ordering and baseline bins. — The algorithm at flat.tex lines 1760--1764 and the explicit graph construction at line 1780.
- reading of the symbol `\alpha_{p_k}`: $\alpha_{p_k}$ is the population lower-boundary representer for the particular component $J_{j,k}$, with the dependence on $j$ suppressed in its notation, for $k=1,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=0,\ldots,l_k-1$; there is no $p_0$ perturbation or correction. — The representer display and following explanation at flat.tex lines 2023--2031 give the component dependence and structural boundary convention; lines 2802--2818 verify the same formula and the absent standalone lower-endpoint term at $j=0$.
- reading of the symbol `\alpha_{p_{k+1}}`: $\alpha_{p_{k+1}}$ denotes the upper interval-boundary representer for the continuous-outcome component $J_{j,k}$, with suppressed dependence on $j$, indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=0,\ldots,l_k-1$; it remains an upper-side representer at $k=0$. — Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous} gives the exact formula for each component, and the product-score construction uses $J_{j-1,k}$ for $j=1,\ldots,l_k$; only minus-side terms are omitted at $k=0$.
- reading of the symbol `IF_{J^+}`: $IF_{J^+}$ denotes the plus-side observation-level residual component indexed implicitly by $k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}$ and $j\in\{0,\ldots,l_k-1\}$, evaluated with the nuisance functions supplied to the continuous-outcome score. — The defining display at flat.tex lines 2016--2020, the target aggregation at lines 2010--2014, and the conditional-expectation perturbation calculation at lines 2786--2792.
- reading of the symbol `IF_{J^-}`: $IF_{J^-}$ denotes the minus-side observation-level residual component indexed implicitly by $k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}$ and $j\in\{0,\ldots,l_k-1\}$, evaluated with the nuisance functions supplied to the continuous-outcome score, with its structural $k=0$ component identically zero. — The influence-function residual display and following boundary convention at flat.tex lines 2016--2021, together with the score construction at lines 2042--2048 and the target aggregation at lines 2010--2014.
- reading of the symbol `IF_{M_{j,k}^+}`: $IF_{M_{j,k}^+}$ is the observation-level residual indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and boundary index $j=0,\ldots,l_k$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome part, the no-atom condition at $y_{\min}$ makes the true residual $IF_{M_{0,k}^+}$ vanish almost surely. — The defining display at flat.tex lines 2016--2019 supplies the residual; Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization} supplies the boundary indices, and the estimation paragraph at line 1810 supplies the lower-boundary convention.
- reading of the symbol `IF_{M_{j,k}^-}`: $IF_{M_{j,k}^-}$ is the observation-level residual indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and boundary index $j=0,\ldots,l_k$, with the structural zero convention at $k=0$. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome part, the no-atom condition at $y_{\min}$ makes the true residual $IF_{M_{0,k}^-}$ vanish almost surely. — The residual display and structural-boundary paragraph at flat.tex lines 2016--2021 give its formula and zero convention; Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization} gives the boundary indices.
- reading of the symbol `\alpha_{P,k+1}^{(j,k)}`: $\alpha_{P,k+1}^{(j,k)}(x)$ is the coefficient of a perturbation of $P_{1,k+1}(x)$ in the binary component $h_{j,k}^{-}(x)$, indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, holding the propensity and alternative-policy levels and $P_{1,k}$ fixed. — The pathwise-derivative paragraph of Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete} states that $h_{j,k}^{-}$ depends on the success regressions through $P_{1,k+1}-P_{1,k}$ and gives the coefficient $D_{j,k}^{-}$.
- reading of the symbol `\alpha_{P,k}^{(j,k)}`: For $k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}$ and $j\in\{1,\ldots,l_k\}$, $\alpha_{P,k}^{(j,k)}(x)=-D_{j,k}^{-}(x)$ is the coefficient of the $P_{1,k}$ perturbation in the derivative of $h_{j,k}^{-}$, with the other nuisance arguments held fixed. — The binary score construction in Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete} gives the derivative coefficients; the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}(ii) explicitly perturbs $P_{1,m}$ and identifies these coefficients.
- reading of the symbol `\alpha_{p,k+1}^{(j,k)}`: $\alpha_{p,k+1}^{(j,k)}(x)$ is the direct upper-propensity coefficient for the binary score component $h_{j,k}^{-}$, indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, holding $P_{1,k}$, $P_{1,k+1}$, and the alternative-policy levels fixed. — Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete} gives $\alpha_{p,k+1}^{(j,k)}=-D_{j,k}^{-}$; the proof in Section~\ref{oa:orthogonality_binary} separates the integration-boundary channel from the alternative-policy channels.
- reading of the symbol `\alpha_{p,k}^{(j,k)}`: $\alpha_{p,k}^{(j,k)}(x)$ denotes the direct lower-propensity coefficient in the binary score for $h_{j,k}^{-}$, indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, holding the success regressions and alternative-policy levels fixed; at $k=0$ its residual correction is omitted because $p_0\equiv0$ is known. — Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete} gives the piecewise coefficient, separates direct propensity derivatives from policy-level corrections, and sets $R_{p,0}\equiv0$ at the structural boundary.
- reading of the symbol `m`: The notation $m(X;\eta)$ denotes the binary lower-bound integrand, whereas bare $m$ in $u_m$ or $\tau_m$ denotes a grid index. In Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete}, Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the binary-outcome case, $m(X;\eta)$ is the displayed integrand with nuisance argument $\eta$; evaluating at $\eta_0$ gives the population target integrand. In Corollary~\ref{cor:continuous_primitive_rate}: $m$ is an integer grid index: $m=0,\ldots,M_g$ for $u_m=m/M_g$ and $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$ for the fitted quantiles at $\tau_m=m/M_Q$. — The integrand is displayed at flat.tex lines 1870--1873; the two grid conventions appear at lines 1893 and 1912 and in Algorithm~\ref{alg:continuous_instrument}.
- reading of the symbol `\nu_{k\mid x}`: $\nu_{k\mid x}$ is the normalized Lebesgue probability law on $G_k(x)$, equivalently the law of $U$ conditional on $X=x$ and $U\in G_k(x)$, for almost every $x$ and $k=0,\ldots,K_x-1$ with $\|G_k(x)\|>0$. — The gap argument around (\ref{eq:pf_cts_gap_constraint}) defines $\nu_{k\mid x}:=\mathrm{Unif}(G_k(x))$ after dividing by the positive gap length and identifies it as the gap $U$-marginal.
- reading of the symbol `T_x`: $T_x:[0,1]\to\mathbb R$, $T_x(u)=\omega(x,u)$, is the target-weight map with the covariate argument fixed at $x$. — The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} fixes a nonempty conditional gap and explicitly defines $T_x(u)=\omega(x,u)$ before pushing forward its resistance law.
- reading of the symbol `\rho_{k\mid x}`: $\rho_{k\mid x}=(T_x)_{\#}\mathrm{Unif}(G_k(x))$ is the population probability law of $\omega(x,U)$ for an auxiliary $U\sim\mathrm{Unif}(G_k(x))$, defined for fixed $x$ and $k=0,\ldots,K_x-1$ with $\|G_k(x)\|>0$. — The conditional gap decomposition and the OT solution in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} specify the index range, omit empty gaps, and define the pushforward law.
- reading of the symbol `F_{\omega,k\mid x}`: $F_{\omega,k\mid x}$ is the population distribution function of the pushforward of $\nu_{k\mid x}$ under $u\mapsto\omega(x,u)$, using a jointly measurable version in $(x,v)$ for almost every $x$ and each nonempty gap indexed by $k=0,\ldots,K_x-1$. — The sharpness construction defines $F_{\omega,k\mid x}$ by integration over $\nu_{k\mid x}$; the paragraph following (\ref{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel}) establishes joint measurability.
- reading of the symbol `R_{k,x}`: $R_{k,x}$ is a measurable map of $(u,s)\in G_k(x)\times[0,1]$ for almost every $x$ and each $k\in\{0,\ldots,K_x-1\}$ with $\|G_k(x)\|>0$, using the left limit of $F_{\omega,k\mid x}$ to distribute each weight atom across its rank interval. — The sharpness construction preceding (\ref{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel}) defines the map on a nonempty gap; its subsequent measurability argument explains the measurable left limits. The construction explicitly accommodates atomic and nonmonotone weights.
- reading of the symbol `S`: $S$ is an auxiliary $\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$ draw independent of $U\sim\nu_{k\mid x}$ for each fixed $x$ and nonempty gap $G_k(x)$, used to randomize ties in $R_{k,x}(U,S)$ and integrated out in the attaining outcome kernel. — The sentence preceding (\ref{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel}) explicitly specifies independent $U\sim\nu_{k\mid x}$ and $S\sim\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$; the kernel display integrates over $s\in[0,1]$.
- reading of the symbol `\pi_{1,x}^\star`: $\pi_{1,x}^\star$ is a jointly measurable population probability kernel for $Y(1)$ given $(X,U)=(x,u)$, defined for almost every $x$ and pasted across the nonempty gaps, non-degenerate LIV intervals and trivial tail, with arbitrary probability-law values at region boundaries. — Equation~(\ref{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel}) and the following paragraph specify all pieces; the final paragraph of the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} establishes joint measurability and compatibility.
- reading of the symbol `\mathcal E_n`: $\mathcal E_n$ is the event of simultaneous exact recovery of all baseline and policy level sets with their population ordering and bin indices, including $\widehat S_0=S_0$, by the procedure using first-stage fits on $I_1$ and their averages over $\{X_i:i\in I_2\}$. — The exact-recovery paragraphs in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality} establish structural-zero recovery and ordered graph-component recovery before defining $\mathcal E_n$; the binary-outcome proof uses the same event.
- reading of the symbol `r_{\mathrm{reg},n}`: $r_{\mathrm{reg},n}$ is the maximum population $L_2$ error of the fitted outcome regressions, propensity regressions, pooled policy levels, and boundary quantiles, with integration over the marginal law of $X$ while holding the fitted functions fixed. In Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}: In the continuous-outcome proof, the errors are those of the oracle nuisance fits trained on $I_1$ using the population level sets; hence their magnitudes are fixed conditional on $I_1$ and satisfy $r_{\mathrm{reg},n}=o_P(n^{-1/4})$. — The second-order remainder paragraph preceding (\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}) defines the maximum and conditions on $I_1$; the preceding exact-recovery paragraph specifies that the subsequent calculation uses oracle nuisance fits.
- reading of the symbol `r_{\mathrm{wt},n}`: $r_{\mathrm{wt},n}$ is the maximum of the componentwise $L_2(\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(\mathrm{d}x))$ errors of the fitted weights $\hat\pi_k,\hat\tau_{j,k},\hat a_{j,k}(z,\cdot),\hat\gamma_{j,k},\hat\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k},\hat\gamma_K$ relative to their population counterparts, over the finitely many fitted indices and instrument categories. — The definitions at flat.tex lines 2199 and 2267 use the same weight-error group in both outcome regimes; the surrounding remainder calculations integrate prediction errors over $X$ and condition on $I_1$ for the oracle estimator.
- reading of the symbol `r_{\mathrm{core},n}`: $r_{\mathrm{core},n}$ is the maximum of the $L_2(\mathbb P_X)$ norms of fitted-minus-population errors for the finitely many $P_{1,k}$, $p_k$, instrument-specific $p(z,\cdot)$, and pooled $q_{j,k}$ nuisances used by the binary oracle score, with the fitted $P_{1,k}$ taken after clipping and rearrangement and the structural zero boundaries retained. — The proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}\textup{(ii)} first passes to the oracle estimator, then clips and rearranges the nonboundary treated-success regressions, integrates their squared errors over $x$, and defines the maximum nuisance error in its second-order remainder argument.
- added condition (`ass:bk-3`, `thm:discrete-lower-bound`): For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, extend $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to $[0,1]$ by $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0):=y_{\min}$ and $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1):=y_{\max}$. — Equation (\ref{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel}) and its upper-bound analogue require feasible outcome-quantile values at exceptional randomized-rank endpoints.
- added condition (`ass:bk-3`, `thm:continuous-lower-bound`): For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$, extend $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to $[0,1]$ by $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0):=y_{\min}$ and $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1):=y_{\max}$. — Equation (\ref{eq:pf_cts_rank_kernel}) and its upper-bound analogue require feasible outcome-quantile values at exceptional randomized-rank endpoints.

- run-level `def:dml-continuous-outcome-algorithm`: [note, V1.5] pinned reading: The structural-zero assignment in Algorithm~\ref{alg:dml_continuous_outcome} is $\widehat S_0=\{z:\bar p_2(z)\leqslant\rho_n\}$, with deterministic $\rho_n\downarrow0$ satisfying $n^{1/4}\rho_n\to\infty$. (The Level Sets step in Section~\ref{app:procedure_continuous_outcome} explicitly defines this assignment and removes precisely its baseline vertices from the graph.)
- run-level `def:discrete-lower-bound-quantiles`: [note, V1.5] pinned reading: For $i=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $0<t<1$, $Q_{Y,i}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,i}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ and $Q_{\omega,i}(t)=\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:\mathbb P(\omega(U)\leqslant v)\geqslant t\}$, where $U\sim\mathrm{Unif}(p_i,p_{i+1})$. (The standard quantile convention applies to the probability laws in Theorem~\ref{thm:discrete_lower_bound}; Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot} uses their quantiles for comonotonic and countermonotonic coupling without assuming atomless laws.)
- run-level `def:continuous-lower-bound-quantiles`: [note, V1.5] pinned reading: For each probability law $\mu$ used here, $Q_\mu(t)=\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,a])\geqslant t\}$ for $t\in(0,1)$, with the displayed quantile integrals interpreted as Lebesgue integrals over $(0,1)$. (This is the standard generalized-inverse convention for the quantiles in 1D Optimal Transport with Product Cost, which General Closed-Form Sharp Bounds invokes; the settled quantile domains are $(0,1)$.)
- run-level `def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-quantiles`: [note, V1.5] pinned reading: For $0<t<1$, use the lower generalized inverses $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ and $Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\}$. (The notation section uses the standard quantile convention, and the measurability step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound explicitly constructs jointly measurable generalized inverses.)
- run-level `def:dml-binary-rearrangement`: [note, V1.5] pinned reading: At each evaluation covariate $X_i$, $(\hat P^*_{1,1}(X_i),\ldots,\hat P^*_{1,K}(X_i))$ is the nondecreasing sort of the clipped vector $(\hat P_{1,1}(X_i),\ldots,\hat P_{1,K}(X_i))$, with $\hat P^*_{1,0}(X_i)=0$; this same vector is used in every score plug-in and residual, without a separate gap projection. (The third step of the binary-outcome algorithm and the appendix's Rearrangement paragraph explicitly give this operation and its use throughout the scores.)
- run-level `def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-rank-kernel`: [note, V1.5] pinned reading: Interpret the kernel formula with any fixed jointly measurable $\mathcal Y$-valued extension of $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to ranks $0$ and $1$; the asserted gap marginal and attaining objective hold for every such extension. (The sharpness step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound constructs a probability kernel and states that the randomized rank is uniform. Consequently endpoint ranks have zero probability under the product law of the independent gap and auxiliary draws.)
- run-level `def:continuous-outcome-score`: [note, V1.5] pinned reading: In this definition, all components of $\psi(O;\eta)$ are those of Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}, in particular $\psi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{prod}}=\gamma_{j,k}\psi_{j-1,k}^{J}+J_{j-1,k}(\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k})$. (The sentence immediately following the display states that all components are given in Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}; that subsection explicitly supplies the fractional product formula and its index shift.)
- run-level `symbol \hat{\underline{\theta}}_{\omega,1}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "$\hat{\underline\theta}_{\omega,1}$ is the evaluation-sample average of the estimated score contributions in equation~\ref{eq:estimator_dml} for finite instruments and equation~\ref{eq:estimator_continuous_instrument} for connected instruments." (The recorded label omits both defining score averages. The notation denotes the aggregated orthogonal estimator in the finite-instrument setting and the three-term plug-in estimator in the connected-instrument setting; the primitive-rate corollary uses the concrete algorithm.)
- run-level `symbol \mathcal{R}_w`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: space "$[0,1]\to\mathcal B([0,1])$" (The definition is correct, but the recorded space describes a single Borel set rather than the map taking a propensity to that set. The arm and propensity domains are explicit in the paper.)
