(The weighted boundary-law label omits the Wasserstein loss, conditioning, and canonical-law comparator. Its decomposition into learner, localization, trimming, and grid errors applies to the concrete construction; it is not the general definition.)
- run-level `symbol r_{p,n}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "$\\|\hat p-p\\|_\infty=O_P(r_{p,n})$, with the supremum taken over $(z,x)\in\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X$." (The entry omits the explicit sup-norm condition and its domain. The later restriction $r_{p,n}=o(\delta_n)$ is a condition for the proposed series boundary-learner construction, not a redefinition of this rate.)
- run-level `symbol r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "$r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n}$ is the rate in $\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\mathbb E_{Z,X}[\|\hat g_1(\hat U_\ell,X)-g_1(\Pi_X(\hat U_\ell),X)\|\mid I_0,I_1]=O_P(r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n})$, where $\hat U_1=\hat q^{\mathrm{tr}}(Z,X)$, $\hat U_2=\hat P$, and $\Pi_x(u)=\min\{\overline p(x),\max\{\underline p(x),u\}\}$." (A generated-design prediction-rate label does not specify the actual loss: prediction is required at both estimated evaluation arguments, including any endpoint mass, against the population regression at their projected values.)
- run-level `symbol r_{Q,n}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "$r_{Q,n}$ is the rate in $\frac1{M_Q}\sum_{m\in\mathcal J_{Q,n}}\mathbb E_X[\underline p(X)\|\widetilde Q_m^c(X)-Q^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid X}(\tau_m)\|\mid I_0,I_1]=O_P(r_{Q,n})$, where $\mathcal J_{Q,n}=\{m:\varepsilon_n\le\tau_m\le1-\varepsilon_n\}$, $\tau_m=m/M_Q$, and $\widetilde Q_m^c$ is the clipped grid fit before rearrangement." (The vague fit-rate label omits the weighted grid-average loss and its selected-law comparator. Localization, excluded quantile tails, and interpolation contribute separate errors and are not included in this learner rate.)
- run-level `symbol \Lambda`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: type "context-dependent population expression or logistic function", space "$[0,1]\cup\{f:\mathbb R\to(0,1)\}$", def "In the endpoint-rate notation, $\Lambda\in\{\underline p(X),\min\{\underline p(X),q(Z,X)\},(q(Z,X)-\overline p(X))_+\}$; in the simulation index $\xi(X)=\Lambda(X'\beta_1)$, $\Lambda$ is the logistic function." (The recorded endpoint definition is correct locally but omits the later logistic reuse. The two meanings are explicitly separated by context, so the reuse is resolvable.)
- run-level `symbol \hat\Lambda`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "$\hat\Lambda$ is respectively $\hat{\underline p}(X)$, $\min\{\hat{\underline p}(X),\hat q(Z,X)\}$, or $(\hat q(Z,X)-\hat{\overline p}(X))_+$, paired with the corresponding choice of $\Lambda$." (The generic plug-in label omits the three paired expressions. The displayed $[0,1]$ space is correct for their evaluated values; conditional evaluation does not turn those values into constants in $(Z,X)$.)
- run-level `symbol \varepsilon_n`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: space "$[M_Q^{-1},1/2)$", def "$M_Q^{-1}\le\varepsilon_n<1/2$, $\varepsilon_n\to0$, and $\varepsilon_n$ defines the interior range $[\varepsilon_n,1-\varepsilon_n]$." (The recorded positive range omits the explicit upper bound, grid-dependent lower bound, and vanishing requirement.)
- run-level `symbol \hat\beta_m`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "For $m=0,\ldots,M_g$, $(\hat g_1(u_m,\cdot),\hat\beta_m)\in\arg\min_{g,\beta\in\mathcal F_n}\sum_{i\in I_1}K_{h_n}(\hat P_i-u_m)[Y_iW_i-g(X_i)-\beta(X_i)(\hat P_i-u_m)]^2$." (The recorded slope label omits the defining weighted joint optimization, including the response, function class, kernel, and training indices. The corrected definition supplies these without requiring uniqueness of the minimizer.)
- run-level `symbol \widetilde Q`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: space "$\{f:\{m/M_Q:m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1\}\times\mathcal X\to\mathbb R\}$", def "For $m=1,\ldots,M_Q-1$, $\widetilde Q(\tau_m\mid\cdot)\in\arg\min_{f\in\mathcal F_Q}\sum_{i\in\mathcal I_{\delta,1}}\rho_{\tau_m}(Y_i-f(X_i))$, where $\rho_\tau(u)=u(\tau-\indicator(u<0))$ and $\mathcal I_{\delta,1}=\{i\in I_1:W_i=1,\ \hat P_i\le\hat{\underline p}(X_i)+\delta_n\}$. If $\mathcal I_{\delta,1}$ is empty, set $\widetilde Q(\tau_m\mid x)=y_{\min}$ for every $(m,x)$." (The entry incorrectly grants the raw fit a domain covering every $\tau\in(0,1)$ and omits its defining optimization and empty-sample branch. The paper defines it only on the interior grid; the processed quantile function receives interpolation and endpoint values.)
- run-level `symbol \mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "$\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}=\mathcal L(Y\mid W=1,\ \hat P\le\hat{\underline p}(x)+\delta_n,\ X=x,I_0)$. At any $x$ for which $\mathbb P(W=1,\hat P\le\hat{\underline p}(x)+\delta_n\mid X=x,I_0)=0$, set $\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}=\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}$." (The recorded label does not specify the selection event or the conditioning that defines the target. The paper defines a training-conditional population law rather than the empirical law of the localized $I_1$ sample.)
- run-level `symbol \pi`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: type "context-dependent probability measure or conditional instrument assignment probability", space "$\mathcal P(\mathcal A\times\mathcal B)$ in transport notation; $[0,1]$ for $\pi(z,x)$", def "A coupling $\pi\in\Pi(\mu,\nu)$ is a joint probability measure with marginals $\mu$ and $\nu$; the notation $\pi(z,x)$ denotes $\mathbb P(Z=z\mid X=x)$." (The recorded coupling meaning is correct in the OT primer but cannot describe the conditional instrument assignment probabilities used in the discrete-score proof.)
- run-level `symbol \mathcal B`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: type "overloaded notation for an OT state space and the Borel sigma-field operator", def "$\mathcal B$ denotes the second OT marginal's state space; $\mathcal B(\mathcal Y)$ denotes the Borel $\sigma$-field of $\mathcal Y$." (The recorded state-space interpretation misses the Borel sigma-field usage in the conditional-kernel proof. These are distinct, contextually determined uses of the same notation.)
- run-level `symbol \Gamma_1`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "$\Gamma_1(\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}})$ is the set of probability measures on $\mathcal Y\times[0,1]\times\mathcal X$ satisfying $\int_0^{p(z,x)}\pi_1(\mathrm dy\mid u,x)\,\mathrm du=H_{1,x}(p(z,x),\mathrm dy)$ for $\mathbb P_{Z,X}$-almost every $(z,x)$ and $\pi_1(\mathrm du,\mathrm dx)=\mathrm du\,\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(\mathrm dx)$." (The recorded entry identifies the treated feasible class without stating what makes a measure feasible. Equations~(\ref{eq:pf_cts_constraint1}) and~(\ref{eq:pf_cts_constraint2}) supply its observational and marginal constraints; the corrected definition records both.)
- run-level `symbol \Gamma_{1,x}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: space "$2^{\{\pi_{1,x}:[0,1]\to\mathcal P(\mathcal Y)\text{ a probability kernel}\}}$", def "$\Gamma_{1,x}$ is the set of probability kernels $\pi_{1,x}(\mathrm dy\mid u)$ on $\mathcal Y$ satisfying $\int_0^{p(z,x)}\pi_{1,x}(\mathrm dy\mid u)\,\mathrm du=H_{1,x}(p(z,x),\mathrm dy)$ for $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(\mathrm dz\mid x)$-almost every $z$." (The recorded definition gives only a location and label, omitting the observational constraint; its space also includes arbitrary functions rather than probability kernels. The paper defines conditional feasibility almost everywhere in $x$ and uses continuity to impose its cumulative constraint throughout the conditional propensity support.)
- run-level `symbol B_Y`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: type "outcome bound constant with context-dependent definition", def "$B_Y$ denotes a fixed bound determined by the feasible outcome endpoints, with its precise definition depending on the proof context." (The entry records only the first absolute-magnitude definition. The later estimation proofs use the outcome diameter, which generally differs from that magnitude bound.)
- run-level `symbol \widehat S_k`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "Choose deterministic $\rho_n\downarrow0$ with $n^{1/4}\rho_n\to\infty$ and define $\widehat S_0=\{z:\bar p_2(z)\leqslant\rho_n\}$. Remove those baseline vertices and form the graph on remaining baseline vertices and all policy vertices, connecting values among $\{\bar p_2(z),\bar q_2(z):z\in\mathcal Z\}$ whose distance is less than $c_{\mathrm{gap}}/2$. The sets $\widehat S_k$, $k\geqslant1$, collect instruments whose remaining $(p,z)$ vertices share a connected component, ordered by component averages." (The entry omits both structural-zero thresholding and the connected-component rule. Recovery uses evaluation covariates, so these sets are not fixed conditional only on training data; the SETTINGS description of remaining fits overlooks this dependence. The proof's equality with population sets is an asymptotic recovery event.)
- run-level `symbol \widehat T_{j,k}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "After removing baseline vertices belonging to $\widehat S_0$, form the undirected graph on the remaining baseline vertices and all policy vertices, connecting two vertices when their scalar values among $\{\bar p_2(z),\bar q_2(z):z\in\mathcal Z\}$ differ by less than $c_{\mathrm{gap}}/2$. The sets $\widehat T_{j,k}$ collect instruments whose $(q,z)$ vertices share a connected component, indexed by the baseline bin containing that component average, using half-open bins for $k<K$ and the closed final bin for $k=K$." (The entry omits the defining graph and bin assignment. These sets use evaluation covariates as well as training fits; conditioning only on the training sample does not fix them. The SETTINGS description of remaining fits as functions only of training data overlooks their dependence on recovered sets. The recovery equality in the proof is an asymptotic event, not a redefinition.)
- run-level `symbol \alpha_{p_k}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "For $k\geqslant1$, $\alpha_{p_k}(x)=-J_{j,k}^-(x)-\nu_{j,k}(x)M_{j,k}^-(x)+\nu_{j+1,k}(x)M_{j+1,k}^-(x)+\indicator(j\geqslant1)\nu_{j,k}(x)-\nu_{j+1,k}(x)$. The $p_0$ correction is omitted because $p_0\equiv0$ is known." (The original label omits the explicit representer, including its essential $\indicator(j\geqslant1)$ term. The notation suppresses dependence on the sub-interval component, so it must not be read as one common function for every $j$.)
- run-level `symbol \alpha_{p_{k+1}}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "$\alpha_{p_{k+1}}(X)=J_{j,k}^{+}(X)+\nu_{j,k}(X)M_{j,k}^{+}(X)-\nu_{j+1,k}(X)M_{j+1,k}^{+}(X)$." (The recorded representer label omits the explicit formula and its suppressed subinterval index. The representer varies with the component $J_{j,k}$ and is retained at the structural lower boundary.)
- run-level `symbol IF_{J^+}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "$IF_{J^+}(O)=\frac{1}{\pi_{k+1}(X)}\indicator(Z\in S_{k+1})W\big(Y\indicator(\nu_{j,k}(X)<Y\leqslant\nu_{j+1,k}(X))-J_{j,k}^+(X)\big)$." (The recorded description does not specify the instrument-set indicator, treatment multiplier, assignment-probability denominator, or centered band outcome. The notation suppresses the band and baseline-interval indices.)
- run-level `symbol IF_{J^-}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "$IF_{J^-}(O)=\pi_k(X)^{-1}\indicator(Z\in S_k)W\big(Y\indicator(\nu_{j,k}(X)<Y\leqslant\nu_{j+1,k}(X))-J_{j,k}^-(X)\big)$ for $k\geqslant1$. For $k=0$, the minus-side residual is omitted and defined as zero solely for notational convenience; the score never divides by $\pi_0(X)$." (The recorded analogue label omits the residual formula and does not record its zero extension at the structural boundary. The suppressed indices refer to a band within a baseline interval, rather than to one common residual for every band.)
- run-level `symbol IF_{M_{j,k}^+}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "$IF_{M_{j,k}^+}(O)=\pi_{k+1}(X)^{-1}\indicator(Z\in S_{k+1})W\big(\indicator(Y\leqslant\nu_{j,k}(X))-M_{j,k}^+(X)\big)$." (The scaled-residual label does not specify the paper's formula. Its scaling is by the instrument-set probability, with a separate factor $W$, and its boundary index includes the fixed lower endpoint.)
- run-level `symbol IF_{M_{j,k}^-}`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "For $k\geqslant1$, $IF_{M_{j,k}^-}(O)=\pi_k(X)^{-1}\indicator(Z\in S_k)W\big(\indicator(Y\leqslant\nu_{j,k}(X))-M_{j,k}^-(X)\big)$. At $k=0$, this residual is omitted and defined as zero solely for notational convenience; no division by $\pi_0(X)$ is performed." (The recorded analogue label omits the scaling, treatment indicator, centering term, and explicit structural zero convention. The paper supplies these and distinguishes the lower boundary residual from residuals at estimated thresholds.)
- run-level `symbol m`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: type "binary target integrand; locally, integer grid index", space "For the integrand, $\mathcal X\times\{\text{admissible nuisance vectors}\}\to\mathbb R$; for a grid index, a finite subset of $\mathbb Z$.", def "$m(X;\eta)=\sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\big(\gamma_{\text{full},k}(X)J_{\text{full},k}(X)+\sum_{j=1}^{l_k}\gamma_{j,k}(X)h_{j,k}^{-}(X)\big)+\Delta_K(X)$; locally, $m$ also indexes the grids $u_m=m/M_g$ and $\tau_m=m/M_Q$." (The recorded integrand definition omits its weights and summation ranges. The paper also uses the same letter locally as an integer grid index; that use does not replace the binary integrand.)
- run-level `symbol \pi_{1,x}^\star`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: def "For $u\in G_k(x)$ on a nonempty gap, $\pi_{1,x}^\star(A\mid u)=\int_0^1\indicator\{Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1-R_{k,x}(u,s))\in A\}\,\mathrm ds$, $A\in\mathcal B(\mathcal Y)$. On every non-degenerate LIV interval, $\pi_{1,x}^\star(\mathrm dy\mid u)=K_{1,x}(\mathrm dy\mid u)$ for the fixed compatible kernel used in (\ref{eq:canonical_submeasures}). On the trivial tail, use $\delta_{y_{\min}}$ where $\omega(x,u)\geqslant0$ and $\delta_{y_{\max}}$ where $\omega(x,u)<0$; values on the finitely many region boundaries may be assigned arbitrarily." (The recorded definition specifies only the gap piece, although the kernel's domain is all of $[0,1]$. The paper also defines its LIV and tail pieces and permits arbitrary boundary assignments.)
- run-level `symbol \mathcal E_n`: [note, V1.5] symbol table corrected: space "The event space of the observed sample together with its random equal-half partition $(I_1,I_2)$." (The definition correctly identifies exact recovery, but the recorded event space contains only observations and omits the random partition on which recovery depends. Exact recovery includes the structural-zero set and the ordered, indexed component families.)
- note on `ass:1d-ot-probability-laws`: pinned reading: For $\rho\in\{\mu,\nu\}$ and $t\in(0,1)$, $Q_\rho(t)=\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\rho((\! -\infty,a])\geqslant t\}$. (The standard generalized-inverse convention for probability quantiles applies to the notation section and Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot}; the theorem evaluates these functions at uniform draws and inside Lebesgue integrals.)
- note on `ass:continuity-p-on-components`: pinned reading: The continuity condition holds for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x\in\mathcal X$. (The prose preceding the conditional range decomposition specifies almost every covariate value under the observed law, and the proof of the Conditional General Bound disintegrates over the marginal law of the covariates.)
- note on `ass:continuous-learners-boundary-grid-rate`: pinned reading: In (L3), $O_P(r_{Q,n})$ describes stochastic order over the training observations and random partition, while $\mathbb E_X[\cdot\mid I_0,I_1]$ averages an independent population draw of $X$ conditional on the partition and the observations indexed by $I_0\cup I_1$. (The learner-condition preamble specifies training conditioning, and the three-way sample-splitting description fixes learned functions during target evaluation. The notation section defines stochastic order as boundedness in probability.)
- note on `ass:continuous-learners-first-stage`: pinned reading: $\\|\hat p-p\\|_\infty=\sup_{(z,x)\in\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X}\|\hat p(z,x)-p(z,x)\|$. (The prose introducing the Continuous-Instrument Learner Conditions requires uniform first-stage control because the fit determines conditional support extrema and shrinking boundary-sample membership.)
- note on `ass:continuous-learners-outcome`: pinned reading: In (L2), $O_P(r_{g,\mathrm{loc},n})$ describes stochastic order over the training observations and random partition, while $\mathbb E_{Z,X}[\cdot\mid I_0,I_1]$ averages an independent population draw of $(Z,X)$ conditional on the partition and the observations indexed by $I_0\cup I_1$. (The three-way sample-splitting description holds fitted functions fixed for evaluation; the prose following the learner conditions explicitly distinguishes training and target-evaluation laws. The notation section defines stochastic order as boundedness in probability.)
- note on `ass:dml-normality-binary`: pinned reading: $Y\in\{0,1\}$ almost surely. (Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}\textup{(ii)} explicitly states this coding before defining the binary subinterval contribution used by Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}\textup{(ii)}.)
- note on `ass:dml-normality-first-stage`: pinned reading: For a fresh $X$ with the population marginal law and independent of $I_1$, $\\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\\|_{L_2}^2=\mathbb E[(\hat p(z,X)-p(z,X))^2\mid I_1]$, and $n^{1/4}\max_{z\in\mathcal Z}\\|\hat p(z,\cdot)-p(z,\cdot)\\|_{L_2}\xrightarrow{P}0$ as $n\to\infty$. (The exact-recovery argument in the proof of \cref{thm:dml_normality} compares $\mathbb E[\hat p(z,X)\mid I_1]$ with $\mathbb E[p(z,X)]$ using this norm, before evaluating the fit on independent covariates in $I_2$.)
- note on `ass:dml-normality-nuisance-rates`: pinned reading: Componentwise $L_2$ errors integrate over the marginal law of $X$ under $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}$, holding each fitted function and any instrument argument $z$ fixed; $o_P(n^{-1/4})$ refers to convergence in probability over the observed sample and random partition as $n\to\infty$. (The sampling setup for Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality} specifies the random sample and partition; its supplied second-order remainder bound, equation~(\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}), uses population covariate integrals of the fitted errors.)
- note on `ass:dml-normality-score-clipping`: pinned reading: Pointwise in $x$, the fitted probability, propensity, share, and indicator-mean nuisances lie in $[0,1]$, $\hat\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}(x)\in[-1,1]$, $\hat\nu_{j,k}(x),\hat J_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\pm}(x)\in[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$, and $\hat J_{j,k}^{\pm}(x)\in[\min\{0,y_{\min}\},\max\{0,y_{\max}\}]$, for each applicable fitted component; denominator clipping is applied further as stated. (The nuisance definitions and estimation steps in \cref{app:procedure_continuous_outcome,app:procedure_binary_outcome} determine these ranges. The quantile remainder in (\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}) explicitly uses both quantiles lying in $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$.)
- note on `ass:j-specialization-continuity`: pinned reading: For each fixed $k\in\{0,\dots,K-1\}$ and $j\in\{0,\dots,l_k-1\}$ to which part~(i) is applied, its continuity hypothesis is $\mu_{1,k\mid X}(\{\nu_{j,k}(X)\})=\mu_{1,k\mid X}(\{\nu_{j+1,k}(X)\})=0$ almost surely under the marginal law of $X$. (The proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization}(i) fixes the band indices and uses continuity of the distribution function at the two boundaries to identify their probability levels and exclude boundary atoms.)
- note on `ass:ot-formulation-untreated-compatibility`: pinned reading: The untreated compatibility equality holds for $\mathbb P_{Z,X}$-almost every $(z,x)$, where $\mathbb P_{Z,X}$ is the $(Z,X)$-marginal of $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}$. (Equation $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ explicitly imposes the same compatibility constraints for $\mathbb P_{Z,X}$-almost every $(z,x)$.)
- note on `ass:overlap-policy`: pinned reading: For every $x\in\mathcal X$ and every nonempty $T_{j,k}$, $\tau_{j,k}(x)\geqslant c_\pi$, where $c_\pi>0$ is the same constant used for $\pi_k$ in \cref{asp:overlap}. (Both clauses belong to the single quantified overlap assumption. The proof of \cref{thm:dml_normality} uses this assumption to bound the inverse-probability weights uniformly.)
- note on `ass:phi-smooth-derivative`: pinned reading: There exist finite constants $C,L\geqslant0$ such that, for all $z\in\mathcal Z$, $x\in\mathcal X$, and $p,p'\in[0,1]$, $\|\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p)\|\leqslant C$ and $\|\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p)-\partial\phi/\partial p(z,x,p')\|\leqslant L\|p-p'\|$. (The exact-recovery argument for \cref{thm:dml_normality} uses a uniformly bounded derivative, and (\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}) uses its uniformly Lipschitz derivative for the quadratic policy remainder.)
- note on `ass:phi-smooth-differentiable`: pinned reading: For every $z\in\mathcal Z$ and $x\in\mathcal X$, $p\mapsto\phi(z,x,p)$ is differentiable on $(0,1)$ and has finite right and left derivatives at $p=0$ and $p=1$, respectively; these are its endpoint values of $\partial\phi/\partial p$. (The policy map has domain $\mathcal Z\times\mathcal X\times[0,1]$. The estimation procedure clips $\hat p$ to this interval, and the policy Taylor remainder in (\ref{eq:pf_dml_bias}) uses differentiation within that domain.)
- note on `ass:structure-consistency`: pinned reading: $Y=WY(1)+(1-W)Y(0)$ almost surely under the structural population law. (Assumption \ref{asp:structure} states consistency as a structural equality of random variables; the subsequent observational derivation states conditional identities almost everywhere, consistently with the standard almost-sure convention.)
- note on `ass:structure-threshold-crossing`: pinned reading: $W=\indicator(U\leqslant p(Z,X))$ almost surely under the structural population law. (The threshold-crossing clause of Assumption \ref{asp:structure} is a structural equality of random variables. The derivation preceding equation \ref{eq:canonical_submeasures} uses it to establish observational identities almost everywhere.)
- note on `ass:structure-uniform-resistance`: pinned reading: $\mathcal L(U\mid X=x)=\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$ for $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$. (Assumption \ref{asp:structure} uses conditional-distribution notation without a pointwise covariate quantifier, while its common-support clause explicitly says every covariate value. The surrounding prose also recognizes that conditional probabilities are identified only almost surely.)
- note on `ass:target-weight-identifiable`: pinned reading: Any two structural laws satisfying Structural Assumptions and inducing the same $\mathbb P_{obs}$ give the same $\omega(x,u)$ for $\mathbb P_{obs}(\mathrm dx)\,\mathrm du$-almost every $(x,u)$. (The paragraph following Structural Assumptions states that the observed law identifies conditional probabilities almost surely; equation (\ref{eq:omega_form}) constructs weights from such conditional expectations, and equation (\ref{eq:target_mte}) integrates against the covariate marginal and Lebesgue measure.)
- note on `def:binary-score`: pinned reading: In this definition, $\psi_{\mathrm{full},k}^{\mathrm{prod}}$ and $\psi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{prod}}$ are the binary-outcome components of Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete}, and $\psi_{\Delta_K}$ is the common tail component of Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}. (The binary nuisance-estimation subsection introduces this display and immediately directs the reader to Section~\ref{app:scores_discrete} for its component formulas.)
- note on `def:boundary-indicator-means`: pinned reading: For $j=1,\ldots,l_k$, interpret $\nu_{j,k}(x)=Q_{Y,k\mid x}(\kappa_{j,k}(x))$ using $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ for $0<t<1$, with $\nu_{0,k}(x)=y_{\min}$. (Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization} defines the regular boundaries through the quantile function and explicitly fixes the lower boundary. The lower generalized inverse is the standard quantile-function convention; the propensity-bin and separation conventions place regular thresholds inside $(0,1)$.)
- note on `def:boundary-treated-law`: pinned reading: For $0<t<1$, use the standard lower generalized quantile $Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$. (The Notation subsection uses quantile functions without imposing atomlessness or strict monotonicity. Proposition~\ref{prop:continuous_reduction} integrates this quantile against Lebesgue measure, so the standard generalized-inverse convention applies and endpoint values do not affect the population integrals.)
- note on `def:continuous-instrument`: pinned reading: Off-grid evaluations of $\widehat C(\tau\mid x)$ use linear interpolation between adjacent cumulative-integral values on $\{0,\tau_1,\ldots,\tau_{M_Q-1},1\}$, whose values are computed by the trapezoid rule. (The Target Evaluation paragraph in the Continuous Instrument estimation appendix explicitly specifies interpolation between adjacent cumulative-integral grid values for off-grid lookup.)
- note on `def:continuous-learners-clipped-grid`: pinned reading: The superscript $c$ in $\widetilde Q_m^c$ labels clipping to $[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$ and does not denote the transport cost $c$. (The defining minimum-maximum formula is immediately described as the clipped grid fit before rearrangement.)
- note on `def:continuous-learners-selected-law`: pinned reading: For $0<t<1$, $Q^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$. (This is the standard lower generalized-inverse reading of the probability-measure quantile notation introduced in the paper's notation section and used for the selected-law target.)
- note on `def:continuous-lower-bound-quantiles`: pinned reading: For each probability law $\mu$ used here, $Q_\mu(t)=\inf\{a\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,a])\geqslant t\}$ for $t\in(0,1)$, with the displayed quantile integrals interpreted as Lebesgue integrals over $(0,1)$. (This is the standard generalized-inverse convention for the quantiles in 1D Optimal Transport with Product Cost, which General Closed-Form Sharp Bounds invokes; the settled quantile domains are $(0,1)$.)
- note on `def:continuous-outcome-score`: pinned reading: In this definition, all components of $\psi(O;\eta)$ are those of Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}, in particular $\psi_{j,k}^{\mathrm{prod}}=\gamma_{j,k}\psi_{j-1,k}^{J}+J_{j-1,k}(\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k})$. (The sentence immediately following the display states that all components are given in Section~\ref{app:scores_continuous}; that subsection explicitly supplies the fractional product formula and its index shift.)
- note on `def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-quantiles`: pinned reading: For $0<t<1$, use the lower generalized inverses $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ and $Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\}$. (The notation section uses the standard quantile convention, and the measurability step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound explicitly constructs jointly measurable generalized inverses.)
- note on `def:covariate-continuous-lower-bound-rank-kernel`: pinned reading: Interpret the kernel formula with any fixed jointly measurable $\mathcal Y$-valued extension of $Q_{Y,k\mid x}$ to ranks $0$ and $1$; the asserted gap marginal and attaining objective hold for every such extension. (The sharpness step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound constructs a probability kernel and states that the randomized rank is uniform. Consequently endpoint ranks have zero probability under the product law of the independent gap and auxiliary draws.)
- note on `def:discrete-lower-bound-quantiles`: pinned reading: For $i=0,\ldots,K-1$ and $0<t<1$, $Q_{Y,i}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,i}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ and $Q_{\omega,i}(t)=\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:\mathbb P(\omega(U)\leqslant v)\geqslant t\}$, where $U\sim\mathrm{Unif}(p_i,p_{i+1})$. (The standard quantile convention applies to the probability laws in Theorem~\ref{thm:discrete_lower_bound}; Theorem~\ref{thm:1d_ot} uses their quantiles for comonotonic and countermonotonic coupling without assuming atomless laws.)
- note on `def:discrete-nuisance-components`: pinned reading: The identified outcome components $J_{\text{full},k}$ and $J_{j,k}$ are defined for $0\leqslant k<K$, with $0\leqslant j\leqslant l_k$ for $J_{j,k}$; the final interval is represented by $\Delta_K$, without an identified law $\mu_{1,K\mid x}$. (Proposition~\ref{prop:discrete_reduction}, equation~(\ref{eq:target_discrete_iv}), sums the identified outcome components only over $k=0,\ldots,K-1$ and adds $\Delta_K$ separately. The cited conditional gap-law definition distinguishes identified gaps from the trivial tail.)
- note on `def:dml-binary-rearrangement`: pinned reading: At each evaluation covariate $X_i$, $(\hat P^*_{1,1}(X_i),\ldots,\hat P^*_{1,K}(X_i))$ is the nondecreasing sort of the clipped vector $(\hat P_{1,1}(X_i),\ldots,\hat P_{1,K}(X_i))$, with $\hat P^*_{1,0}(X_i)=0$; this same vector is used in every score plug-in and residual, without a separate gap projection. (The third step of the binary-outcome algorithm and the appendix's Rearrangement paragraph explicitly give this operation and its use throughout the scores.)
- note on `def:dml-continuous-outcome-algorithm`: pinned reading: The structural-zero assignment in Algorithm~\ref{alg:dml_continuous_outcome} is $\widehat S_0=\{z:\bar p_2(z)\leqslant\rho_n\}$, with deterministic $\rho_n\downarrow0$ satisfying $n^{1/4}\rho_n\to\infty$. (The Level Sets step in Section~\ref{app:procedure_continuous_outcome} explicitly defines this assignment and removes precisely its baseline vertices from the graph.)
- note on `def:dml-estimation-procedure`: pinned reading: Two remaining labeled vertices are connected exactly when their averaged values differ by less than $c_{\mathrm{gap}}/2$. (Both Algorithm~\ref{alg:dml_continuous_outcome} and the Level Sets step in Section~\ref{app:procedure_continuous_outcome} explicitly use ``less than''.)
- note on `def:dml-level-recovery`: pinned reading: The estimated baseline bins used to index $\widehat T_{j,k}$ include their left endpoints and exclude their right endpoints for $k<K$; the final bin for $k=K$ includes both endpoints, including $1$. (The baseline and alternative instrument level-set definition specifies $[p_k(x),p_{k+1}(x))$ for $k<K$ and $[p_K(x),1]$ for the final bin; the recovery paragraph explicitly uses those bin conventions.)
- note on `def:dml-normality-binary-nuisances`: pinned reading: The binary nuisance list supplements the separately fitted first-stage propensity, so $\eta(x)=\{p(z,x),p_k(x),P_{1,k}(x),\pi_k(x),\tau_{j,k}(x),a_{j,k}(z,x),\gamma_{\mathrm{full},k}(x),\gamma_{j,k}(x),\gamma_K(x)\}_{j,k}$, with $p_0=P_{1,0}=0$ fixed and no fitted $\pi_0$. (Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality} separately imposes the instrument-specific first-stage rate before specifying the remaining score nuisances. Section~\ref{app:procedure_binary_outcome} explicitly prints the full vector including $p(z,x)$ and states the structural-zero conventions.)
- note on `def:j-specialization-boundaries`: pinned reading: For $0<t<1$, use the standard lower generalized quantile $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$. (The Notation subsection calls these quantile functions, and the binary-outcome part of Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization} explicitly uses an atomic law. This fixes generalized inversion rather than ordinary inversion; the displayed formula specifies the standard lower-quantile convention at flat pieces.)
- note on `def:mte`: pinned reading: $\text{MTE}(x,u)$ denotes a jointly measurable version of $\mathbb E[Y(1)-Y(0)\mid X=x,U=u]$, determined only for $(\mathbb P_X\otimes\mathrm du)$-almost every $(x,u)$. (The structural assumption specifies $U\mid X\sim\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$, and the target in equation~(\ref{eq:target_mte}) integrates this conditional mean against $\omega(X,u)$.)
- note on `def:ot-formulation`: pinned reading: Every constraint in $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ is imposed simultaneously for both $w\in\{0,1\}$. (The sentence immediately preceding equation $\eqref{eq:ot_formulation}$ explicitly says that every constraint is imposed for both treatment states.)
- note on `def:quantile-endpoint-convention`: pinned reading: The endpoint-invariance assertion for population Lebesgue integrals concerns changes only at $\tau\in\{0,1\}$ of a fixed population quantile; the two permitted fitted interpolants may differ on $[0,M_Q^{-1}]\cup[1-M_Q^{-1},1]$. (The endpoint-convention paragraph explicitly distinguishes a grid-integral difference of order $O(M_Q^{-1})$ from invariance of population Lebesgue integrals.)
- note on `def:range-decomposition-cov`: pinned reading: For $\mathbb P_X$-almost every $x$, the intervals $L_k(x)$, $1\leqslant k\leqslant K_x$, the gaps $G_k(x)$, $0\leqslant k<K_x$, and the tail $(\overline p_{K_x}(x),1]$ partition $[0,1]$ up to Lebesgue-null endpoints. (The introduction to the definition gives almost-sure covariate scope. The maximal-interval decomposition step in the proof of the Conditional General Bound explicitly states the index ranges and the partition up to Lebesgue-null endpoints.)
- note on `def:range-regions`: pinned reading: The partition assertion means $\int_{[0,1]\setminus\left(\bigcup_{k=1}^{K}L_k\,\cup\,\bigcup_{k=0}^{K-1}G_k\,\cup\,(\overline p_K,1]\right)}\mathrm du=0$. (Structural Assumptions gives uniform latent resistance, and the objective decomposition immediately following the region definition uses Lebesgue integrals over precisely these regions in deriving General Closed-Form Sharp Bounds.)
- note on `def:wasserstein-distance`: pinned reading: For every probability measure $\mu$ on $\mathbb R$ and $0<t<1$, $Q_\mu(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu(( -\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$; the integral defining $W_1$ is a Lebesgue integral over $(0,1)$ and requires no endpoint values. (The notation paragraph calls $Q_\mu$ the quantile function, invoking the standard lower generalized-inverse convention. Its displayed formula for $W_1$ integrates quantiles against Lebesgue measure.)
- note on `lem:orthogonality-discrete`: pinned reading: Part~\textup{(i)} assumes the common hypotheses and continuous-outcome case of Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality}; part~\textup{(ii)} assumes its common hypotheses and binary-outcome case. (Lemma~\ref{lem:orthogonality_discrete} explicitly references the respective theorem parts, and its separate proofs use the density conditions for continuous boundaries and the discrete-outcome gap for binary boundaries.)
- note on `prop:continuous-reduction`: pinned reading: For $\underline p(x)>0$ and $0<t<1$, $Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$; at $t=0,1$ use the endpoint extension of the initial-gap outcome quantile in the referenced bound. (The boundary-treated-law definition identifies this law with the outcome law on the initial gap. The covariate continuous lower-bound theorem supplies the binding generalized-inverse reading and endpoint extension; the continuous-reduction proof uses that same gap law.)
- note on `prop:discrete-reduction`: [statement-wrong] The frozen finite instrument setup lacks the topology/singleton-support condition needed to identify every instrument level; Lean supplies it through DiscreteTopology Z. (finding withdrawn by the paper's owner on prop:discrete-reduction: A finite instrument domain $\mathcal Z$ carries the discrete topology, so the common-support condition $\mathrm{Supp}(Z\mid X=x)=\mathcal Z$ says that every instrument value has positive conditional probability given $X=x$.)
- note on `prop:j-specialization`: pinned reading: The boundary quantile uses the bounded-domain lower generalized inverse $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in\mathcal Y:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$ for $0\leqslant t<1$, so $Q_{Y,k\mid x}(0)=y_{\min}$. (The standard lower generalized-inverse convention supplies the interior quantiles. The known compact outcome domain and the structural boundary $\nu_{0,k}=y_{\min}$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:J_specialization} supply the bounded extension at zero; the policy-level definition explicitly permits $q_{1,k}=p_k$. The proof of part (i) uses the resulting boundary relation $F(\nu_{j,k})=\kappa_{j,k}$ under its continuity hypothesis.)
- note on `symbol \alpha_{p_{k+1}}`: symbol table corrected: def "$\alpha_{p_{k+1}}(X)=J_{j,k}^{+}(X)+\nu_{j,k}(X)M_{j,k}^{+}(X)-\nu_{j+1,k}(X)M_{j+1,k}^{+}(X)$." (The recorded representer label omits the explicit formula and its suppressed subinterval index. The representer varies with the component $J_{j,k}$ and is retained at the structural lower boundary.)
- note on `symbol \alpha_{p_k}`: symbol table corrected: def "For $k\geqslant1$, $\alpha_{p_k}(x)=-J_{j,k}^-(x)-\nu_{j,k}(x)M_{j,k}^-(x)+\nu_{j+1,k}(x)M_{j+1,k}^-(x)+\indicator(j\geqslant1)\nu_{j,k}(x)-\nu_{j+1,k}(x)$. The $p_0$ correction is omitted because $p_0\equiv0$ is known." (The original label omits the explicit representer, including its essential $\indicator(j\geqslant1)$ term. The notation suppresses dependence on the sub-interval component, so it must not be read as one common function for every $j$.)
- note on `symbol \delta`: symbol table corrected: type "context-dependent nuisance direction or boundary-localization window", space "The nuisance function space for $\delta$; real localization windows indexed by $n$ for $\delta_n$.", def "$\delta$ is a perturbation direction in the same function space as $\eta$; $\delta_n$ is the localization window used to form $\mathcal I_{\delta,1}=\{i\in I_1:W_i=1,\ \hat P_i\leqslant\hat{\underline p}(X_i)+\delta_n\}$." (The entry describes only a nuisance perturbation, but the continuous-instrument construction also uses the indexed notation for a fixed localization window. These meanings are separated by scope; the localization clause has balanced math delimiters.)
- note on `symbol \Gamma`: symbol table corrected: def "$\Gamma(\mathbb P_{obs})$ is the set of all measure pairs $(\pi_0,\pi_1)$ satisfying, for both $w\in\{0,1\}$, $\int_{\mathcal R_w(p(z,x))}\pi_w(\mathrm dy\mid u,x)\,\mathrm du=H_{w,x}(p(z,x),\mathrm dy)$ for $\mathbb P_{Z,X}$-almost every $(z,x)$, $\pi_w(\mathrm dx,\mathrm du)=\mathrm du\,\mathbb P_{obs}(\mathrm dx)$, and support on $\mathcal Y\times[0,1]\times\mathcal X$, where $\mathcal R_1(p)=(0,p]$ and $\mathcal R_0(p)=(p,1]$." (The recorded definition names the categories of CCOT constraints but omits their mathematical content. The paper defines this class by the constraints in equation~(\ref{eq:ot_formulation}), imposed for both treatment indices; those constraints specify exactly which marginal pairs belong to the class.)
- note on `symbol \Gamma_{1,x}`: symbol table corrected: space "$2^{\{\pi_{1,x}:[0,1]\to\mathcal P(\mathcal Y)\text{ a probability kernel}\}}$", def "$\Gamma_{1,x}$ is the set of probability kernels $\pi_{1,x}(\mathrm dy\mid u)$ on $\mathcal Y$ satisfying $\int_0^{p(z,x)}\pi_{1,x}(\mathrm dy\mid u)\,\mathrm du=H_{1,x}(p(z,x),\mathrm dy)$ for $\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(\mathrm dz\mid x)$-almost every $z$." (The recorded definition gives only a location and label, omitting the observational constraint; its space also includes arbitrary functions rather than probability kernels. The paper defines conditional feasibility almost everywhere in $x$ and uses continuity to impose its cumulative constraint throughout the conditional propensity support.)
- note on `symbol \Gamma_1`: symbol table corrected: def "$\Gamma_1(\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}})$ is the set of probability measures on $\mathcal Y\times[0,1]\times\mathcal X$ satisfying $\int_0^{p(z,x)}\pi_1(\mathrm dy\mid u,x)\,\mathrm du=H_{1,x}(p(z,x),\mathrm dy)$ for $\mathbb P_{Z,X}$-almost every $(z,x)$ and $\pi_1(\mathrm du,\mathrm dx)=\mathrm du\,\mathbb P_{\mathrm{obs}}(\mathrm dx)$." (The recorded entry identifies the treated feasible class without stating what makes a measure feasible. Equations~(\ref{eq:pf_cts_constraint1}) and~(\ref{eq:pf_cts_constraint2}) supply its observational and marginal constraints; the corrected definition records both.)
- note on `symbol \hat{\underline{\theta}}_{\omega,1}`: symbol table corrected: def "$\hat{\underline\theta}_{\omega,1}$ is the evaluation-sample average of the estimated score contributions in equation~\ref{eq:estimator_dml} for finite instruments and equation~\ref{eq:estimator_continuous_instrument} for connected instruments." (The recorded label omits both defining score averages. The notation denotes the aggregated orthogonal estimator in the finite-instrument setting and the three-term plug-in estimator in the connected-instrument setting; the primitive-rate corollary uses the concrete algorithm.)
- note on `symbol \hat\beta_m`: symbol table corrected: def "For $m=0,\ldots,M_g$, $(\hat g_1(u_m,\cdot),\hat\beta_m)\in\arg\min_{g,\beta\in\mathcal F_n}\sum_{i\in I_1}K_{h_n}(\hat P_i-u_m)[Y_iW_i-g(X_i)-\beta(X_i)(\hat P_i-u_m)]^2$." (The recorded slope label omits the defining weighted joint optimization, including the response, function class, kernel, and training indices. The corrected definition supplies these without requiring uniqueness of the minimizer.)
- note on `symbol \hat\Lambda`: symbol table corrected: def "$\hat\Lambda$ is respectively $\hat{\underline p}(X)$, $\min\{\hat{\underline p}(X),\hat q(Z,X)\}$, or $(\hat q(Z,X)-\hat{\overline p}(X))_+$, paired with the corresponding choice of $\Lambda$." (The generic plug-in label omits the three paired expressions. The displayed $[0,1]$ space is correct for their evaluated values; conditional evaluation does not turn those values into constants in $(Z,X)$.)
- note on `symbol \hat\sigma^2`: symbol table corrected: def "$\hat\sigma^2=\frac{1}{\|I_2\|}\sum_{i\in I_2}\bigl(\hat\psi(O_i;\hat\eta)-\hat{\underline\theta}_{\omega,1}\bigr)^2$." (The recorded description omits the displayed centering and normalization. The paper uses the evaluation-sample mean as the center and divides by $\|I_2\|$; subsequent occurrences retain this definition.)
- note on `symbol \kappa_{j,k}`: symbol table corrected: space "$\mathcal X\to[0,1]$" (The formula is correct, but the recorded space treats a covariate-dependent function as a scalar. The threshold indices include the integration endpoint $l_k+1$, whereas the displayed outcome-boundary quantiles stop at $l_k$.)
- note on `symbol \Lambda`: symbol table corrected: type "context-dependent population expression or logistic function", space "$[0,1]\cup\{f:\mathbb R\to(0,1)\}$", def "In the endpoint-rate notation, $\Lambda\in\{\underline p(X),\min\{\underline p(X),q(Z,X)\},(q(Z,X)-\overline p(X))_+\}$; in the simulation index $\xi(X)=\Lambda(X'\beta_1)$, $\Lambda$ is the logistic function." (The recorded endpoint definition is correct locally but omits the later logistic reuse. The two meanings are explicitly separated by context, so the reuse is resolvable.)
- note on `symbol \mathcal A`: symbol table corrected: type "context-dependent state space or measure-determining class", space "A measurable state space in the OT primer; a countable subset of $\mathcal B(\mathcal Y)$ in the LIV argument.", def "$\mathcal A$ denotes the state space carrying $\mu$ in the OT primer; in the LIV argument, $\mathcal A:=\{\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,r]:r\in\mathbb Q\}\cup\{\mathcal Y\}$." (The entry records only the primer state space. Later the same notation denotes a collection of measurable outcome sets, requiring a different type and a scoped reading.)
- note on `symbol \mathcal B`: symbol table corrected: type "overloaded notation for an OT state space and the Borel sigma-field operator", def "$\mathcal B$ denotes the second OT marginal's state space; $\mathcal B(\mathcal Y)$ denotes the Borel $\sigma$-field of $\mathcal Y$." (The recorded state-space interpretation misses the Borel sigma-field usage in the conditional-kernel proof. These are distinct, contextually determined uses of the same notation.)
- note on `symbol \mathcal E_n`: symbol table corrected: space "The event space of the observed sample together with its random equal-half partition $(I_1,I_2)$." (The definition correctly identifies exact recovery, but the recorded event space contains only observations and omits the random partition on which recovery depends. Exact recovery includes the structural-zero set and the ordered, indexed component families.)
- note on `symbol \mathcal{R}_w`: symbol table corrected: space "$[0,1]\to\mathcal B([0,1])$" (The definition is correct, but the recorded space describes a single Borel set rather than the map taking a propensity to that set. The arm and propensity domains are explicit in the paper.)
- note on `symbol \mu_{1,i}`: symbol table corrected: def "$\mu_{1,i}(\mathrm dy)=\begin{cases}\dfrac{\mathbb P_{obs}(\mathrm dy,W=1\mid p(Z)=p_1)}{p_1},&i=0,\\[4pt]\dfrac{\mathbb P_{obs}(\mathrm dy,W=1\mid p(Z)=p_{i+1})-\mathbb P_{obs}(\mathrm dy,W=1\mid p(Z)=p_i)}{p_{i+1}-p_i},&0<i<K.\end{cases}$" (The recorded reference to a submeasure difference omits the defining normalization and the separate first-interval formula. There is no identified $\mu_{1,K}$ for the residual tail.)
- note on `symbol \mu_{1,k}`: symbol table corrected: def "$\mu_{1,k}(\mathrm{d}y):=\frac{H_1(\underline p_{k+1},\mathrm{d}y)-H_1(\overline p_k,\mathrm{d}y)}{\underline p_{k+1}-\overline p_k}$ for $k\in\{0,\ldots,K-1\}$ with $\overline p_k<\underline p_{k+1}$, where $\overline p_0=0$ and $H_1(0,\cdot)=0$." (The recorded definition gives only a description of an adjacent difference and does not specify the arm, endpoints, or boundary submeasure. The displayed normalized treated-submeasure difference supplies those missing parts.)
- note on `symbol \mu_{1,k\mid x}`: symbol table corrected: def "$\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathrm dy)=\frac{H_{1,x}(\underline p_{k+1}(x),\mathrm dy)-H_{1,x}(\overline p_k(x),\mathrm dy)}{\underline p_{k+1}(x)-\overline p_k(x)}$ for $\overline p_k(x)<\underline p_{k+1}(x)$, with $H_{1,x}(0,\cdot)=0$." (The entry names the law but omits its defining normalized submeasure difference. The finite-instrument complier law is a specialization of the conditional gap law, not a later redefinition.)
- note on `symbol \mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}`: symbol table corrected: def "$\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}=\mathcal L(Y\mid W=1,\ \hat P\le\hat{\underline p}(x)+\delta_n,\ X=x,I_0)$. (The recorded label does not specify the selection event or the conditioning that defines the target. The paper defines a training-conditional population law rather than the empirical law of the localized $I_1$ sample.)
- note on `symbol \nu`: symbol table corrected: type "context-dependent probability measure, quantile-regression candidate function, or scalar quantile boundary", space "$\mathcal P(\mathcal B)$ in transport notation; $\mathcal X\to\mathbb R$ in the quantile-regression minimization; $\mathbb R$ for the local scalar boundary", def "$\nu$ denotes the second marginal probability measure in transport notation; in $\arg\min_{\nu}$ for the conditional quantile regression, $\nu$ is the candidate function evaluated as $\nu(X)$; in the local indicator-boundary calculation, $\nu$ is the unperturbed scalar quantile boundary, with $a_t=\nu+t\,\delta\nu$." (The recorded measure-only entry omits both the candidate-function meaning and the local scalar-boundary meaning. The existing joint clause for the quantile-regression meaning is explicitly retained by replacement, and a separate clause pins the scalar shorthand in the orthogonality proof. The existing transport clause remains in force.)
- note on `symbol \pi`: symbol table corrected: type "context-dependent probability measure or conditional instrument assignment probability", space "$\mathcal P(\mathcal A\times\mathcal B)$ in transport notation; $[0,1]$ for $\pi(z,x)$", def "A coupling $\pi\in\Pi(\mu,\nu)$ is a joint probability measure with marginals $\mu$ and $\nu$; the notation $\pi(z,x)$ denotes $\mathbb P(Z=z\mid X=x)$." (The recorded coupling meaning is correct in the OT primer but cannot describe the conditional instrument assignment probabilities used in the discrete-score proof.)
- note on `symbol \pi_{1,x}^\star`: symbol table corrected: def "For $u\in G_k(x)$ on a nonempty gap, $\pi_{1,x}^\star(A\mid u)=\int_0^1\indicator\{Q_{Y,k\mid x}(1-R_{k,x}(u,s))\in A\}\,\mathrm ds$, $A\in\mathcal B(\mathcal Y)$. On every non-degenerate LIV interval, $\pi_{1,x}^\star(\mathrm dy\mid u)=K_{1,x}(\mathrm dy\mid u)$ for the fixed compatible kernel used in (\ref{eq:canonical_submeasures}). On the trivial tail, use $\delta_{y_{\min}}$ where $\omega(x,u)\geqslant0$ and $\delta_{y_{\max}}$ where $\omega(x,u)<0$; values on the finitely many region boundaries may be assigned arbitrarily." (The recorded definition specifies only the gap piece, although the kernel's domain is all of $[0,1]$. The paper also defines its LIV and tail pieces and permits arbitrary boundary assignments.)
- note on `symbol \psi_{\Delta_K}`: symbol table corrected: def "$\psi_{\Delta_K}(O;\eta)=y_{\min}\indicator\big(Z\in\bigcup_{j=1}^{l_K}T_{j,K}\big)(q(Z,X)-p_K(X))+y_{\min}\indicator\big(Z\in\bigcup_{j=1}^{l_K}T_{j,K}\big)\frac{\partial\phi}{\partial p}(Z,X,p(Z,X))(W-p(Z,X))-y_{\min}\gamma_K(X)\frac{\indicator(Z\in S_K)}{\pi_K(X)}(W-p_K(X))$." (The label omits the observed tail contribution and both propensity corrections. The paper specifies a common lower-bound score in both outcome settings; it is not simply the plug-in conditional tail expectation.)
- note on `symbol \psi_{\text{full},k}^{\text{prod}}`: symbol table corrected: def "$\psi_{\text{full},k}^{\text{prod}}(O;\eta)=\gamma_{\text{full},k}(X)\psi_{\text{full},k}^{J}(O;\eta)+J_{\text{full},k}(X)\big(\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{m=k+1}^{K}\bigcup_{l=0}^{l_m}T_{l,m})-\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{m=k+1}^{K}S_m)-\gamma_{\text{full},k}(X)\big)$." (The entry omits the product rule correction for the weighting regression. The product formula is common to both outcome settings; its component score has the setting-specific formulas recorded separately.)
- note on `symbol \psi_{\text{full},k}^J`: symbol table corrected: def "For continuous outcomes, $\psi_{\text{full},k}^{J}(O;\eta)=J_{\text{full},k}(X)+\frac{\indicator(Z\in S_{k+1})W}{\pi_{k+1}(X)}(Y-J_{\text{full},k}^{+}(X))-\frac{\indicator(Z\in S_k)W}{\pi_k(X)}(Y-J_{\text{full},k}^{-}(X))+J_{\text{full},k}^{+}(X)\frac{\indicator(Z\in S_{k+1})}{\pi_{k+1}(X)}(W-p_{k+1}(X))-J_{\text{full},k}^{-}(X)\frac{\indicator(Z\in S_k)}{\pi_k(X)}(W-p_k(X))$, with every minus-side term omitted at $k=0$. - note on `symbol \psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}`: symbol table corrected: def "For continuous outcomes, $\psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}(O;\eta)=\gamma_{j,k}(X)\psi_{j-1,k}^{J}(O;\eta)+J_{j-1,k}(X)\big(\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k}(X)\big)$; for binary outcomes, $\psi_{j,k}^{\text{prod}}(O;\eta)=\gamma_{j,k}(X)\psi_{j,k}^{h}(O;\eta)+h_{j,k}^{-}(X)\big(\indicator(Z\in\bigcup_{l=j}^{l_k}T_{l,k})-\gamma_{j,k}(X)\big)$." - note on `symbol \psi_{j,k}^{h}`: symbol table corrected: def "$\psi_{j,k}^{h}(O;\eta)=h_{j,k}^{-}(X)+D_{j,k}^{-}(X)(R_{P,k+1}(O)-R_{P,k}(O))+\alpha_{p,k+1}^{(j,k)}(X)R_{p,k+1}(O)+\alpha_{p,k}^{(j,k)}(X)R_{p,k}(O)+I_{j,k}^{-}(X)R_{q,j,k}(O)-E_{j,k}^{-}(X)I_{j,k}^{-}(X)R_{q,j-1,k}(O)$." - note on `symbol \psi_{j,k}^J`: symbol table corrected: def "$\psi_{j,k}^{J}(O;\eta)=J_{j,k}(X)+IF_{J^{+}}(O)-IF_{J^{-}}(O)+\nu_{j,k}(X)\big(p_{k+1}(X)M_{j,k}^{+}(X)-p_k(X)M_{j,k}^{-}(X)-(q_{j,k}(X)-p_k(X))+IF_{M_{j,k}^{+}}(O)-IF_{M_{j,k}^{-}}(O)\big)-\nu_{j+1,k}(X)\big(p_{k+1}(X)M_{j+1,k}^{+}(X)-p_k(X)M_{j+1,k}^{-}(X)-(q_{j+1,k}(X)-p_k(X))+IF_{M_{j+1,k}^{+}}(O)-IF_{M_{j+1,k}^{-}}(O)\big)+\alpha_{p_k}(X)\frac{\indicator(Z\in S_k)}{\pi_k(X)}(W-p_k(X))+\alpha_{p_{k+1}}(X)\frac{\indicator(Z\in S_{k+1})}{\pi_{k+1}(X)}(W-p_{k+1}(X))-\nu_{j,k}(X)R_{q,j,k}(O)+\nu_{j+1,k}(X)R_{q,j+1,k}(O)$, omitting every minus-side term and the $p_k$ correction at $k=0$." (The label omits the outcome, quantile-moment, propensity, and pooled policy corrections. The paper fixes the lower-index conventions explicitly: the product score with index j uses this component with index j-1, and the structural boundary never requires division by pi_0.)
- note on `symbol \varepsilon_n`: symbol table corrected: space "$[M_Q^{-1},1/2)$", def "$M_Q^{-1}\le\varepsilon_n<1/2$, $\varepsilon_n\to0$, and $\varepsilon_n$ defines the interior range $[\varepsilon_n,1-\varepsilon_n]$." (The recorded positive range omits the explicit upper bound, grid-dependent lower bound, and vanishing requirement.)
- note on `symbol \widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}`: symbol table corrected: space "$[0,1]\to[y_{\min},y_{\max}]$", def "$\widehat Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}$ is the quantile function of the estimated treated boundary law $\hat\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}$, estimating the quantile function $Q_{1,\underline p\mid x}$ of $\mu_{1,\underline p\mid x}$." - note on `thm:continuous-primitive-rate`: pinned reading: For $0<t<1$, $Q^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}(t)=\inf\{y\in[y_{\min},y_{\max}]:\mu^{\mathrm{sel}}_{1,n\mid x}(( -\infty,y])\geqslant t\}$. (This is the standard lower generalized-inverse convention for the selected-law quantile function in \cref{asp:continuous_learners}; the proof compares the sorted fitted grid with this monotone population quantile vector. Endpoint interpolation is separately specified in \cref{app:procedure_continuous_instrument}.)
- note on `thm:covariate-continuous-lower-bound`: pinned reading: For every nonempty conditional gap $G_k(x)$ and $0<t<1$, $(Q_{Y,k\mid x}(t),Q_{\omega,k\mid x}(t))=(\inf\{y\in\mathbb R:\mu_{1,k\mid x}(\mathcal Y\cap(-\infty,y])\geqslant t\},\inf\{v\in\mathbb R:F_{\omega,k\mid x}(v)\geqslant t\})$. (The pinned quantile convention of \cref{thm:1d_ot}, applied to the conditional outcome and weight laws, and the generalized inverses used in the sharpness construction of \cref{thm:covariate_continuous_lower_bound} determine this reading.)
- note on `thm:dml-normality`: pinned reading: Every nuisance-error norm $\\|\hat f-f\\|_{L_2}$ denotes $\bigl(\mathbb E_X[\|\hat f(X)-f(X)\|^2]\bigr)^{1/2}$, integrating over the population marginal law of $X$ with the fitted function held fixed. (The exact-recovery argument in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:dml_normality} bounds training-conditional population mean errors by these norms, and both remainder arguments integrate nuisance errors over an independent evaluation draw.)
